## Summary

A ZFS write is a **transaction group** (txg): every changed block is written to free space with birth T, the chain is
copied up to a new MOS root, every allocation and free of T is recorded in a **new log space map** in OpenZFS's own
format, the device is flushed, and **one uberblock slot** in all four labels flips the pool from T−1 to T. Labels are
never written; only cleanly **exported** pools are accepted. Space comes from per-metaslab bitmaps rebuilt from the
metaslab space maps plus the log space maps; space freed during a boot is never handed out again in that boot. Blocks
are killed exactly as OpenZFS kills them (free / snapshot deadlist / `free_bpobj` for gang, dedup and clone blocks),
and dataset, `$MOS`, `$FREE`, user/group/project and feature-refcount accounting are kept as exact deltas. The oracle is
OpenZFS itself in the FreeBSD 15.1 guest: zdb, import, scrub, `zfs destroy` → `freeing`/`leaked` = 0, and a round trip
where OpenZFS writes on top of agnos's state. Estimated **≈ 4,950 production lines ≈ 193 KB** of the **262,728 B**
image headroom, in 12 gated bites.

## (a) Scope

### a.1 Verbs

| Verb | ABI entry | 1.57.12 behaviour |
|---|---|---|
| create | `open`#7 with `AO_CREAT` (0x100), `AO_EXCL` (0x2000) | new SA znode, mode `0100644` (no mode argument in the ABI), uid 0, gid = parent's; `AO_EXCL` tested before `AO_TRUNC`, no-follow on the final component (mirrors `syscall.cyr:1648-1651`) |
| write | `write`#1 on a writable fd; `lseek`#58 | overwrite, append, extend, sparse (seek past EOF → holes); RMW of existing blocks through the reader's decompressors |
| truncate | `AO_TRUNC` (0x200) at open — to zero length | frees every block (holes with birth, WPA § 5). **No `ftruncate` syscall exists**; the internal `zfs_trunc(obj, len)` handles any length so a later syscall is glue only |
| unlink | `unlink`#30 | free the object in the same txg (or the `DELETE_QUEUE` path, § f.5) |
| mkdir / rmdir | `mkdir`#9 / `rmdir`#10 | 512-byte microzap directory; rmdir requires `SIZE == 2` |
| rename | `rename`#31 | within one dataset; over an existing name; directories (PARENT moves, cycle check) |
| link | `link`#32 | same dataset; not for directories |
| symlink | `symlink`#63 | targets ≤ 144 bytes (fits the 512-byte dnode's bonus with a 3-ACE ACL); longer → refused (no spill creation) |
| sync | `sync`#12 | commit the open txg (policy B) and flush the device |

### a.2 What is accepted for write

**Pool**: label `state == EXPORTED` (1); `ub_version == 5000`; little-endian; one top-level vdev of type `disk`/`file`
(not mirror); multihost off and no MMP in the best ub; no checkpoint; no removal/indirect vdevs; `log_spacemap`,
`spacemap_v2`, `spacemap_histogram` **active**; every *active* feature is on the writer's list (below); the MOS
`config` parses and names `com.delphix:vdev_zap_top`; `(1 << ashift) ≥` the device's LBA size; no dataset has an unclaimed
live or dangling ZIL (WPA § 3); the 8 MiB writer area could be allocated; every metaslab used for allocation loads
consistently (§ c.2). **(review fix)** Two more: (i) the device size is known (`zfs_dev_bytes ≠ 0`) — the reader
falls back to L0/L1 only when it is not (`zfs.cyr:406-407`), and the commit must write the slot in L2/L3 at
`psize − 512 KiB`/`− 256 KiB` (`vdev_label.c:163-170`); (ii) the chosen ub is the **maximum txg over every slot of
every label that carries the uberblock magic in either byte order** — the reader only scores native-order slots
(`zfs.cyr:415-417`); if any byte-swapped or otherwise-unscored slot has a higher txg, T = best + 1 could lose to it at
the next OpenZFS import (`vdev_uberblock_compare` takes the highest txg, `vdev_label.c:1495-1527`) and every agnos
txg would silently vanish → refuse rw by name.

**Writer feature list** (active is fine): `async_destroy`, `empty_bpobj`, `lz4_compress`, `spacemap_histogram`,
`enabled_txg`, `hole_birth`, `extensible_dataset`, `embedded_data`, `filesystem_limits`, `large_blocks`, `large_dnode`,
`sha512`, `skein`/`edonr`/`blake3` (their datasets are not grafted), `userobj_accounting`, `encryption` (its datasets
refused), `project_quota`, `spacemap_v2`, `log_spacemap`, `zstd_compress`, `zilsaxattr`, `head_errlog`, `vdev_zaps_v2`,
`dynamic_gang_header`, `bookmarks`/`bookmark_v2`/`bookmark_written` (datasets *with* bookmarks refused), `livelist`
(**(B9)** clones WITH a livelist refused), `longname`, `large_microzap` (adds to a > 128 KiB microzap refused), `physical_rewrite` (the birth
function honours `blk_prop2` bit 63, WPA § 4), `block_cloning` (+ `com.truenas:block_cloning_endian`, which `brt.c:483-492` activates together with it — **(B1)**) and `fast_dedup` (every kill deferred, § e.2),
`redaction_*`/`redacted_datasets` (redacted datasets refused). **Not on it** (active → read-only): `device_removal`,
`obsolete_counts`, `zpool_checkpoint`, `allocation_classes`, `resilver_defer`, `device_rebuild`, `draid`,
`raidz_expansion`, and any unknown name.

**Dataset**: ZPL `VERSION ≥ 5`; not encrypted (`dd_crypto_obj == 0`); a head filesystem (not snapshot/zvol/`%recv`,
no `DS_FLAG_INCONSISTENT`); **(B9)** a clone only without a livelist; no bookmarks; ZIL without `REPLAY_NEEDED`; `normalization == 0`,
`casesensitivity == 0`; `readonly=off`; its metadata checksum is fletcher4/sha256/sha512; **snapshots accepted since
bite B9** (refused before; a snapshotted head's or a clone's deadlist must be a DEADLIST ZAP with its header or
the old one-BPOBJ form — else `deadlist unreadable`). Reservations and quotas accepted (§ e).

### a.3 Every refusal, by name

The pattern is the reader's: one named `zfs:` line at mount (`zfs: <pool> rw` / `zfs: <pool> read-only (<why>)`,
`zfs: <pool>/<ds> read-only (<why>)`), and per-operation `-1` with `zfs_why` set.

| Refused | Level | Why (WPA) | Cost |
|---|---|---|---|
| label state ACTIVE / foreign hostid | pool | another host may own it; the `-f` rule (§ 3) | a FreeBSD **root** pool is never exported → read-only unless D7 (b) |
| DESTROYED / SPARE / L2CACHE | pool | § 3 | none |
| multihost / MMP in the best ub | pool | MMP protocol not implemented (§ 3) | HA pools read-only |
| checkpoint (`ub_checkpoint_txg`, MOS key, top-ZAP key) | pool | frees must go to the checkpoint SM (§ 3) | until `zpool checkpoint -d` |
| removal / indirect / `obsolete_counts` | pool | `svr_sync` + remap (§ 3) | rare |
| mirror, raidz, dRAID, > 1 top-level vdev, SLOG | pool | every child must be written; agnos assembles one device per backend (§ 3) | mirror pools read-only (D8) |
| `ub_version < 5000` | pool | no feature flags / log space maps (§ 3) | the v28 fixture read-only |
| no `log_spacemap` | pool | direct metaslab-SM path not built (§ 6.6) | makefs-built and pre-0.8 pools read-only (MAY lift after B10, which builds SM appends) |
| unlisted active feature | pool | unknown write obligations | named |
| unclaimed live ZIL or dangling ZIL head in any dataset | pool | chain blocks outside the space maps (§ 3) | cannot occur on an exported pool; defensive |
| SM/log inconsistency at load (overlap, missing segment, `smp_alloc` ≠ net) | pool | the pool is already damaged | named, read-only |
| no 8 MiB run below 256 MB (`pmm_alloc_2mb_run`) | pool | memory | read-only with a named line |
| encrypted | dataset | keys, per-block MACs, IV in DVA[2], `os_*_mac` (`module/zfs/dmu_objset.c:503-506`, `include/sys/spa.h:188-263`) | encrypted datasets read-only |
| **(B9)** clone WITH a livelist (`clone with a livelist`) | dataset | livelist ALLOC/FREE records, flushed and condensed at sync (§ 7.3) | clones that still share > 75 % of their origin (OpenZFS drops the livelist below that, `dsl_livelist_should_disable` — the fixture's `wr/clone` has none and is WRITTEN, k.92) |
| bookmarks present | dataset | `dsl_bookmark_block_killed` (§ 7.3) | rare on FreeBSD |
| `ZIL_REPLAY_NEEDED` | dataset | replay would land on agnos's changes (§ 3) | until one OpenZFS mount |
| ZPL < 5, or an object with `ZNODE` bonus | dataset / object | pre-SA format (§ 10) | `zpl4` fixture read-only |
| normalization / case-insensitive / ZAP normflags | dataset / ZAP | hash of the normalised name (§ 10) | SMB-style datasets read-only |
| ~~snapshots (until B9)~~ **(B9, built)** a deadlist agnos cannot write (`deadlist unreadable`) | dataset | deadlist insert (§ 7.4) | none on any fixture: every snapshotted head and clone is written |
| **(B9)** the empty_bpobj's LAST reference (`empty_bpobj's last reference (its removal is not built)`) | op — **(B9 review)** judged at the ADMISSION that first counts an entry naming it (`zfs_wdl_matok`; a truncation for all its txgs at its scan, `zfs_wf_dlscan`), never inside the commit, where a refusal made the pool read-only (k.100) | `bpobj_decr_empty` at 0 removes the MOS directory's key and frees the object — a fat-ZAP removal (k.93) | none: every snapshot-free head's deadlist names it once |
| **(B9)** a materialisation in a FAT deadlist ZAP (`deadlist is a fat ZAP (B8)`) | op — at the admission, as above (B9 review, k.100) | the entry's value updated in a fat leaf | a deadlist that ever outgrew its microzap (≈ 2,047 keys): OpenZFS never shrinks it back, so one pruned under 256 keys too — below ≈ 2,047 the 256-key limit next binds first (k.93, k.104) |
| **(B9 review)** the open txg's deadlists holding more than 256 keys in all (`deadlist entries in one txg over 256`) | op — when its txg first loads the deadlist; a truncation at its scan, before anything is freed (k.99) | `ZWA_DLE` holds EVERY key of each deadlist a txg loads (`ZWK_MAXDLE`, 32 KiB of the writer area) | a head or clone with 256+ snapshot intervals before it (k.104) |
| **(B9)** a snapshot-owned block born before every deadlist entry | op | `zfs_panic_recover` in OpenZFS (`dsl_deadlist.c:493-498`) | none: every deadlist has key 0 |
| **(B9 review)** a block whose path's deadlist appends no txg holds (`one block does not fit a txg: the snapshot intervals its path reaches, N`; another verb's `the op does not fit a txg: a snapshot-owned block's deadlist blocks`) | op — a `write` ENDS its txg before such a block (predicted, `zfs_wdl_path`) and goes on; refused only for the block first in its txg | each counted entry dirties ≥ one 128 KiB bpobj block in the metadata arena, of which a fresh txg's path already holds ≈ 0.8 MB (2 levels) / 0.94 MB (3 levels) of the 1.375 MB an op may use (k.100) | a block reaching four snapshot intervals in a 3-level file (RECIPE 8's `edge/q.bin`); 2 levels hold four (`edge/p.bin`) |
| **(B9 review)** a truncation that needs a second txg while a later one might not hold one block's deadlist appends (`truncation: a later txg might not hold one block's deadlist appends`) | op — at the end of its first txg, BEFORE it commits (k.99) | a later txg that cannot free one block would stop the truncation midway (SIZE unchanged, the upper range zeros) | deep files whose remaining kills reach large or many entries — the bound is conservative |
| zstd / skein / edonr / blake3 block under a partial overwrite; data block > 1 MiB | op | no decompressor / reader limit | `write` −1, named |
| name ≥ 256 bytes | op | `longname` activation | ENAMETOOLONG-equivalent |
| **(B6)** a create whose dataset's SA REGISTRY lacks an attribute the new object needs, or whose LAYOUTS hold no entry for its attribute array | op | a REGISTRY / LAYOUTS add (the LAYOUTS ZAP is fat: B8) | a symlink in any fixture CHILD dataset, on agnosfull / agnoslogs (k.2). **(B8)** the LAYOUTS add is BUILT (`zfs_wc_layout`: `count + 2`, a decimal key, a u16 array, in the create's txg — k.79); only a REGISTRY gap is still refused |
| **(B6)** a non-ASCII name on a `utf8only` dataset — **(B7 review)** a link's or a rename's NEW name too (`zfs_wname`; B7 checked neither) | op | UTF-8 validation (`u8_validate`, EILSEQ) not built | non-ASCII names there |
| **(B7 review)** a NEW name (create, link, rename) holding a NUL byte | op | the microzap chunk keeps a C string: `"e04\0x"` collides with `e04` (`mzap_open`'s VERIFY), a leading NUL reads as a free chunk | none: a C path holds no NUL |
| **(B6)** an extended-attribute directory, an immutable directory (EPERM) or a FUID group as the parent | op | `IS_XATTR` objects; the FUID table | none on FreeBSD defaults |
| symlink target > 144 bytes; any change that would need a new spill block or relayout a spilled object | op | spill creation not built (§ 10) | long symlinks, big ACLs |
| **(B6 review)** a symlink target holding a NUL byte (ext2 too) | op | `symlink(2)` takes a C string: OpenZFS never writes one | none |
| **(B6 review)** a create on a dataset whose objset_phys OpenZFS grows in memory — 2 KiB on a `project_quota` pool (or < 2 KiB) | op | its files / directories would carry `PROJID` and its sync would make the projectused ZAP (`dmu_objset_open_impl`, § f.1) | an upgraded pool's datasets not written since the upgrade |
| **(B6 review)** a mode whose trivial ACL needs `zfs_acl_chmod`'s allow0 / deny entries | op | the 6-entry form not built (`zfs_wc_trivok`) | unreachable: the ABI has no mode argument (k.55) |
| parent with `ZFS_INHERIT_ACE` | op | ACL inheritance not built (§ 10) | rare |
| `ZFS_IMMUTABLE` / `APPENDONLY` / `NOUNLINK` targets | op | OpenZFS semantics (EPERM) | none |
| add to a > 128 KiB microzap; fat ZAP with `zap_flags ≠ 0` or mid-grow (`zt_nextblk ≠ 0`); external pointer-table doubling | op | `large_microzap` / HASH64 / incremental table copy (§ 9) | > ~150 k-entry directories. **(B6 review)** the large microzap is judged on the parent's dnode (block > 128 KiB) BEFORE the name lookup, which the reader cannot serve there (k.57). **(B8, as built)** a mid-grow table and an external DOUBLING are written in dataset ZAPs (`zap_table_grow`, one block a call; `zap_table_store`'s dual writes); still refused by name: a microzap past 128 KiB, `zap_flags ≠ 0`, a normalised microzap or a non-ASCII name in a normalised fat ZAP (a normalised dataset is refused above anyway), a pointer-table growth of either kind in a MOS ZAP (before anything changes; the gate refuses an external or mid-grow log ZAP — B8 review, k.85), an accounting ZAP whose table is already external (k.77) or whose shape the commit would refuse (k.89), a table at `zt_shift` 26 (`zap_grow_ptrtbl`'s ENOSPC), an entry larger than a leaf (E2BIG), a doubling's switch from 2,048+ old blocks (2^22+ entries: the kill list's bound, k.88) |
| cross-dataset rename/link; graft or virtual directory as source/target/parent | op | one ZAP cannot name another objset's object; a graft shadows the parent's directory (`zfs.cyr:1383-1394`) | POSIX EXDEV |
| allocation past slop | op | WPA § 6.7 | short write / −1 at about half of a 256 MiB pool |
| **(B5 review)** allocation past the metaslabs WITH a space map, less one commit's metadata (`ZWK_MDRES` = the metadata arena × 3) — agnos allocates in no other (`zfs_wroom` = min(OpenZFS's figure, `zfs_wphys`)); a txg opens only while that reserve is left (`zfs_wf_begin`) | op | agnos's allocator, not OpenZFS (k.46) | short write / −1 (`no space: the metaslabs with a space map are full`) — `wr`: 4 of 15 metaslabs synced, ≈ 51 MiB of user room against OpenZFS's ≈ 113; before, the commit failed and the pool went read-only |
| refquota / quota exceeded | op | EDQUOT predicate (§ 7.5) | none |
| **(B5 review)** user / group / project quota exceeded (`userquota@` / `groupquota@` / `projectquota@` objects and the `default…quota` entries of the master node; `zfs_id_overblockquota` before each txg — `zfs_wd_idq`) | op | EDQUOT (`zfs_vnops.c:798-805`) — **enforced**, not refused | short write / −1 (`over quota (EDQUOT): userquota@`); no fixture sets one yet (k.45). **(B6 review)** as OpenZFS: object quotas only once `os_flags` has `USEROBJACCOUNTING_COMPLETE`, project quotas (bytes and objects) only once it has `PROJECTQUOTA_COMPLETE` (k.59) |
| **(B10, k.63)** the DRAIN of a queued object holding an extended-attribute directory (`an object holding an extended-attribute directory (zfs_rmnode's purge: not built)`), or of a queued directory too big for one txg | the drain step — the object STAYS QUEUED, the op that queued it succeeded | zfs_rmnode's purge (the directory queued, each xattr link_destroyed in its own tx) | OpenZFS's next writable mount drains it (agnos9's `xd.txt`, on disk) |
| **(B10, k.27)** a MOS DEDUP predecessor; a MOS gang tree deeper than 2 | commit (named) | the DDT holds a dedup block's references; the reader's own gang bound | none: the MOS is never deduplicated. A MOS GANG predecessor is FREED since B10 (k.110), no longer a refusal |
| **(B1)** redacted dataset (`com.delphix:redacted_datasets` present on a zapified dataset) | dataset | redaction | redacted receives read-only |
| **(B1)** feature refcount mismatch (`spacemap_histogram`, `log_spacemap`) | pool | B3 increments in place; a wrong base corrupts it | named, read-only |
| **(B1)** label has no `state` key; a safety-relevant ZAP unreadable (`unreadable: <key>`); properties unreadable | pool / dataset | an unreadable gate input must refuse, never read as "absent" | named |
| **(B1)** DSL nesting > 1024 levels | pool | iterative walk bound (OpenZFS allows 50) | none in practice |

### a.4 ABI § 3.6 deltas (`docs/development/agnos-userland-abi.md:458-495`)

"Imports it read-only" → "read-write when the write gate passes, otherwise read-only with a named line". `open`#7:
`AO_WRONLY`/`AO_RDWR` → writable fd; `AO_CREAT`, `AO_EXCL`, `AO_TRUNC` as § a.1; `AO_APPEND` still unhonoured (as on
ext2, `:368`). `write`#1: bytes / −1; short at ENOSPC; **durable at `close`/`sync` (policy B) — not at return**. Mutation
row: 0 / −1 with the § a.3 op-level reasons. `sync`#12 commits + flushes. `stat`: `st_size`/`st_nlink`/`st_mtime`/
`st_blocks` live after commit; `st_ino` can be reused after unlink (generation-checked fds). `statfs`: `f_bavail`
subtracts OpenZFS's slop, not 1/32. `readdir`: entries added/removed during a listing may be skipped or repeated (a
leaf split moves entries under the `blk<<20|chunk` cursor, `zfs.cyr:781`). §3.2 `link`/`symlink` rows gain ZFS.
`tests/zfs/zfsx.cyr:36,107-113` and `scripts/harness/zfs-ring3-test.py:101-117` (clauses 81–85, 88 assert refusal) are
rewritten to assert refusal only on read-only datasets/pools; new write clauses 100–140.

## (b) The txg model

### b.1 The engine is policy-free

A txg is **the dirty set**: dirty blocks keyed `(objset, object, level, blkid)` with a private copy of their content
and the BP they replace; dirty dnodes are edits inside the dirty meta-dnode L0 block that holds them (two edits to one
dnode block merge). The writer's own reads go **dirty-first, then the reader** (a block fetched for modification is
copied out of the cache — never edited in place, `zfs.md` § 3). `zfs_commit()` turns the dirty set into
txg T (§ d). *When* `zfs_commit()` runs is the policy.

### b.2 Policy (D1)

- **A — one txg per mutating syscall** (a `write()` above 4 MiB is chunked into several — **(B5 review)** with `fs_lock`
  released between the chunks, so the hold is one commit, k.8). Simplest crash argument; a
  1 MiB `cp` in 64 KiB writes is 16 txgs ≈ 16 × ~0.65 MiB of metadata writes and 16 log space maps.
- **B — A, plus per-fd write coalescing (recommended).** `write()` adds to the dirty set without committing; the txg
  commits on: `close` of a fd that wrote (both `proc_destroy_fd_table` paths, including the reap path with
  `do_flush == 0`, `vfs.cyr:262-303`), `sync`#12, `power_flush` (`power.cyr:36-61`), dirty data ≥ 4 MiB or dirty
  metadata ≥ 2 MiB or pending log entries near one 128 KiB block, a dirty set older than 5 s seen at any ZFS entry, and
  **before any other ZFS entry point runs** (commit-before-observe: `stat`/`read`/`readdir`/`open`/namespace verbs see
  only committed state, because the reader never consults the dirty set). Namespace verbs commit at their own end.
  Result: one txg per file written, one per namespace operation.
  > **(B11, as built — `zfs_write.cyr` § "B11: POLICY B")** B for every `write`#1 through an fd (the op list's direct
  > writes stay A; the knob `zfs_wp_pol` 0 makes fd writes A too — the op list's comparison). The hooks are all there —
  > `close`#6 (`vfs_close_inner`), BOTH `proc_destroy_fd_table` paths (exit, and the reap with `do_flush` 0 — also the
  > tick ISR's death chain for a killed orphan, k.126), `sync`#12, `power_flush`, the thresholds (a write the open txg
  > cannot take whole commits it and goes on — the 4 MiB data arena, the metadata arena less `ZWF_MRSV`, `ZWF_TXGBLK`,
  > ERESTART), the 5 s age bound at a ZFS entry — but COMMIT-BEFORE-OBSERVE is the NARROWER rule k.124 proves: before an
  > entry that reads the pending OBJECT (read through any fd, `SEEK_END`, `stat`, `open`, exec), `statfs`, every
  > mutation and a write of another object — never before a read of another file, `getdents`, `readdir` or `readlink`,
  > so a ZFS → ZFS `cp` stays one txg per file (measured: 8 × 64 KiB through one fd = 8 txgs under A, ONE under B).
  > "Dirty metadata ≥ 2 MiB" is the metadata arena (1.875 MiB less 512 KiB kept for the commit's own); "pending log
  > entries near one 128 KiB block" is `ZWF_TXGBLK` (k.28).
  > **(B11 review, k.131)** The exit hook is the DEATH, not the reap (`zfs_release_pid` in `proc_death_release`, before
  > the publish); nothing commits inside an interrupt handler (the tick's death chain OWES it to the next B1,
  > `zfs_wp_owed`); and a pending txg is never dropped by another op (`by=yield` after every lock release of a
  > multi-txg op, `zfs_wp_own` before any op opens a txg of its own).
- **C — OpenZFS's open txg + sync thread with a dirty overlay for every reader.** Not now: all four kthread stacks in
  region 7 are taken (CLAUDE.md), and an overlay reaches into every reader path.

### b.3 Crash consistency

Every block of T goes to space that ub T−1 considers free and that no earlier txg of this boot freed; nothing reachable
from T−1 is written (WPA § 2.3). The device is flushed, then slot `T % N` is written in all four labels, then flushed.
Until one valid copy of that slot exists, the pool **is** T−1 and every T block is unreferenced free space; afterwards
it is T, whose blocks were already durable. A torn slot fails its embedded SHA-256 and loses to T−1's slot (a different
index). Frees are not reused within the boot, so T−1 and T−2 stay fully readable (`zpool import -F`, `zdb -t`).
Durability under B: data handed to `write()` is durable at `close`/`sync`/threshold; a crash loses at most the open
txg, never consistency.
> **(B11, as built — k.125)** "Not reused within the boot" is now OpenZFS's rule exactly: the frees of txg T are released
> when txg T + 2 is durable (`zfs_wdef_rel`, `metaslab_sync_done`'s `ms_defer` rotation), so they are allocatable from
> T + 3; the commit of T can therefore take only units freed in T − 3 or earlier — referenced by no uberblock after T − 4
> — and **T − 1, T − 2 and T − 3 stay whole whatever T's writes did before its uberblock** (spa_load's
> `safe_rewind_txg` = last synced − TXG_DEFER_SIZE). The argument holds for agnos's order (§ d): the allocations of T
> all happen in its plan, before any of its writes; its first FLUSH precedes every uberblock copy, so a landed copy
> names durable blocks. Held on disk by the crash-cut lanes (k.127: halted after N device writes of a txg whose first
> data block was steered onto a block T − 2 freed; OpenZFS opens T − 1 exactly and its rewinds to T − 2 and T − 3 are
> clean) and in memory by every read-back. Durability under B as built: `close`, `sync`, exit, `power_flush`, a
> threshold — and an observation of the object (k.124).

**Commit failure** (an I/O or flush error before any ub copy is written): set `zfs_rw = 0`, re-run `zfs_ub_load` +
`zfs_mount_pool` (`zfs.cyr:393`, `:1527`) to drop in-memory roots, print
`zfs: commit txg N failed: <why> - pool read-only until reboot`; every write verb returns −1. An error *during* the ub
writes leaves T possibly live — same read-only state; the next mount picks whichever ub is best, and both are consistent.

**Hold time.** The commit runs inside the syscall under `fs_spin_lock` with IF=0 and preemption off (`smp.cyr:585-645`;
ABI §1). One txg ≈ 20–40 blocks + 2 flushes ≈ 5–35 ms on QEMU NVMe (≈ 75 4-KiB commands at 20–50 µs + flushes at
1–10 ms). Bounded by the 4 MiB / 2 MiB thresholds; measured per bite (§ k).
**(review fix)** The "≈ 75 commands" contradicts § b.2's own ≈ 0.65 MiB of metadata per txg: at ≤ 4 KiB per
`blk_write_sectors_on` command that is ≈ 165 NVMe commands per *minimum* txg (the uncompressed 128 KiB MOS meta-dnode L1
alone is 3 × 32 = 96), ≈ 3–8 ms + flushes. And the per-command bound is NVMe-only: on AHCI, virtio-blk and USB-MSC
`blk_write_sectors_on` issues **one command per sector** (`block.cyr:399-424`), so the same txg is ≈ 1,330 commands,
and a 4 MiB dirty-data commit ≈ 9,500 — hundreds of ms with IF=0 and every other CPU spinning on `fs_lock`. The
thresholds must be per-backend (sized from a measured hold-time budget), not a fixed 4 MiB / 2 MiB.
**(B3b review, measured — both estimates above are 20–100× low)** A null txg (8 blocks, ≈ 0.5 MiB written, two
flushes, four slot writes) holds `fs_lock` with preemption off for **0.6–0.9 s** on QEMU/KVM (k.8; an uberblock-only
commit 0.2–0.9 s), and B3b's op list, which keeps `fs_lock` across all its commits and read-backs at its boot slot
(IF=0), for **3.9–5.1 s** (three null txgs) and **11.0–11.3 s** (`full`, eight). The flush is a host `fdatasync` of
an image the smoke has just written, so these bound QEMU, not a device — but the ≈ 5–35 ms above was a command count,
never a measurement. The thresholds wait for iron (k.8); most of a null txg's time is the chain's write volume
(k.25).
**(B11, measured by the commit's own clock — k.126)** The 0.6–0.9 s above had the op list's own console inside it (its
plan line ≈ 0.4 s). `zfs_commit` from entry to publish holds `fs_lock` **12–70 ms a txg on QEMU/KVM NVMe** (ashift9 77
txgs, mean 20.5; main 346 txgs 16.6–56.7, mean 22.3), 40–490 ms on AHCI and USB (mean ≈ 80), with tails to 1.4 s (wr) and
2.5 s (fz) that are the host's `fdatasync` under load (the gap between two window points stayed ≤ 0.7 ms through them).
Since B11 the committing CPU takes its interrupts every ≥ 20 µs inside that hold on NVMe / AHCI (k.126 (2)); another
CPU that wants `fs_lock` still waits for all of it (k.126 (4), k.130 (1)).

**(review fix) Lock contract.** `zfs_commit()` is a *helper* that assumes `fs_lock` is held and never takes it (the
lock does not nest — `smp.cyr` re-entry proofs): policy B's hooks run it from `proc_destroy_fd_table`, which already
holds `fs_lock` (`vfs.cyr:280`), and from `power_flush`, which takes it around the sweep (`power.cyr:42`); the hook
there goes inside that bracket, before `fs_spin_unlock`. Device flushes under `fs_lock` are forward order (fs < nvme/
ahci, `smp.cyr:590-593`).

## (c) The allocator

### c.1 State loaded at rw mount

> **(B1, as built)** A mount does ONE log scan, recording per metaslab the applied entry count and net bytes, and reads
> each space map's dnode header; allocated = `smp_alloc` + net, range-checked. Bitmaps are built **lazily** on first
> use (`zfs_ms_load`, which must then count exactly the recorded entries) — so a load-time strictness refusal can now
> happen at first allocation and must flip the pool read-only mid-operation, ~~and a metaslab the open txg has touched
> is PINNED (never evicted)~~ — **superseded at B3a** (§ c.2's B3a note: nothing is pinned; the open txg's extents are
> re-applied at every load). `ZFS_WRITE_SELFTEST` still replays every metaslab at mount (the smoke diffs that dump
> against `scripts/smoke/lib/zfs-wms-check.py`). The feature refcount check is: `spacemap_histogram` == every SM
> with a 320-byte bonus (metaslabs + the DTL SM named by the MOS config's `DTL`) + every log; `log_spacemap` == 1
> and the log ZAP exists (`zdb.c:1440-1567`).

From the label: `ashift`, `asize`, `metaslab_shift`, `metaslab_array`; `ms_count = asize >> ms_shift`. From the MOS
`config` (packed nvlist; the reader's XDR parser): `com.delphix:vdev_zap_top`. From the top ZAP:
`com.delphix:ms_unflushed_phys_txgs` → `U[i]`; presence of `com.delphix:pool_checkpoint_sm` refuses. From the MOS
directory: `com.delphix:log_spacemap_zap` → `{txg → log object}` sorted by txg. Per metaslab: `sm_obj[i]` from the array
and its bonus `smp_alloc`; pool allocated = Σ `smp_alloc` + Σ net log entries ≥ `U[i]` (`spa_log_spacemap.c:1251-1261`)
— the slop baseline.

### c.2 Free space = a bitmap per loaded metaslab

A loaded metaslab is a **bitmap of `ms_size >> ashift` bits** (16 MiB metaslab at ashift 12 → 512 B; 512 MiB → 16 KiB;
cap 512 KiB, larger metaslabs are never loaded — on a pool where none fits, rw is refused by name). Build: replay the SM
in file order (ALLOC requires all bits clear then sets; FREE requires all set then clears; one-word, two-word and debug
entries; WPA § 6.3), check `smp_alloc` == net (zdb's "INCONSISTENT" rule, `zdb.c:1669-1673`), then replay every log
with `txg ≥ U[i]` ascending, entries of metaslab i only, with the same strictness. Any strictness violation → pool
read-only (`zfs: <pool> read-only (space map inconsistent ms N)`).

**(review fix) Two bitmaps with two different meanings — never conflate them.** The **allocated** bitmap is the pool's
TRUE state (SM + every log ≥ `U[i]`, including agnos's own logs): an agnos free **clears** its bits. The **deferred**
bitmap marks units that must not be handed out this boot: every FREE in any log with `txg ≥ T0 − 2` (regardless of
`U[i]` — conservative), every FREE agnos logs this boot, and every FREE in a metaslab-SM append whose debug txg is
`≥ T0 + 1` (agnos's own B10 flushes, which outlive the logs B10 destroys). **(B11)** "this boot" became the WINDOW
`[zfs_wdef_lo, T]` — T0 − 2 at mount, t − 1 once txg t is durable (k.125): every FREE of a log in it, and every
B10-appended SM FREE whose txg is in it. A unit is allocatable iff
`!allocated && !deferred`. The original text kept agnos's frees as *set* allocated bits ("bits are never cleared");
that is harmless for allocation but makes the allocated bitmap a lie, and § c.5's flush delta (`bitmap XOR SM-only`)
and clean-bump test would then silently drop every agnos FREE of a flushed metaslab — `U[i]` moves past the logs that
carried them, so the space is allocated in the SM and referenced by nothing → `zdb -b` "leaked space" (MUST; the
flush only ever writes what the true state says, `metaslab.c:4200-4203` writes `ms_unflushed_allocs`/`_frees`, which
the log replay built from *every* entry, `spa_log_spacemap.c:1110-1122`). Both bitmaps are reconstructible from disk
by the rule above, so a metaslab may be evicted — **except one touched by the open txg** (its pending ALLOC/FREE
lists exist only in memory; evicting it and re-replaying would lose them → double allocation). **Residency (review
fix):** the § g budget is 1 MiB for bitmaps; resident count = `1 MiB / (2 × bitmap bytes)`, at least 1. OpenZFS sizes
metaslabs at ~200 per vdev from 100 GB to 3 TB and 16 GiB above (`vdev.c:2985-2993`), so a 1–3 TB disk has 4–8 GiB
metaslabs (128–256 KiB bitmaps, 1–2 resident) and ≥ 3 TB has 16 GiB (512 KiB, exactly 1 resident) — "≤ 4 resident"
holds only up to 4 GiB metaslabs at ashift 12.
> **(B3a, as built — a change to the rule above)** No metaslab is PINNED. The open txg's ALLOCs and FREEs are recorded in
> its extent list (`ZWA_XL`, the input of log T) *and* applied to the bitmaps at once; `zfs_ms_load` re-applies the
> list's share of a metaslab after its strict replay (`zfs_wxl_apply`), so an evicted metaslab comes back exactly as
> T left it. The pin rule's reason ("pending lists exist only in memory") is gone, and so is its failure mode: with
> one resident slot (16 GiB metaslabs at ashift 12, every ≥ 3 TB disk) a pinned design could touch ONE metaslab per
> txg — a single MOS kill in another metaslab would fail the commit. Frees are applied at once too (allocated bits
> cleared, deferred set), which makes a double free in one txg a strict-replay error, not a silent second record.
> ⚠ B3b: before the commit drops the list it must add log T to the log table and every touched metaslab's `U`/net
> (per-metaslab +8/+56), and `zfs_w_alloc += alloc − free`; else a later reload or the slop check loses T.
> **(B3b, built)** `zfs_wc_pub` does it once uberblock T is durable — and sums log T into +48/+56 with the MOUNT's own
> scan (`zfs_wsm_walk` over log T read back from the device, checksum verified), so publish and mount cannot disagree;
> the op list's read-back (`zfs_wt_rb`) then re-runs the whole state load from disk and compares every table.
> **(B3a review)** The two bitmaps CAN overlap: on a pool OpenZFS last ran at its slop it does not defer
> (`metaslab.c:4573-4577`, `:4616-4632`), so a range freed in T0 − 1 and reallocated in T0 is both allocated and
> deferred here. A FREE therefore adds only the units it NEWLY defers to the metaslab's deferred count and records the
> others in its extent (`+8` bits 40–63, `zfs_xl_pre`); its undo un-defers only when it deferred every unit itself and
> otherwise EVICTS the metaslab (the next load rebuilds the deferred map from disk + the shortened list). The rule is
> proven in memory by `zfs_wst_b3` (one injected pre-deferred unit); B3b's `full` lane should also exercise it on disk.
> **(B3b review — on disk now)** B3b's lanes never ran it: every final image had **0** agnos FREE units inside the
> deferred set. The state is out of reach of an ordinary export: it leaves ONE log, T0 (k.3), and a range log T0 frees
> cannot be allocated at its end (a freed range returns to the allocator only at `metaslab_sync_done`). The overlap needs
> the logs T0 − 2 .. T0 to OUTLIVE the export — `zfs_keep_log_spacemaps_at_export = 1` (`spa.c:2073`: no flush, every
> log kept) on a pool OpenZFS last ran at its slop. `zfs-damage.py pre-defer` forges its single-log equivalent: at the
> end of log T0, a FREE then an ALLOC (two later sync-pass groups, `smp_alloc` unchanged) of every DVA of
> `features_for_write`'s block, the first block each commit kills, so its units are allocated AND deferred. The op
> list's plan line now prints `pre=` (units T's FREEs found already deferred, `zfs_xl_pre`) and `reapply=` (such units
> `zfs_wxl_apply` re-applied at a reload; `zfs_xl_rap`, a production counter like `zfs_ms_nload`). On disk:
> `wr@u0` (pre-defer, then zero-u): `rounds=2 loads=3 pre=3 reapply=0` — round 1 freed the units, its undo EVICTED
> metaslabs 0, 2 and 4 (`zfs_wxl_undo`), round 2 reloaded them (`loads=0` without the forge: every metaslab stays
> resident); `full@pre` (the full image + pre-defer, ONE slot): `pre=3 reapply=24` — eight reloads of the three
> metaslabs mid-txg. The read-back (which compares each resident metaslab's tracked deferred bytes with a reload from
> disk) and OpenZFS's verify (30/30 steps each) pass; every unforged lane must print `pre=0 reapply=0`.
> **(B3a review)** The no-pin rule is exercised by `ZFS_WRITE_SELFTEST`'s evict stage at ONE resident slot on every rw
> lane (eviction of a touched metaslab, `zfs_wxl_apply` with ALLOC + FREE extents, `zfs_wxl_undo` on non-resident
> metaslabs — each checked against a byte snapshot or the pre-exercise digests). Every fixture lane has all metaslabs
> resident on its own (5 × 4 KiB / 15 × 512 B bitmaps), so B3b's write smoke should add a lane whose metaslab count
> exceeds the slots (k.22).
> **(B10, as built — the rule above corrected, k.108)** The deferred bitmap's third source, "every FREE in a
> metaslab-SM append whose debug txg is ≥ T0 + 1", is GONE: a flush appends a metaslab's NET unflushed changes, so a
> range agnos allocated and later freed this boot is in no append, and the reload after its logs are destroyed would
> hand it out again. A destroyed log with txg ≥ T0 − 2 becomes a GHOST (`ZWA_GHO`: its txg, its FREE bytes, the
> dnode bytes that reach its block, never reused this boot) that every reload walks for deferral and `zfs_wscan_logs`
> sums — the deferred set is exactly the pre-B10 one whatever B10 flushed or destroyed. ⚠ (B10 review) Ghosts only grow
> until B11 retires them: the table bounds a boot of B10 alone at ≈ 2,000 txgs (k.122). **(The B10 / B11 merge, k.133
> (a))** Retired now: a ghost is made only for a log inside B11's deferral window and `zfs_wdef_rel` releases and drops
> it as its txg leaves — before its blocks (freed in the destroying txg) are released; ≤ 3 ghosts.

### c.3 Choice, allocation, frees

- **Candidates**: metaslabs with `sm_obj[i] ≠ 0` whose bitmap fits; most-free first (lowest allocated), loaded on
  demand (residency per § c.2). `sm_obj[i] == 0` metaslabs are not used in 1.57.12 (MAY later: create the SM object exactly as
  `metaslab.c:4309-4327`). **(review fix)** *Every* metaslab that receives a log entry in T — an ALLOC **or a FREE**
  (a killed predecessor can live in a metaslab agnos never allocates from) — gets `U[i] = T` in that txg if it is 0
  (`metaslab_unflushed_add`, `metaslab.c:4025-4041`); a FREE into a `U == 0` metaslab without it is the NULL
  log-summary dereference at OpenZFS's next writable import (`spa_log_spacemap.c:1123-1127` → `:611-622`).
  **(B3b, measured — the rationale corrected, the rule stands):** FreeBSD 15.1's production kernel did not crash
  there — the guest imported, scrubbed, wrote on top of and destroyed such a pool with every zdb clean (negative N3,
  § i.4). **(B3b review)** That is the build's outcome, not a property: the lookup finds no row, `target` is NULL, and
  `target->lse_msdcount++` after the compiled-out ASSERT is undefined behaviour — a debug or INVARIANTS build ASSERTs,
  a build that keeps the store faults at import, and only a WRITABLE import runs it at all
  (zdb loads read-only and `spa_ld_log_sm_data` returns first, `spa_log_spacemap.c:1139-1142`; only the guest's
  writable imports, phases C and D, executed it). So "may panic, build-dependent" stays in the rationale beside the damage every build has, which is
  quieter: a `U == 0` metaslab is not
  in `spa_metaslabs_by_flushed`, so it is never flushed, and its log-only changes are LOST once those logs are
  destroyed — unless OpenZFS happens to allocate there first (phase C did, which hid it). So the oracle for this rule
  is the host (§ i.2 h6), and agnos's own mount scan refuses such a pool (`zfs_wsm_ent`). A FREE
  never lands in an `sm_obj[i] == 0` metaslab (it would be a free of never-allocated space — refuse the pool).
- **Allocate**: first-fit from a per-metaslab cursor for `asize >> ashift` contiguous allocatable units, offset and
  size ashift-aligned, never crossing the metaslab end; set the bits; append `(offset, asize)` to the txg's ALLOC list.
  Ditto copies: each DVA a distinct extent, preferably in a different metaslab (MAY). No gang fallback — ENOSPC.
  Refuse any allocation that would take pool free below **slop** (WPA § 6.7).
- **Free**: every DVA of a BP killed down branch A (§ e.2) is appended to the txg's FREE list (header extent only for a
  gang DVA is never needed — gang kills are deferred). **(review fix)** ~~At commit~~ **At the free itself** (B3a, as
  built — superseded wording; § c.2's B3a note: `zfs_wfree_bp` clears and defers at once, and the extent list, not the
  bitmap, carries T into log T) the FREE runs **clear** the allocated
  bits and **set** the deferred bits (§ c.2) — so a range freed in T is never reused in T (MUST) nor later in the boot
  (stronger than OpenZFS's 2-txg defer), while the allocated bitmap stays the truth B10 flushes. Cost: space freed by
  agnos returns only at the next mount (OpenZFS's or agnos's). **(B11, k.125)** Superseded: freed space returns when
  T + 2 is durable (`zfs_wdef_rel`), OpenZFS's TXG_DEFER_SIZE — never reused in T, T + 1 or T + 2.
- A range allocated and freed inside one txg cannot happen (each dirty block is written once per txg).
- **(B3b, as built) The `U[i] = T` rule iterates.** Which metaslabs get an entry in log T is known only after every
  allocation of T, but the U block is an ordinary (ZLE, non-chain) block written before the chain. So `zfs_commit`
  plans in ROUNDS: when a metaslab with `U == 0` received an entry, the round is undone (`zfs_wxl_undo(0)` + the dirty
  set dropped; what it wrote lies in space nothing references) and planned again with `U = T` preset for it. The preset
  only grows → at most `ms_count + 1` rounds. ~~On a pool OpenZFS exported there is never a second round (its export
  flushes every metaslab, so each one with a space map has `U ≠ 0`, § k.3)~~ **(B3b review: false for every UPGRADED
  pool.)** OpenZFS gives a metaslab its U only at its first log entry (`metaslab_sync` → `metaslab_unflushed_add`,
  `metaslab.c:4376-4377`, `:4025-4041`); the import puts only `U ≠ 0` metaslabs in `spa_metaslabs_by_flushed`
  (`spa_log_spacemap.c:1319-1324`); the export's flush walks only that set and returns at once when it is empty
  (`:771`), and `metaslab_unflushed_bump` also requires `U ≠ 0`. So a metaslab whose space map predates `log_spacemap`
  and that nothing has touched since keeps `U = 0` through ANY number of exports — and the first commit that gives it
  an entry plans **two rounds, exported or not**: rounds = 2 is the normal first commit on an upgraded pool. The
  fixtures read `U ≠ 0` everywhere only because they were created with `log_spacemap` active (every metaslab got its U
  at its first sync, k.3). `zfs-write-smoke`'s `wr@u0` lane is that upgraded-pool state (`zfs-damage.py zero-u`:
  metaslabs 1, 3, 5 of `wr` — exactly where the allocator goes) and
  shows `rounds=2`, `U = T` for the three, OpenZFS accepting it. A preset metaslab that ends untouched keeps `U = T`
  with no entry in log T — legal: `spa_ld_log_sm_metadata` only needs log `U` to exist (`metaslab_unflushed_bump` does
  the same to clean metaslabs).
  **(B4) The rounds are gone** (k.24, decided 2026-10-10): the first commit after the mount presets U = T for every
  metaslab with a space map and U 0, so every txg plans once — a dataset phase, once written, cannot be replanned —
  and a metaslab with U 0 that still gets an entry fails the commit by name. `wr@u0` plans one round now.

### c.4 Recording txg T — the log space map (WPA § 6.5–6.6)

- New MOS object (first free MOS slot; meta-dnode grows if none): `dn_type` 8, `dn_bonustype` 7, `dn_bonuslen` 320,
  `dn_nblkptr` 1, `dn_indblkshift` 14, `dn_nlevels` as needed, data block size = smallest power of two ≥ max(bytes,
  `1 << ashift`), ≤ 128 KiB per block (multi-block logs grow levels by the generic DMU rule). MOS copies (3 DVAs).
- Content: per metaslab touched in T, ascending: `dbg(ALLOC, pass 1, T)` = `0x8000000000000000 | 0<<60 | 1<<50 | T`
  then two-word ALLOC entries `w0 = 0xC000000000000000 | (run−1) << 24 | 0`, `w1 = 0<<63 | (offset >> 9)` with `run =
  bytes >> 9`; then `dbg(FREE, 1, T)` = `… | 1<<60 | 1<<50 | T` and two-word FREEs (`w1` bit 63 = 1). Runs coalesced,
  split at metaslab boundaries and at 2^36 units; a two-word entry that would start in a block's last word is preceded
  by the pad word `0x8000000000000000`. Bonus: `smp_object` = obj, `smp_length` = bytes, `smp_alloc` = ΣALLOC − ΣFREE,
  pad 0, histogram 0.
- MOS mutations in T: log ZAP add `"%llx"(T) → obj` (micro; hex lowercase, no prefix); `features_for_write`
  `com.delphix:spacemap_histogram` += 1 (value updated in place, micro or fat); `U[i] = T` where needed (COW of the
  128 KiB `DMU_OTN_UINT64_METADATA` block).
- **Never** touch a metaslab SM's `smp_alloc`/entries/histogram outside a flush (B10).
- **(B3b, as built)** The object: the first free MOS slot — all 512 bytes zero (`dnode_sync_free` zeroes a freed
  dnode), not inside a multi-slot dnode, scanned dirty-first; a new block past `dn_maxblkid` when all are full (object
  2 on every fixture: dnode block 0 holds only object 1). Its dnode is `space_map_alloc`'s field for field (checked
  against the fixtures' logs: type 8, ibs 14, 1 level, 1 blkptr, bonus type 7 / 320 B, checksum/compress inherit,
  `USED_BYTES`) but for the block size: OpenZFS's is `zfs_log_sm_blksz` (128 KiB, lz4); agnos's is the smallest power
  of two ≥ max(the allocation unit, an upper bound on the encoding — `8·(2·6N + 2·min(ms_count, 6N) + 1 + pads)`, N the
  dirty blocks, each allocating ≤ 3 DVAs and freeing ≤ 3), written compression OFF as a chain block — **1 KiB** for a
  null txg at ashift 9 (N = 7 other blocks and 5 metaslabs bound it at 864 bytes; measured: every ashift-9 log is
  1,024 B), **4 KiB** at 12; in general the bound's power of two, 1–4 KiB for these txgs (B3b review: this said 2 KiB at
  ashift 9). Any block size is legal for a log OpenZFS only reads and destroys. Entries: the extent list sorted
  (metaslab, ALLOC before FREE, offset) and adjacent extents coalesced, as a range tree holds them; a type with no
  entry writes no debug word either (`space_map_write` returns on an empty tree). Histogram 0 (the fixtures' logs: all
  zero). The log ZAP add: the first free chunk, cd = the lowest unused among entries with the same 28-bit hash
  (`mze_find_unused_cd`), 512 B growth (`zfs_dirty_grow` + `dn_datablkszsec`) — exercised on disk by the `full` lane's
  eight null txgs (one entry after the export + 8 > 7): the microzap grows WITHIN its one block, 512 → 1,024 B, nine
  entries (B3b review; the B3b report said "past one block"). A FAT log ZAP or `features_for_write` refuses rw at mount
  (`zfs_wgate_objs`, named) — neither occurs on a real pool (feature guids are < 50 bytes; ≤ 2,047 logs).

### c.5 Log hygiene (B10, D10)

Each txg leaves one log until every metaslab's `U` exceeds it. B10 implements OpenZFS's own bound: at the start of
txg T, (1) for metaslabs with no unflushed changes (their bitmap equals their SM-only state), set `U[i] = T`
(`metaslab_unflushed_bump`, `metaslab.c:4044-4077`); (2) flush up to K metaslabs with the oldest `U`: append their net
unflushed allocs/frees (bitmap XOR SM-only state) to **their own SM** — `dbg` words, one-word entries relative to
`ms_start` in ashift units (two-word for runs > 2^15 units; spacemap_v2 is active), straddle padding, `smp_length`,
`smp_alloc += net`, histogram recomputed from the bitmap — then `U[i] = T` (`metaslab_flush`, `metaslab.c:4196-4231`);
(3) destroy every log with txg < min `U` (≠ 0): FREE its blocks in log T, zero its MOS slot, remove its ZAP entry,
spacemap_histogram −1 (`spa_log_spacemap.c:911-940`). Target: ≤ 16 agnos logs alive.

**(review fix) Which bitmap, and when.** "Bitmap" in (1) and (2) is the § c.2 **allocated (true-state)** bitmap — the
one agnos's frees clear — never the allocatable view; with the original "bits never cleared" rule a metaslab where agnos
only freed looked clean to (1) and empty to (2), and both moved `U[i]` past the logs holding those FREEs → leaked space
(MUST). The SM-only state is re-derived by re-reading the metaslab's SM (no third resident bitmap). The flush delta is
**snapshotted before any allocation or free of T** — OpenZFS flushes in `spa_flush_metaslabs` before `vdev_sync`
writes T's entries (`spa.c:10673-10678`), and `U[i] = T` means "T's own entries are still applied from log T"; a delta
taken after T's allocations (e.g. the COW of this very SM's last block) would put T's ALLOC both in the SM and in log
T → "overlapping" panic at the next writable import. Every flush's FREE runs are added to the deferred bitmap (§ c.2).
The B10 gate must include a flush of a metaslab whose unflushed changes are agnos FREEs only.

> **(B10, as built — `zfs_write.cyr` § "B10"; the record in § h)** `zfs_wl_plan` runs FIRST in `zfs_commit1`, before
> the dataset phase (nothing of T allocated or freed), and walks the metaslabs with U ≠ 0 oldest U first (ties by
> number), exactly `spa_flush_metaslabs`' loop: a CLEAN one (no log entry since U — the scan's +48) is bumped, a DIRTY one
> flushed (`zfs_wl_flush`); `want` is `spa_estimate_metaslabs_to_flush` over agnos's per-log summary with
> `zfs_unflushed_log_txg_max` = **14** and no binding block limit (`zfs_wl_est`, at least 1), and past it every
> metaslab whose U is 14 txgs old is flushed too — two txgs of slack under this section's 16 for txgs too full to
> flush (k.115). The flush: the space map ALONE
> replayed strictly into `ZWA_FSB`, the delta against the allocated bitmap (its net must equal the scan's, +56),
> appended as `space_map_write` writes a tree — `zfs_sm_dbg` debug words and each run through `zfs_sm_seg` (host-proven:
> one-word entries, two-word ones under spacemap_v2 past 2^15 units, the pad word) — into the dirtied blocks, growing
> the object past `dn_maxblkid` and its levels where the append needs (`zfs_wf_levels`); `smp_length`, `smp_alloc` +=
> the net, the histogram the EXACT one of the pre-T state (k.109), U = T through the U preset (`zfs_wc_pre`). An empty
> delta appends nothing (U = T only). A flush the arena (beside every admission reservation, `zfs_wf_mfit`), the dirty
> set or the extent list cannot hold waits (`skip=1`); the destruction of every live log is reserved before any U moves
> (`skip=3` otherwise) — and, since the B10 review, hygiene has its own 384 KiB of the metadata arena that no admission
> reaches (`ZWL_HAR`, `zfs_wf_alim`), while every admission holds back the next txg's extent need (`zfs_wl_hres`,
> `zfs_wf_xfit`), so a txg its admissions filled still takes its first flush (k.119); the destroy's backstop is a named
> commit failure (k.121). Then `zfs_wc_plan` — once log T's object is chosen — destroys every log below the least
> U (`zfs_wl_kill_logs`): a ghost first (k.108), its tree killed (`zfs_wc_kill`), its slot zeroed, its key removed from
> the log ZAP (`zfs_wz_op`), `spacemap_histogram` − 1 each. The publish moves each flushed metaslab's net into
> `smp_alloc` (+16), zeroes its +48 / +56 before log T is summed, drops the destroyed logs from the table and the
> refcount. Both of this section's cases run on disk on every lane of 20+ txgs (FREE-only flushes / flushes of a
> metaslab holding a copy of its own space map's block, of all flushes): ashift9 4 / 37 of 56, wr 15 / 69 of 129, main 13 / 60 of 286, full 3 / 8 of 16, logs 10 / 5 of 17, fz 4 / 95 of 460.
## (d) The COW write-out order of `zfs_commit()` (txg T = previous + 1)

1. **Dataset phase**, per dirty objset (independent of the MOS):
   1. Per dirty non-special object, levels bottom-up: finalise each dirty block — data as written; an all-zero data
      block on a dataset with compression ≠ off becomes a hole with birth T if the replaced BP had birth ≠ 0 (else a
      birth-0 hole); an indirect whose children are all holes becomes a birth-T hole (`lsize 1 << ibs`, type, level)
      **only if the BP it replaces had logical birth ≠ 0, else it stays an all-zero birth-0 hole** (review fix: the
      `io_bp_orig` birth test in `zio.c:2097-2104` applies at every level, not just L0);
      re-materialised hole indirects give their children birth-B holes (WPA § 4). Fill (WPA § 5). Compress (data OFF;
      metadata ZLE, OFF if `roundup(clen, 1 << ashift) ≥ lsize`, tail zeroed to the rounded psize). Allocate `copies`
      DVAs. Checksum over psize. **Write to the device now** (data first is fine — it is all unreferenced space).
      Store the BP in the parent indirect / `dn_blkptr`; born(ds, new); kill(ds, old) (§ e.2). Then the dnode:
      `dn_used` (Σ dsize, bytes, `USED_BYTES`), `dn_maxblkid`, `nlevels` growth, flags.
   2. **User/group/project deltas** for every dnode changed in this objset (§ e.3) → dirty the special ZAPs (objects
      −1/−2/−3) → sync them like 1.1 (never charged themselves).
   3. Meta-dnode L0 dnode blocks (fill = allocated dnodes, a multi-slot dnode once; all-free block → hole birth T) →
      meta-dnode indirects (6 levels on OpenZFS ZPL datasets) → `objset_phys` (new `os_meta_dnode`, special dnodes; ZIL
      header, `os_type`, `os_flags`, zero MACs, lsize preserved) → objset BP (fill = Σ meta-dnode top fills; metadata
      copies) → born/kill the objset BP into the dataset.
   4. Dataset deltas → `ds_bp`, `ds_*_bytes`, the `dsl_dir` chain to the root (§ e.1) — edits in dirty MOS dnode blocks.
2. **MOS phase**:
   1. Apply MOS mutations: dataset/dir bonuses, deadlist ZAP + bpobj appends (§ e.2), `free_bpobj` appends + `$FREE`,
      `empty_bpobj` and `spacemap_histogram` refcounts, `U[i]`, B10's SM appends and log destruction, the **new log
      object's dnode** (§ c.4) and its ZAP entry.
   2. Partition dirty MOS blocks. **Chain** = the log's data (and indirect) blocks; every MOS dnode block holding the
      log dnode, the `$MOS` dir or the root dir (their bonuses depend on the final MOS delta); the MOS meta-dnode
      indirects; MOS `objset_phys`. **Non-chain** = everything else (ZAP blocks, bpobj blocks, the U block, SM blocks,
      other dnode blocks).
   3. Non-chain blocks: finalise, ZLE, allocate 3 DVAs, checksum, write; kill predecessors (MOS kills are frees, or
      `free_bpobj` for a gang/dedup predecessor).
   4. Chain blocks: **compression OFF**, so psize = lsize is known before content; allocate 3 DVAs each; kill
      predecessors. **(B4: chain INDIRECTS are ZLE into a reserved psize, re-planned when it overflows — k.25.)** The log's data size was reserved from an upper bound: `8 × (2·(#ALLOC DVAs + #FREE DVAs) +
      2·#metaslabs + pads)`, rounded up as § c.4. (MAY later: reserved-size ZLE for the meta-dnode L1 with re-plan when
      the real output does not fit — OpenZFS's convergence in miniature.)
   5. Now every allocation and free of T is known: compute ΔMOS (Σ dsize born − killed; comp = uncomp = Σ psize) and
      write it into `$MOS` (`used`, `comp`, `uncomp`, `bd[HEAD]`) and the root dir (`… bd[CHILD]`).
   6. Encode log T (§ c.4) into its reserved block(s).
   7. Fill the chain bottom-up: log data → log dnode (`dn_blkptr`, bonus, `dn_used`) → its dnode block → meta-dnode
      indirects → MOS `objset_phys` (`os_type` META, ZIL header zero) → MOS objset BP (fill = MOS object count; 3 DVAs;
      birth T). Checksum and write each.
3. **Wait for every write; FLUSH** (`blk_flush_on(zfs_backend)`).
4. **Uberblock T**: copy of the best ub with `ub_txg = T`, `ub_timestamp = max(ntp_now(), previous)`,
   `ub_rootbp` = the new MOS BP, `ub_software_version` 5000, `ub_mmp_magic` 0xa11cea11, delay/config/checkpoint 0,
   `ub_version`/`ub_guid_sum`/`ub_raidz_reflow_info` copied; slot `n = T % (128 KiB >> min(max(ashift,10),13))`; slot
   = ub + zeros + eck (SHA-256, verifier `(leaf offset of this slot, 0, 0, 0)`); written into L0, L1, L2, L3. Labels
   themselves (`[0, 128 KiB)` of each) are never written.
5. **FLUSH.**
6. **Publish**: refresh `ZR_UB`, `ZR_MOS`, every touched dataset slot (meta-dnode copy, SA numbers), `zfs_txg`,
   `zfs_used`; drop the dirty set; the txg's ALLOC bits stay set, its FREE runs are cleared in the allocated bitmap and
   set in the deferred bitmap (review fix, § c.2) — unallocatable until the next mount. Cache slots
   need no invalidation: every rewritten block has a new (DVA, birth) key, and no extent is reused within a boot
   (`zfs.md` § 3).

> **(B3b, as built — the null txg; the dataset phase d.1 is B4's.)** `zfs_commit` → `zfs_wc_plan` (rounds, § c.3) →
> `zfs_commit_ubw(rootbp)` (B2's uberblock with the new rootbp: FLUSH, four slot copies, FLUSH) → `zfs_wc_pub`. The
> plan, in one pass ("plan the whole chain first", not a fixed-point iteration — only U[] iterates, § c.3):
> (0) the edits that depend on no allocation — `spacemap_histogram` + 1, log T's dnode, the log-ZAP entry, preset
> U[] — each into a private copy of its block, read dirty-first through the reader (`zfs_wblk`: the block and every
> indirect above it up to the MOS objset_phys); (1) every NON-chain block finalised in the walk order (fill, ZLE,
> 3 copies, fletcher4 — `zfs_bp_build`), written, its BP stored in the parent's copy, its predecessor killed
> (`zfs_wc_kill`: freed — `zfs_wfree_bp` — with `$MOS` and the object's `dn_used` debited; embedded: nothing);
> (2) every CHAIN block allocated, compression OFF so psize = lsize is fixed (DVAs + prop in the parent; fill and
> checksum 0); (3) the MOS's space up the dsl_dirs (`zfs_wdd_use`: `$MOS` HEAD, then each parent through
> `parent_delta`, CHILD / CHILD_RSRV) and log T encoded (`zfs_wc_log`); (4) the chain filled bottom-up — fills
> (`zfs_fill_dnodes`, `zfs_ind_ready`, the objset's Σ top-level fills), fletcher4 — and written. Every block of T is
> written before step 3 of § d flushes. The chain is exactly § d.2.2's: log T's block; the dnode blocks holding log
> T's dnode, `$MOS`'s or any dsl_dir above it; every meta-dnode indirect; the objset_phys. On the fixtures it is 5 of
> 8 blocks (§ k.3).
> **Failure (B2's open question answered: no reload).** Nothing the reader uses moves before uberblock T is durable —
> the plan works on private copies in the writer area — so any failure before it leaves the reader at T − 1, every
> block of which is still on disk (nothing T − 1 references is written, no free is reused this boot): `zfs_wfail_now`
> names the refusal (`zfs: commit txg T failed: <why> - pool read-only until reboot`), returns the writer area with
> every table in it, and the pool is read-only until reboot. A failure after the uberblock (publish) leaves T live and
> the pool read-only. `zfs_commit` refuses (nothing done) on a read-only pool, a dirty set it does not own (until B4)
> and a full log table.

> **(B4, as built — the dataset phase d.1; `zfs_write.cyr` § "B4".)** `zfs_commit` = `zfs_wd_phase` (every dirty
> dataset, before anything of the MOS) → B3b's MOS phase (`zfs_wc_plan`) → B2's uberblock → `zfs_wc_pub` (which also
> puts each changed dataset's new meta-dnode into the reader's slot, `zfs_ds(di) + 0`). A dataset block is dirtied by
> `zfs_wd_dirty(di, obj, bid)`: `zfs_wds_open` (the dataset's write policy — checksum, compression, copies,
> redundant_metadata, refreservation, refquota, whether sha512 is active on it — and its objset_phys, whose size and the
> pool's `userobj_accounting` / `project_quota` decide its accounting), the space and quota predicates (`zfs_wd_space`,
> D13), the object's dnode in its dirty dnode block (`zfs_wos_dn`: the meta-dnode's six-level indirect chain and the
> objset_phys above it), the block and its indirects (`zfs_wblk`). ⭐ **Every BP a dirtied block replaces is judged
> when it is dirtied** (`zfs_wd_okbp`: a gang, dedup, encrypted or snapshot-owned predecessor, or `block_cloning`
> active on the pool, refuses the operation by name — never the commit), and a refused operation truncates the dirty
> set back to what it was (`zfs_dirty_trunc`: entries are popped newest first, each the head of its hash chain and the
> end of its arena). The phase, in `zfs_dirty_plan`'s order: (a) every ordinary object's blocks (data OFF —
> `ZWP_HOLE` where compression is on — metadata ZLE; each built, written, stored in its parent's copy, born and its
> predecessor killed: `zfs_wd_born` / `zfs_wd_kill`, the object's `dn_used` moved by both); (b) user / group /
> project accounting (`zfs_wd_uacct`, § e.3's note); (c) the special ZAPs, the meta-dnode's dnode blocks and
> indirects, the objset_phys (fill = Σ the meta-dnode's top-level fills), its BP into `ds_bp`, then `zfs_wd_dsdelta`
> (the dataset's born − killed into `ds_referenced/compressed/uncompressed/unique` once — every term is linear and
> `parent_delta` telescopes, so one application equals OpenZFS's block-by-block one — and up the dsl_dir chain,
> REFRSRV → HEAD at its own dir, CHILD_RSRV → CHILD above, `zfs_wdd_use`). Nothing in a dataset depends on the MOS
> phase, so a dataset block has no chain, no reservation and its exact ZLE psize (D4). The MOS blocks the phase
> dirtied (the dsl_dataset_phys, the dsl_dirs) are ordinary MOS blocks to the MOS phase: non-chain unless they share
> a dnode block with log T's dnode, `$MOS`'s dir or the root dir (the root dir is always in the chain). Measured
> (zfs-write-smoke): one rewritten 128 KiB block of a 2-level file = 20 dirty blocks (12 of the dataset: L0, L1, the
> dnode block, five meta-dnode indirects, the objset_phys, three accounting microzaps), 82 extents, 280,576 B
> allocated at ashift 9 (380,928 at 12), commit hold 0.6–0.8 s like a null txg (§ k.8).

The `zfs_wr(off, len, src)` primitive (the mirror of `zfs_rd`, `zfs.cyr:157-191`): ≤ 4 KiB per
`blk_write_sectors_on` command (the single-command NVMe path, `nvme.cyr:1249-1261`), bounces unaligned 4Kn I/O,
bounds-checked against `zfs_dev_bytes`, and **refuses** any data-area write not inside an extent allocated in the
current txg (makefs's own assertion, `makefs/zfs/vdev.c:83-110`) and any label-area write outside the 128 KiB uberblock
ring. Never the pad, boot env, nvlist or the 3.5 MiB boot region.

## (e) Accounting

### e.1 What agnos maintains (each with its oracle)

| Item | Rule (WPA) | Oracle that proves it |
|---|---|---|
| space record: log T, `U[i]`, spacemap_histogram refcount | § 6.5–6.6 | import (ENOENT/NULL rules), `zdb -bcc` "No leaks", `zdb -y`, every zdb run's refcount check, export → zdb again (OpenZFS flushes agnos's entries) |
| `ds_referenced/compressed/uncompressed/unique` | born/kill, § 7.2–7.3 | **(B0 review fix)** A5: `ds_referenced` == Σ `-dd` dsize of every object (meta-dnode and user/group/project-used objects included) + the objset block's asize — exactly the blocks reachable from `ds_bp`, exact under `zdb -P`; B-acct: `referenced == usedbydataset` on every non-clone head (born and every kill branch move `ds_referenced` and `dd_used_breakdown[HEAD]` by the same dsize, `dsl_dataset.c:150-376`), a clone's `referenced − usedbydataset` unchanged. ⛔ **Phase D cannot see it**: the async destroy charges `$FREE` from `dd_used_bytes` alone (`dsl_destroy.c:990-1007`) — the original oracle here was wrong, and a forged `ds_referenced + 4096` passed every phase. **(B4 review)** `ds_unique_bytes` on the image (h7): == `ds_referenced` on a snapshot-free non-clone head, else == Σ dsize of its objset's blocks born after `ds_prev_snap_txg` |
| `dsl_dir` used/comp/uncomp + breakdown to root, reservations (`parent_delta`) | § 7.2 | Phase D, **one dataset at a time** (each destroy + `zpool wait -t free` must leave `freeing` where it was — batched, one dataset's +X cancelled another's −X inside `$FREE`); `used == Σ usedby*`; parent `usedbychildren` = Σ children; `Δ zpool allocated == Δ root used` — **(B4 fix) minus the unused reservations**: `Δ allocated == Δ (root used − Σ usedbyrefreservation − Σ max(0, reservation − used))`, because a write into a reserved dataset below its reservation moves allocated and not the root's used (`parent_delta` 0 at the dataset and at its dir); the identity as written went red on `wr`'s `resv/r.txt` COW (28,672 B) with agnos's accounting exactly OpenZFS's. **(B4 review, MAJOR)** that subtraction — and identities 1 / 2, which sum REFRSRV back into used — made a wrong refreservation SPLIT invisible until phase D destroyed that dataset (never, for a root dataset; negative N5): now held on its own — on the image (h7: Σ breakdown == used, CHILD == Σ child dirs' used, CHILD_RSRV == Σ max(0, reserved − used), REFRSRV == max(0, refreservation − `ds_unique_bytes`), a non-clone head's HEAD == referenced) and by OpenZFS (B-acct identity 6: `usedbyrefreservation == max(0, refreservation − written)`, `written` being the unique bytes OpenZFS derives from the deadlist, not from `ds_unique_bytes`) |
| `$MOS` | § 7.2, MOS formula | A6: `$MOS dd_used` (exact, `zdb -P -dd POOL 0:max`, `zdb.c:4321-4325`) − (Σ MOS-object dsize + the uberblock rootbp's asize) == the pristine pool's residual (the fixture's `.acct` `mos` line); B-acct: the root's hidden children (`usedbychildren` − Σ children) == `$MOS` + `freeing` + `leaked` + the pristine residual — **(B0 review fix)** it was `≥ 0` and INFO, so moving bytes between the root's HEAD and CHILD breakdown passed; `Δ allocated == Δ root used` with *allocated* taken as § i.3 B now says |
| `$FREE` + `free_bpobj` sums | § 7.3 A (deferral) | after import `zpool wait -t free`: `freeing` 0, `leaked` 0 |
| deadlist `dl_*`, bpobj `bpo_*`, prev `ds_unique_bytes`, HEAD→SNAP, `empty_bpobj` refcount | § 7.4 | `zfs destroy @snap1` + scrub + `zdb -bcc` clean; `usedbysnapshots` identity; no "bpobj leaked" |
| user/group/project used + `obj-` counts | § 8 | `zfs userspace/groupspace/projectspace -Hp` == a host recount (validated on pristine images first) |
| `dn_used`, `dn_maxblkid`, fills, objset fill | § 5 | `zdb -ddddd` fill lines; harness object-count check |
| dir SIZE / LINKS, file LINKS, PARENT | § 10 | FreeBSD `rmdir`; the fixture dirsize check (strict for agnos dirs); `zdb -ddddd` (review fix: five d, `zdb.c:3790`) "possibly leaked" objects ⊆ the k.15 list (`zfs-expect.py --leakok`, by object id) |

### e.2 The kill function (exactly OpenZFS's, `dsl_dataset.c:254-376`)

`kill(ds, bp)`: hole or redacted → nothing. Embedded → accounting only (dsize 0, psize 0, ucsize = lsize **for data
types only** — review fix: `BP_GET_UCSIZE` is psize for metadata and an embedded BP's psize is 0, `spa.h:421-423`,
`:558-559`, so killing an embedded *metadata* BP moves nothing; OpenZFS embeds any level-0 non-dnode/objset type that
compresses to ≤ 112 B, `zio.c:1993-1994` — small directory microzaps and MOS ZAPs included, so B6's first add to a
small directory can kill an embedded ZAP block — check the fixture with `zdb -ddddd` on a small dir). `ds == MOS` → free
(or `free_bpobj` if gang/dedup) and **always** `$MOS −used/−psize/−ucsize` (`dsl_dataset.c:269-273`); **(review fix)**
OpenZFS frees a MOS gang through `dsl_free` → `spa_free_bplist` within the same txg, so routing it to `free_bpobj`
instead is agnos's own (legal) deferral and must *also* charge `$FREE` HEAD `+used/+psize/+ucsize` exactly as branch A
does (`:327-331`) — otherwise `$FREE` underflows when OpenZFS's async destroy debits it (`dsl_scan.c:3618-3625`). MOS
gangs exist on fragmented real pools (the fixture's forced-ganging txg produced none, `main.summary` 576: "ganged
count: 32" = gang.bin's 16 L0 × 2 DVAs). birth ≤ `ds_prev_snap_txg` → **deadlist** (B9; refused before). Else, if gang, dedup,
or `block_cloning` is active → **append to `free_bpobj`** (fill 0, checksum zeroed unless dedup) and charge `$FREE`;
otherwise **free** every DVA. Then the dataset/dir deltas of branch A or B and C. Birth = logical, or physical when
`blk_prop2` bit 63 is set. The `free_bpobj`/deadlist bpobj append (B7) is one function: COW the last 128 KiB block or
add one, header counts.
> **(B4, as built)** A dataset kill (`zfs_wd_kill`) is branch A (freed: `zfs_wfree_bp`, deferred for the boot, in log
> T) + C, or an embedded predecessor's accounting (UCSIZE only — `zfs_bp_ucsize`: lsize for a data type, 0 for
> metadata); birth = `zfs_bp_birth` (BP_GET_BIRTH: the physical birth under the rewrite flag, never for an embedded
> BP, whose word 7 is payload). Branch B (deadlist), and `free_bpobj` for gang / dedup / `block_cloning`, are REFUSED —
> when the block is dirtied (`zfs_wd_okbp`), so the commit never meets one — until B9 / B7. The born / kill sums are
> applied once per dataset per txg (`zfs_wd_dsdelta`), not per block: OpenZFS's per-block `parent_delta` calls
> telescope, so the result is identical (host vectors pin `zfs_pdelta`, gate 37).
> **(B7, as built)** Branch A's other half is built: `zfs_wd_kill` asks `zfs_wd_defer` — a gang block (any DVA's gang
> bit), a dedup block, or ANY block while `block_cloning` or `fast_dedup` is active (agnos reads no BRT: the superset of
> `brt_maybe_exists`) — and such a kill is appended to the pool's `free_bpobj` (`zfs_wfb_add`: bpobj_enqueue's stored
> BP, checksum zeroed unless dedup, fill 0; `bpo_num_blkptrs` / `bpo_bytes` and, with a ≥ 32-byte bonus, `bpo_comp` /
> `bpo_uncomp`; its block dirtied in the MOS, a new one past `dn_maxblkid`) instead of freed; the dataset's side of the
> kill is the same, and `$FREE` gets `HEAD +used/+psize/+ucsize` up to the root's CHILD once per txg (`zfs_wfb_charge`,
> at the end of the dataset phase, so the MOS phase plans the blocks it dirtied). `zfs_wd_okbp` no longer refuses
> gang / dedup BPs, and `zfs_wds_open` no longer refuses a `block_cloning`-active pool (D15). `free_bpobj` and `$FREE`
> are learnt at the write gate (`zfs_wgate_objs`; a malformed `free_bpobj` refuses rw by name, an absent one — no pool
> since SPA_VERSION_DEADLISTS — refuses such kills by name). The `empty_bpobj` refcount is not involved: `free_bpobj`
> is never the empty bpobj (bpobj.c:883) — it is B9's deadlist rule. ⚠ The MOS's own kills (`zfs_wc_kill`) still
> refuse a gang / dedup predecessor at the commit (k.27 — **(B10) DECIDED and built otherwise: a MOS gang predecessor is
> FREED in its txg, header and tree, as OpenZFS drains `spa_free_bplist` in the same sync — k.110; never free_bpobj**): a MOS deferral would put the free_bpobj block and `$FREE`'s
> dnode block into the CHAIN (their content depends on step (2)'s kills) — not built; no fixture has a MOS gang.
> **(B7 review)** The appends are METADATA the commit dirties, and B7 as built never budgeted them: forced on disk (the
> op list runs `wr`'s 20 MiB truncation as on a `block_cloning`-active pool), its first txg ran the commit out of the
> metadata arena (`dirty metadata full`, the pool read-only). Now every admission counts them (k.70): `wr`'s truncation
> takes six txgs and `free_bpobj` grows to 5,212 BPs in six blocks — past `dn_nblkptr` × 1,024, so `zfs_wf_levels`
> grew it to two levels — and OpenZFS frees all of it at import (D-drain).
> **(B9, as built — `zfs_write.cyr` § "B9")** Branch B is `zfs_wd_kill`'s first test: a non-hole BP — an EMBEDDED one too
> (it carries an UCSIZE; OpenZFS deadlists it) — with `BP_GET_BIRTH ≤ ds_prev_snap_txg` goes to the head's deadlist,
> `zfs_wdl_add` = `dsl_deadlist_insert`: the entry is the largest mintxg below the birth (the deadlist's committed ZAP
> read once per txg into `ZWA_DLE`, `zfs_wdl_load`; none → refused by name), an entry still naming the pool's
> `empty_bpobj` is MATERIALISED first (`zfs_wdl_mat` = `dle_enqueue`'s `bpobj_alloc` + `bpobj_decr_empty`: a new MOS
> object from `zfs_wmos_free` — `DMU_OT_BPOBJ`, 128 KiB blocks, ibs 17, 3 blkptrs, a 48-byte `BPOBJ_HDR` (56 while
> `livelist` is active) — its key's value in the deadlist MICROZAP updated in place, `features_for_write`
> `com.delphix:empty_bpobj` − 1 in place), the BP appended by B7's append generalised (`zfs_wbpo_add`, any bpobj; an
> embedded BP stored as blk_prop + logical birth), `dl_used / dl_comp / dl_uncomp` += dsize / PSIZE / UCSIZE in the
> deadlist's bonus; an OLD-format deadlist (one BPOBJ) takes the BP itself. The dataset's side, summed per txg in its
> SNAPSHOT SIDE (`ZWA_DSB`, `zfs_dsb`: deadlist, previous snapshot, its `ds_prev_snap_txg`, whether the head is its
> `ds_next_snap_obj`, the dir's `dd_origin_txg` — a clone's origin's creation txg, else 0) and applied once by
> `zfs_wd_dsdelta`: `ds_referenced / compressed / uncompressed` −= every kill (C), `ds_unique_bytes` and the `dsl_dir`
> chain (`zfs_wdd_use`, `parent_delta`) move by born − branch A only; branch B moves the head dir's breakdown HEAD → SNAP
> for the blocks born after `dd_origin_txg` (no `dd_used_bytes`, no parent: `dsl_dir_transfer_space`, only with
> `DD_FLAG_USED_BREAKDOWN`) and the previous snapshot's `ds_unique_bytes` for the blocks born after ITS
> `ds_prev_snap_txg` when the head is its next. Gang / dedup / cloned blocks are deadlisted like any (the `free_bpobj`
> deferral is branch A's, `zfs_wd_okbp` no longer refuses a snapshot-owned BP). A CLONE is written exactly so — its
> origin's blocks (born ≤ `dd_origin_txg`) go to its deadlist with neither unique nor HEAD → SNAP — unless it has a
> LIVELIST (refused at the gate, k.92). **Budget** (k.70's rule): every admission counts its snapshot-owned kills per
> entry (`zfs_wdl_q`: `zfs_wblk`'s predecessors, the objset_phys, B5's and B7's kill lists) and `zfs_wf_mfit` adds
> `zfs_wdl_meta` — each counted entry's appends (`zfs_wbpo_meta`, k.70's formula + `ZWF_FBM`), a materialisation's dnode
> block and ZAP block, 32 KiB per snapshotted dataset (its deadlist's and its previous snapshot's dnode blocks) and ONE
> entry's first append in reserve (`ZWK_DLMAT`, 144 KiB, + a ZAP block) for an entry the next admission reaches first
> (k.91).
> **(B9 review, k.99 / k.100 / k.101)** Every load and every refusal of a deadlist is now an OP's, never the commit's:
> `zfs_wds_open` counts the accounting microzaps' snapshot-owned predecessors (the commit COWs them with `zfs_wc_in`
> set, where nothing counted them — B9 loaded the head's deadlist INSIDE the commit when they were a txg's only
> snapshot-owned kills), and `zfs_wdl_q` judges an entry's MATERIALISATION when it first counts it (`zfs_wdl_matok`:
> the `empty_bpobj`'s last reference, a fat deadlist — `zfs_wdl_mat` keeps both as its backstop). The reserve is GONE:
> a write or a truncation PREDICTS each block's whole path before dirtying it (`zfs_wdl_path`, dirty-first — the
> deadlist bytes with every predecessor zfs_wblk will count) and ends its txg where the next block does not fit; only
> a block first in its txg is refused (`one block does not fit a txg: the snapshot intervals its path reaches, N`); a
> truncation resolves every snapshot-owned block's entry before its first txg (`zfs_wf_okscan`, the scan's per-entry
> counts in `ZWA_DLE` +64) and `zfs_wf_dlscan` judges every materialisation it will need and bounds what a LATER
> txg's first step needs — one it might not hold is refused before txg 1 commits. Branch B is taken only where it can
> be: `ds_prev_snap_txg` ≥ TXG_INITIAL (`ZWK_TXGI`), whatever object `ds_prev_snap_obj` names (k.101). The op list's
> plan line carries `dlx=` — appends whose entry no admission counted — 0 on every txg of every lane.

### e.3 User/group/project (D3)

For every dnode changed in a ZPL objset with user accounting (objset ≥ 2 KiB, userused dnode present): old side from the
committed dnode (`dn_flags` bits 1/3, `DN_USED_BYTES`, uid/gid/projid at SA hdr +24/+32/+128-if-`ZFS_PROJID`), new side
from the final dnode; delta `±(512 + used)` on `"%llx"` keys, `±1` on `"obj-%llx"` keys, project only when project quota
is enabled; set bits 1 and 3 on every dnode written; keys reaching 0 are removed. Applied after the dnode's `dn_used` is
final (d.1.2).

**(review fix) Scope, exactly as OpenZFS.** Deltas come only from dnodes whose bonus is SA (44) — bonus-held or
spill-held (`bonuslen == 0 && bonustype == SA`) — because `dmu_objset_userquota_get_ids` returns without
`DN_ID_OLD/NEW_EXIST` for anything else (`dmu_objset.c:2191-2290`); the master node, SA REGISTRY/LAYOUTS,
`DELETE_QUEUE` and other ZAP objects get bits 1/3 set when written (`dnode_sync.c:703-713`) but contribute no delta.
"Project quota enabled" means *both* `project_quota` enabled on the pool **and** the objset_phys being 4 KiB on disk
(the projectused dnode @2048 exists) — `dmu_objset_projectquota_enabled`, `dmu_objset.c:1813-1818`; a 2 KiB objset
keeps its size and gets no project deltas (OpenZFS grows it in memory on its own next open). Bit 3
(`USEROBJUSED_ACCOUNTED`) is set only when `userobj_accounting` is enabled (`dmu_objset.c:1806-1810`).
**(B6 review)** That growth makes `dmu_objset_projectquota_enabled` TRUE on FreeBSD for such a dataset, so its creates
carry `PROJID` + `ZFS_PROJID` — a create there is REFUSED by name (`dse +104` bit 64; § f.1); B4 / B5's writes keep the
rule above (the object is unchanged either way; only the objset's size and the projectused ZAP differ, k.60).
> **(B4, as built — `zfs_wd_uacct` / `zfs_wd_uqapply`)** Which dnodes are "synced" (OpenZFS: the dirty ones,
> `os_synced_dnodes`) is found by DIFFING every dirty dnode block against T − 1's (the reader's slot holds T − 1's
> meta-dnode until the publish): a slot whose bytes changed is a synced dnode — its old side (T − 1's flags,
> `DN_USED_BYTES`, ids) subtracted, `USERUSED_ACCOUNTED` (+ `USEROBJUSED_ACCOUNTED`) set, its new side (its final
> `dn_used`, after step (a)) added. Ids: `zfs_sa_ids` (`zpl_get_file_info`, `zfs_quota.c:42-110` — which also accepts a
> ZNODE (17) bonus, uid @128 / gid @136, so agnos does too) over the bonus or, for a spill-held SA, the spill block.
> The deltas are summed per key (`userquota_update_cache`), a key whose sum is 0 is not touched (`zap_increment`
> returns on delta 0, so its ZAP block is not dirtied), and each other key is `zap_increment`ed in the special
> MICROZAP (value + delta; the chunk zeroed at 0, `mzap_remove`; a new chunk with 512 B growth for a new key); a FAT
> accounting ZAP refuses the dataset at `zfs_wds_open` (its add / remove is B8). The oracle is a host RECOUNT (§ i.2
> h7), validated first on every pristine fixture image: it holds exactly on what OpenZFS wrote.
> **(B4 review)** The accounting ZAP blocks are first dirtied INSIDE the commit, so everything that could refuse them is
> now judged when the operation is dirtied: `zfs_wds_open` runs `zfs_wd_okbp` on each accounting ZAP's BP (a gang,
> dedup, encrypted or snapshot-owned block refuses the dataset), refuses one with indirects, and records how many NEW
> keys the ZAPs can still take (free chunks + 8 per 512 B of growth to 128 KiB, the least of them, dse +120);
> `zfs_wd_dirty1` reserves, per operation, 6 entries of the txg's delta table (`ZWK_MAXUQ`) and — where T − 1's dnode
> lacks the accounting flags, an upgraded pool's — 2 new keys per ZAP (B4's verb keeps the ids, so an accounted dnode
> adds no key). The commit's own checks stay as backstops. The add / remove-at-0 / growth paths, which no fixture COW
> reaches (§ k.37), run in memory on every rw lane (`zfs_wst_uacct`, ZFS_WRITE_SELFTEST).
> **(B8, as built — `zfs_wz_urs`, `zfs_wd_urs1`)** A FAT accounting ZAP is written, no longer refused: `zfs_wd_uqapply`
> goes through the one ZAP path (`zfs_wz_get` / `zfs_wz_op`: `zap_increment` = lookup, then update, or remove at 0 —
> the leaf's chunks freed, `zap_num_entries` − 1). dse +120 now packs 16 bits per ZAP (`zfs_wz_ucap`: a microzap's room
> for NEW keys, 0xFFFF a fat one). Because the commit cannot grow its arena, every op's reservation (`zfs_wd_urs1`, which
> replaced the B4 reservations in `zfs_wd_dirty1`, `zfs_wf_begin`, `zfs_wu_rsv` and a create's two ids) does at
> ADMISSION what the commit would need: a microzap without room for the op's new keys is UPGRADED there and then
> (`zfs_wz_upgrade`; OpenZFS does it inside that sync's `zap_increment` — k.78); a fat one has its header and the
> leaves of the op's keys (`"%llx"` and `"obj-%llx"` of each id) dirtied now, and `nk × (a leaf + a table block)` + one
> indirect per ZAP reserved for the splits the commit may make (`zfs_wz_mres`, counted by `zfs_wf_mfit`, cleared with
> the dirty set). Refused by name: an accounting ZAP whose pointer table is already EXTERNAL (its doubling would
> allocate unbudgeted inside the commit — > 1,024 leaves, ≈ 10^5 owners; k.77). On disk (fixture RECIPE 7's `fz`):
> `uids` (user- and groupused fat, 2 × 1,102 keys) takes a write and a create (updates) and an unlink (the last owner's
> two keys removed at 0); `uidm` (a 128 KiB userused microzap of 2,046 keys, one chunk free) is upgraded by agnos's
> first create there — h7's recount and the guest's `zfs userspace` agree on both. A fat accounting ADD needs a NEW
> owner, which no agnos op makes on an accounted pool (k.86).

### e.4 What is left to OpenZFS, deliberately

Metaslab `smp_alloc` and space map entries until B10 flushes; vdev
and class stats (none on disk); `zpool history` (no record of agnos's txgs); errlog; `written` (derived);
`free_bpobj` processing (OpenZFS's async destroy); space freed by agnos becomes reusable at the next mount.
