**B11 gate record (2026-10-12 / 13, its own track — worktree `zfs-b11` on the merged tree) — POLICY B, THE DEFERRAL
WINDOW, THE COMMIT'S HOLD, THE CRASH CUT, UTF-8 NAMES** (`zfs_write.cyr` § "B11: POLICY B", `zfs_wenc.cyr`
`zfs_u8_valid`, `io.cyr` `kirqw_point`; k.124–k.130). As built: a `write`#1 through an fd joins its object's open txg
and commits at `close`#6 (`zfs_wp_close` — also from `proc_destroy_fd_table`, so at exit AND at a reap), `sync`#12 and
`power_flush` (`zfs_wp_sync`), the § g thresholds (4 MiB data / 2 MiB metadata: `by=full`), the age bound (`by=age`) and
before any entry that observes the pending object or the pool's space (`zfs_wp_obs` / `zfs_wp_flush` — k.124's narrower
rule, with its proof); a truncating open still commits in its syscall. Deferred frees released after TXG_DEFER_SIZE = 2
(k.125, with the crash argument for agnos's commit order); the hold measured by the commit's own clock and windowed for
interrupts on NVMe / AHCI (k.126); the crash cut (`\boot\cmdline zfs_cut=[CLASS:]N`, four cut lanes, k.127); UTF-8 names
on `utf8only` datasets (k.128); eight defects fixed on the way (k.129). **Fixture:** RECIPE unchanged (8); B11's zfsx
payload re-keyed the cold build `r8-edfb6e66c0a9-c579a04a` → **`r8-4ee74703473a-c579a04a`** (expected — k.129 (f) is
what it moved). **Size and builds:** § g's "Measured after B11" (11,672 B production, 2.0× § h's estimate; the
`ZFS_WRITE_OPS` build binding at 236,624 B); aarch64 32 / 17, the identical set. **Gates, on the final source** (kernel,
scripts and payload unchanged from the final builds on): zfs-write-smoke **21/21 lanes PASS boot + audit** (h1–h9 and
space / uacct / holes / fatzap / sa / room on every rw lane; six firmware VOIDs retried; ≈ 62 min for the lanes) and
**OpenZFS verify 12/12 — 36 steps each**, in two guest boots (the verify's 8-lane cap, k.129 (h)): b1 ashift9, the four
cut lanes and wr in 456 s; b2 wr@u0, main, full, full@pre, logs and fz in 655 s — b2 run once more on the run's
kept images (the lane images the verify reads, never writes) after the session that started it was stopped inside it;
`uspace` 12/12. The op list on agnos9: `cp` 8 × 64 KiB through one fd = **1 txg, at its close, under policy B** (8 under
policy A); 72 × 64 KiB (4.5 MiB) = 3 txgs, 2 of them the 4 MiB threshold's; the observe, sync and age hooks; the
release op (P + 2's block elsewhere, P + 3's ON P's freed block). The run's commits by trigger: 1,391 op, 45 close, 10
full, 6 observe, 6 sync, 6 age. Hold per txg: k.126 (1) (ashift9 12.8–69.3 ms, mean 20.5; main mean 22.3; AHCI 80.5;
USB 76.6). **zfs-ring3-smoke PASS** (zfsx exit 95 — clauses 156–161: one txg at close for 8 × 64 KiB, a read through
another fd and a stat committing first, `sync`#12, the exit's teardown committing an open file; 28 refusals by name and
in order; 44 txgs 287..330; the commit at close's per-CPU ticks 10 / 10 / 10 / 10; OpenZFS 36/36 in 206 s; 5 min 20 s).
**zfs-smoke PASS 8/8** (4 min 22 s). **check.sh 38/38** (81 s; gate 37's 2,332 cases, 1,358 of them the UTF-8
validator's). **Negatives** (each applied through cyim in the worktree, the source restored and sha256-checked against
its pre-mutant hash; every one red in its phase): **N1** a close that does not commit (`zfs_wp_close`'s flush skipped)
→ `ashift9@cut-data` red in the kernel's own count (no `commit txg 28` / `30`, the knob armed for the wrong txg), h3
(new txgs 26..29), sa, and the guest's H / A2 / B-manifest / C (the cut took hello.txt's write); **N2**
commit-before-observe skipped (`zfs_wp_obs` dropped from the read arm) → zfs-ring3-smoke red, zfsx exit 150 (the
snapshotted root's `tiny.bin` RMW did not read back — the read arm served T − 1); **N3** a deferred free released one txg early (the window's

wrong) and lane wr red (the op list's refusal accepted, the extra file `/agnoswr/utf8/surrogate-…`, the guest's
B-manifest / B-acct / C-rt). The windows' measure (k.126 (3)) is a fifth scratch build, `zfs_wirq_on` answering 0:

**B11 review-fix record (2026-10-13, its own track — worktree `zfs-b11`; k.131 (a)–(k), k.132).** Eleven findings, each
confirmed by reading the code and fixed in this cut, none rejected. As built:
- **The multi-txg race (a):** the entry rule re-runs after every `zfs_wf_yield` (`by=yield`); `zfs_wp_own` makes every
  op that opens a txg of its own commit a pending one first (`by=begin`); `zfs_wf_drop` never discards one silently.
- **The exit hook at the DEATH (b):** `zfs_release_pid` in `proc_death_release`.
- **No commit in an interrupt handler (c):** `zfs_wp_owed`, run at the next B1, aged meanwhile.
- **`ZR3_BUS=ahci` / `ZR3_KSTACK_HW=1` in the ring-3 harness (d).**
- **`zfs_wirq_no` for `power_flush`'s final commit (e).**
- **The fresh txg's second -2 dropped (f).**
- **A continued txg's block-0 growth judged and charged before it is applied (g):** `zfs_wf_gsz`.
- **The observers' failed commit-before-observe is -1 (h):** stat, `SEEK_END` and statfs.
- **A failed release answers 0 (i).**
- **The duplicate `zfs_wt_msonly` removed (j).**
- **The stale comments rewritten (k).**

**The op list:** the RACE op (`zfs_wt_race` + the yield hook `zfs_wt_yhook`, agnos9, after the release op; the smoke's
`b11_ops` +2 model lines, `b11_nw` 10 → 11, two new `--want`s; `zfs-sa-check.py` puts the gap's write on the race op's
clock step). **Fixture:** unchanged (RECIPE 8, `r8-4ee74703473a-c579a04a`). The payload did not change.

## (i) The write oracle

### i.1 Producers

- `ZFS_WRITE_SELFTEST` (`build.sh` beside `:459`, called beside `main.cyr:2184-2187`): a fixed op list through the same
  inner functions the syscall arms use, under `fs_spin_lock`; prints `zfs-wtest: <op> <path> ok|FAIL <why>`,
  `zfs-wtest: commit txg N`, `F <path> <size> <sha256>`, finally `zfs-wtest: done txg=N ops=M fails=0`; skips itself
  when its marker file exists. Ops: micro create; ≥ 50-char name (upgrade); `dir3000` insert/remove; ~200 inserts (splits);
  tiny / one-record / multi-record-with-L1 / sparse writes; RMW in `off` (4 K records) and `big1m` (1 MiB); append to a
  gzip block in `gz`; zstd partial overwrite (must be −1); `AO_TRUNC` of `gang/gang.bin`; unlink of a hard link, of
  `dnode/n00` (SA xattrs, large dnode), of a symlink; mkdir/rmdir; renames (same dir, across, over, a directory); link;
  short and 400-byte symlink (the latter −1); refusals (cross-dataset, virtual dir, graft, `zpl4`, `agnostank` before B9).
  Deterministic clock under the flag (pool ub timestamp + op counter) so post-write images are byte-reproducible.
  **(B2, built)** The flag is `ZFS_WRITE_OPS` (`ZFS_WRITE_SELFTEST` is the state dump and writes nothing). The clock is
  `max(ts0 + ops, previous)`. The list runs only on a pool named `agnos*` (the fixture recipe's names; anything else
  prints `zfs-wtest: skipped (pool <name> is not a fixture pool)`), because every boot of the image runs it. The
  marker skip needs a file the list creates, so it lands with B6's create, as the list's first op.
  **(B5, built)** The file-data ops run what the syscalls run (`zfs_wopen`, then `zfs_wf_write` / `zfs_wtrunc`) with
  their bytes from **agnos-gen-1** generated by FILE offset straight into the dirty block (`zfs_wf_copy`, mode 1;
  zeros: mode 2) — the Cyrius port is `zfs_wenc.cyr` `zfs_gen_*` under `ZFS_WRITE_OPS`, byte-identical with
  `zfs-expect.py`'s `gen` on gate 37 (31 vectors: its reference vectors, word / byte edges against a guard page, and the
  SHA-256 of every seed / offset / length the list and the ring-3 exerciser write). Per op: `zfs-wtest: write|trunc begin
  txg=T <path> …`, a plan line per txg, `zfs-wtest: commit txg N` per txg, `zfs-wtest: write|trunc <path> -> <r>
  txgs=<k>`, the read-back of its last txg (`zfs_wt_rb`), **`zfs-wtest: F <path> <size> <sha256>`** (the file through
  the reader at T — the walk's own format behind the `zfs-wtest: ` prefix, so zfs-manifest-diff never reads it as the
  walk), then `… ok` / `… ok refused (<why>)` / `… FAIL <why>`. The list (`zfs_wt_b5`), after the B4 COWs and before
  the null txgs: `agnos9` tiny RMW of an embedded 8-byte file, a whole 128 KiB record (not read), hello.txt 26 B →
  300,000 B (growth 512 → 128 KiB, nlevels 1 → 2), a sparse write 1 MiB past `dir20/e01`'s end, `AO_TRUNC` of
  rand-2m.bin; `agnostank` RMW in `off` (4 KiB records, compression off) and `big1m` (1 MiB records), `big1m`
  truncated to 1,572,987 B (the any-length truncation: tail zeroed, block 2 freed), an append across `gz`'s last gzip
  block, a partial `lzjb` block, a zstd partial overwrite (**refused**), 128 KiB of zeros over `sha256/rand-256k.bin`
  block 1 (a birth-T hole), `AO_TRUNC` of `gang/gang.bin` (**refused**: gang frees are B7's), `AO_TRUNC` of
  `sha512/text-64k.txt`; `agnoswr` the 0-byte `empty` (a hole block 0 grown 512 → 1,024 B without being dirtied),
  copies=2 RMW, `rs4k` written at block 1024 (nlevels 2 → 3), `quota/q.bin` (refquota) in quota; `agnosfull`
  `fill-5.bin` + 4 MiB at ONE bitmap slot — **SHORT** at the slop (524,288 B in 3 txgs: the in-flight predicate cut
  them, k.36) — then 128 KiB more, **refused** ENOSPC. The smoke holds the same list as `zfs-expect.py` ops (the fill's
  length taken from the kernel's `->` line), regenerates the in-place files' original bytes from the fixture recipe
  (`scripts/smoke/lib/zfs-orig.py`: rand / text / printf, each checked against the manifest by `zfs-expect.py`), and
  holds every written file's LAST F line and the walk after the list to the expected manifest; the guest's phase B
  diffs the same expected manifest (`<lane>.ops` + `<lane>.orig/`).
- The ring-3 exerciser (`zfsx` + write clauses, exec'd from `agnostank/payload`; exit 95 = pass), then extract the pool
  partition (`zfs-ring3-test.py:67-69`) and verify lane `ring3`.
  **(B5, built)** Clauses 100–113 write `agnostank/custom`'s `custom.txt` (at `/somewhere/else`): `AO_RDWR`, 40 bytes
  over its 18, `SEEK_END` (the SA size), read back, 1,000 bytes at 40 (single-block growth to 1,536 B), 100 bytes at
  300,000 (growth to 128 KiB, a hole, nlevels 1 → 2), the hole and the writes read back, `AO_WRONLY|AO_TRUNC` (its own
  txg), 10 bytes — **five txgs** — a read-only fd refusing `write`, ZPL-4 `zpl4/old.txt` refusing `AO_RDWR`, the
  read-only root refusing `AO_TRUNC`, a zstd partial overwrite refused; 81–85 / 88 now assert refusal on the read-only
  ROOT dataset (snapshots until B9). On exit 95 the harness kills QEMU, extracts the pool as lane **`main@ring3`**
  (base `main`: the verify's `lane%%@*` rule), writes its `.ops` (`RING3_OPS`), `.orig/` (`zfs-orig.py`), `.txg` (T0 + 5,
  computed — never read off the image), runs the host audit (h1–h3 `--final-txg --min-txgs 5`, h6 / h7 / h8 under
  `--space`) and `zfs-fixture.sh --verify`. zfsx IS the fixture's payload: B5 re-keyed the fixture (r3-1839cf8d4036).
  **(B6 + its review, built)** Clauses 114–130 create on `agnostank/custom`: `mkdir` b6, `AO_CREAT|AO_EXCL` b6/new.txt +
  100 bytes, **`symlink`#63 b6/sym → new.txt — a SUCCESS** (custom holds the symlink layout since fixture RECIPE 5) read
  back by `lstat` (`0120755`, nlink 1, size 7), `readlink` and `stat` through it, `AO_CREAT|AO_TRUNC` b6/t.txt + 10 (a new
  name: not truncated) — six txgs, `.txg` T0 + 11 — and the refusals: an existing name, `AO_EXCL`, a symlink in
  `coff/kid` (no symlink layout), one whose target holds a NUL, one on the read-only root, a 50-byte name, a 266-byte
  RELATIVE `AO_CREAT` path (refused whole — `open`#7 truncated it to 254 bytes and created the truncated name; its
  absence is checked). Every refused ZFS verb logs `zfs: <verb> refused (<why>)` (k.58) and the harness holds the run to
  `EXPECT_REFUSED` — 14 refusals, each by its NAME, in order. Before the guest, `zfs-sa-check.py`'s **ring-3 form** (`--ops
  --t0 --clock`) holds every object zfsx made or wrote to zfs_mknode's values and a FreeBSD twin, with the PRODUCTION
  clock (`ntp_now`: the RTC seed + ticks) inside the run's wall-clock window, one value per op, in op order, and GEN =
  the op's txg.
  **(B8)** What B6 refused there SUCCEEDS: clause 128's `symlink`#63 in `coff/kid` (a NEW SA layout; `readlink` reads
  `kid.txt`), clause 124's 50-byte name (b6's microzap UPGRADED; `stat` 0100644), clause 126's listing of the now-fat b6
  (6 names); clauses 148–149 create, `stat` and unlink a 255-byte name (13 name chunks) and unlink the 50-byte one —
  five more txgs (26), the refusal list two shorter (28).
- **(B8) The fat-ZAP pool's op list** (`zfs_wt_b8`, lane `fz`): its expected ops are `scripts/smoke/lib/zfs-fz-ops.py`'s —
  the same plan re-derived on the host: `b8/`'s names chosen by hash under the list's salt with the model's `zap_hash`,
  the `a` count from the model run over the same adds, and `dir3000`'s smallest leaf read off the PRISTINE image after
  the model applies the ops that reach `dir3000` first; every `zap` checkpoint line is the model's prediction.
- **(B8 review) What no lane can force, on the host and at boot:** gate 37's two failure cases — a doubling's switch
  whose old-table free fails (ZZ_IO through the maybe-grow, never swallowed; the header still mid-grow, twice; then
  the switch) and a table that may not grow (ZZ_NOGROW at the split, the maybe-growth skipped; then the move out),
  the model following agnos's named rule (k.88) — and the boot's `zfs-wb3: uafat` (every rw lane; zfs-smoke requires
  its line): the commit's FAT accounting add in memory, splits and a special dnode's level within the reservation, an
  inadmissible shape refused at admission (k.89, k.90).
  **(B9, built)** Clauses 81–85, 112, 123 and 143 assert refusal on `agnostank/zpl4` (ZPL 4) — the root is writable —
  and 150–155 (148–153 in its own track; renumbered past B8's 148–149 at the merge) WRITE IN THE SNAPSHOTTED ROOT: an RMW of `tiny.bin`, `rand-1m.bin` AO_TRUNC'd (eight records and their
  L1) and written, `hello.txt`'s EMBEDDED block rewritten and read back under `hello-link.txt`, the unlink of
  `deep/a/b/c/d/e/leaf.txt`, the emptied `e` moved to `deep/e9`, `mkdir deep/z9` + a new 20-byte file — nine txgs
  (`RING3_TXGS` 30 in its track, 35 with B8's five since the merge), every kill of a block `agnostank@snap1` owns
  deadlisted; the refusal list unchanged by B9 (30 by name in its track; 28 since the merge, B8 lifting two).
  The harness also requires the verify's B-snap and B-snapacct PASS.
  **(B9, built) The op list** (`zfs_wt_b9`, after B7's pre-list, before B5's): `agnostank`'s root — RMW of the
  EMBEDDED `small.txt`, one record of `rand-1m.bin`, `snap-changed.txt` (its block born after @snap1: freed beside the
  snapshot's deadlisted ones, one txg), `big.bin` truncated to 1,000,000 B, `text-3m.txt` AO_TRUNC'd, the unlink of
  `deep/a/b/c/d/e/leaf.txt`, `e` moved to `deep/a/e2`, `mkdir deep/b9` + a file (the root directory is a fat ZAP: B8's);
  `agnoswr` — `snap` (@s1): the EMBEDDED `keep.txt`, `rewritten.bin` (born after @s1: freed), the unlink of
  `moved.txt`; `clone` (no livelist): its origin's `keep.txt` (deadlisted, neither unique nor HEAD → SNAP), the unlink
  of its own `own.txt`; `snap2` (RECIPE 7): `a.bin`'s first 4 KiB — TWO entries materialised from `empty_bpobj` in one
  txg — `b.bin`'s second, `a.bin` AO_TRUNC'd; then `zfs-wtest: deadlist <path> …` per touched dataset. Each plan line
  gains `dl=` (deadlist appends) `dlu=` (their Σ dsize) `mat=` (materialisations); the smoke holds the txgs with
  `dl > 0` and Σ `mat` to the lane (`--dl`, `--mat`: wr / wr@u0 `mat` 2, main 0, every other lane `dl` 0).
  **(B9 review, fixture RECIPE 8)** `agnoswr`'s list grows nine ops on `edge` / `edgec` (`zfs_wt_b9`): `edge/p.bin`
  written with every deadlist read as FAT (`zfs_wt_dlfat`: refused at its admission, nothing written), `q.bin`'s block
  0 (three levels, four intervals: refused by name), `p.bin`'s (two levels, four intervals: written — four entries
  materialised in one txg, `mat=4`); the four boundary births (`edge/e4.bin` at `ds_prev_snap_txg`, `edge/e3.bin` at a
  key and the previous snapshot's `ds_prev_snap_txg`, `edgec/e4.bin` at a key, the previous's and `dd_origin_txg`,
  `edgec/c1.bin` at `ds_prev_snap_txg`); `edgec/t.bin` truncated four kill-list entries a txg (`zfs_wt_ftight`) — first
  under a deadlist table capped at 3 entries (`zfs_wt_dlmax`: refused at its SCAN, txgs=0), then in 4 txgs, the last
  two deadlisting. Every plan line ends `dlx=0` (appends no admission counted), the smoke holds it on every lane.
- **(B10, built)** Every commit prints its HYGIENE line beside its plan line (`zfs_wt_stats`): `zfs-wtest: txg T
  hygiene: flushed= bumped= destroyed= logs= ghosts= minu= fonly= self= words= skip= gang=` — the metaslabs flushed and
  bumped, the logs destroyed, the logs alive after T, the ghosts, the least U, the flushes whose delta was FREEs only
  and those of a metaslab holding a copy of its own space map's block (§ c.5's two cases), the words appended, a
  skipped flush, the MOS gang predecessors freed. Every list's every txg runs the hygiene — main's and fz's lists are
  the 300+-txg runs the bite's gate names. The DRAIN ops (`zfs_wt_b10`, after B8's, before the null txgs; k.111):
  `agnos9` drains its root DELETE_QUEUE (`zfs_wt_dq`: B7's `lz2.bin` and `lzd` freed, one txg each; `xd.txt` refused
  by name), `agnostank` drains `off`'s (`lazy.bin` in bounded parts under the knobs `zfs_wq_tight` 1 / `zfs_wt_ftight`
  4) and then runs the production trigger itself (`zfs_wt_ul`: `off/b10.bin` created, written 12 KiB, unlinked with
  `zfs_wq_auto` on — queued by its txg, drained by the same op). Lines: `zfs-wtest: drain <path> object N: freed
  txgs=K parts=P` / `… refused (<why>)`, `zfs-wtest: drain <path> -> 0 txgs=K`, `drain ok`; `zfs-wtest: unlink <path>
  object N: freed (queued by its txg, drained by K more)`. The B7 lists keep their lazy entries (`zfs_wq_auto` 0 there)
  for OpenZFS's own drain to judge.
- Crash-cut lanes (B11): halt after N device writes of the last txg, before its ub.

### i.2 Host audit (`scripts/smoke/lib/zfs-image-audit.py`, seconds)

h1 every label's `[0, 128 KiB)` byte-identical; h2 `[512 KiB, 4 MiB)` identical; h3 only ring slots `t % N` of agnos's
txgs changed, each with magic, valid eck, identical across the four labels, zero after 216 bytes — and with
`--same-rootbp T` (the write smoke), every txg ≤ T naming the pristine rootbp and **every txg above T a rootbp whose
logical birth is its own txg and that differs from the previous new txg's** (B3b review: it compared each with the
PRISTINE rootbp only, so a null txg that re-published the previous commit's MOS passed — and the guest accepts such an
uberblock, k.1: h3 is that rule's only oracle; proven on a forged copy of the `ashift9` result, txg 27 naming 26's or
25's rootbp: the old audit PASSES both, the new one fails them); h4 refused lanes
(v28, mirror-a, raidz-0) and every read-only `zfs-smoke` lane **byte-identical** (G1); h5 nothing outside the ZFS
partition changed. **(B3b)** h6 (`--space`, pool-only images): the space record replayed on the host in OpenZFS's
model (`zfs-wms-check.py --replay`, which the write smoke also runs per rw lane as `space`): every space map and log
strict, `smp_alloc` == entries, `spacemap_histogram` == space maps + logs, every nonzero `U[i]` names a log, and no log
entry for a `U == 0` metaslab — the one rule the guest cannot judge (§ c.3 note, N3 below). The verify's H runs it.
**(B4)** h7 (with `--space`): `scripts/smoke/lib/zfs-uacct-check.py` — every dnode's `dn_used` == Σ dsize of its tree
(+ spill; § k.14), every head ZPL dataset's `ds_referenced` == Σ `dn_used` + its objset block (A5's identity, before any
boot), the user / group / project-used ZAPs == a recount over every dnode (§ e.3), and every snapshot-free, non-clone
dsl_dir's compressed / uncompressed == its head dataset's + its child dirs' (added after negative N2 went green on
`wr`: a parent propagation that moves no USED bytes was invisible) — what zdb checks none of. **(B4 review, MAJOR)**
plus every dsl_dir's breakdown read straight from the MOS — Σ == `dd_used_bytes`, CHILD == Σ the child dirs'
`dd_used_bytes` (`$MOS` / `$FREE` / `$LEAK` / `$ORIGIN` included), CHILD_RSRV == Σ max(0, `dd_reserved` −
`dd_used_bytes`) over them, REFRSRV == max(0, refreservation − `ds_unique_bytes`) at a dir with a head (0 without), a
non-clone head's HEAD == `ds_referenced` — and every head's `ds_unique_bytes` against its blocks (== referenced when
snapshot-free and no clone; else Σ dsize of the BPs born after `ds_prev_snap_txg`, a subtree born no later skipped
whole). Exact on every pristine image (38 dirs on wr / main / ashift9 / full / mirror-a, snapshotted, clone, zvol and
encrypted heads among them); a wrong refreservation split (N5) was green in every phase before D.
**(B5)** h8 (with `--space`, so the verify's H runs it too; the write smoke runs it per rw lane as `holes`):
`scripts/smoke/lib/zfs-hole-check.py FINAL PRISTINE` — with hole_birth active (features_for_READ: it is not read-only
compatible), every hole BP, at every level, of every object whose dnode changed (same number, same type) is compared
with the pristine image at the same (level, blkid): a birth-0 hole where the pristine tree had a block — or a hole of
birth B, itself or punched by an ancestor — is a FAIL (the punch was not recorded: `zfs send -i` would skip it); a hole
with a birth must be ≤ the pool's txg with its dnode's type and its level (zdb's own check, `zdb.c:2683-2696`). Pristine
images trivially pass (no changed object); the B5 lanes hold 1 / 3 / 0 / 0 holes with a birth (ashift9 / main / wr /
full) and the ring-3 pool 1,023 (custom.txt's re-materialised L1: OpenZFS's `dbuf_handle_indirect_hole` children).
Validated on every pristine fixture image first (all exact); the write smoke runs it per rw lane (`uacct`), the
verify's H through the audit.
**(B8)** h9 (with `--space`; the write smoke runs it per rw lane as `fatzap`): `scripts/smoke/lib/zfs-fatzap-check.py
FINAL PRISTINE` — every ZAP of the pool (each head dataset's objects, its user / group / project-used objects and the
MOS's) held to the structure zdb never checks whole and OpenZFS ASSERTs only on the paths it happens to take: a fat
header's magic, `zap_flags` (a flagged ZAP is reported, not judged), `zt_shift`, `zap_freeblk` past every block named;
the POINTER TABLE (embedded or `zt_numblks` blocks) — every entry a leaf below `zap_freeblk` whose prefix it is, each
leaf owning exactly its one range of `2^(zt_shift − lh_prefix_len)` entries (so a split that left an entry on the OLD
leaf, or a wrong prefix, shows), `zap_num_leafs`; mid-doubling, every written entry of the new table == the old one it
doubles; every LEAF's free list (walked) == its FREE chunks == `lh_nfree`, FREE pads zero, `lh_nentries` == its ENTRY
chunks, every entry in exactly one chain — its hash's bucket, cd non-decreasing — its hash's top bits the prefix, its
arrays owned by it alone, its name NUL-terminated and RE-HASHED (salt, TOUPPER) == `le_hash`, no orphan ARRAY chunk;
`zap_num_entries` == Σ `lh_nentries`; (hash, cd) unique; a microzap's (hash, cd) unique and names terminated; every
salt non-zero and, against PRISTINE, an UPGRADED microzap's salt kept (`fzap_upgrade` copies it). The scripts/check
model (`zfs-fatzap.py`) it shares with gate 37 reproduces OpenZFS's `agnoswr/fat` (2 blocks) and `dir3000` (17) byte for
byte. Validated on every pristine fixture image (makefs's 128 KiB-block ZAPs included).
**(B9)** h6 + h7 learn the deadlists. h6 (`zfs-wms-check.py --replay`, `owned_allocated`): after the replay, every
DVA of every block of every SNAPSHOT's objset (walked from the snapshot's own `ds_prev_snap_txg`, like zdb's
`traverse_dataset`) and of every BP a deadlist or `free_bpobj` holds must be ALLOCATED — a writer that freed a block
a snapshot owns, instead of deadlisting it, leaves it free there (sanity-forced: every unit free → "snapshot 77
objset: a block a snapshot still owns is FREE"). h7 (`zfs-uacct-check.py`, `dl_check`): every deadlist of every head
and snapshot — each entry's bpobj recounted (`bpo_bytes / comp / uncomp` == Σ its BPs and subobjs, every BP in
bpobj_enqueue's form: fill 0, checksum zeroed unless dedup, an embedded one reduced), every BP of entry K born in
(K, next key], `dl_used / dl_comp / dl_uncomp` == Σ its entries; `features_for_write`'s `com.delphix:empty_bpobj`
== the deadlist entries naming the `empty_bpobj` (each deadlist once; livelists counted), the `empty_bpobj` empty; and
OpenZFS's space rule over them — each snapshot's `ds_unique_bytes` == its next's deadlist from its `ds_prev_snap_txg`
on, the dir's `dd_used_breakdown[SNAP]` == every deadlist after the first snapshot from the first's
`ds_prev_snap_txg` on (`dsl_dataset_space_wouldfree`). Validated on every pristine RECIPE 6 image first (main: 22
deadlists, 23 entries, refcount 21 == 21 references; wr: 17 / 19 / 16 == 16; both identities exact).
**(B9 review, k.105)** h6 / h7 compare `BP_GET_BIRTH` (`zfs-damage.py` `BP.gbirth`: the physical birth under
`blk_prop2`'s rewrite flag, as the kill, the deadlist insert and a traversal do), not the logical birth; `dl_check`
caps the newest entry at its owner's `ds_prev_snap_txg`. Validated on every pristine RECIPE 8 image (wr: 27 deadlists,
51 entries, refcount 36 == 36, 12 unique / SNAP identities; main: 22 / 23 / 21 == 21, 2) and on every rw lane.
**(B10)** h6 (`zfs-wms-check.py --replay`) learns the LOG HYGIENE rules OpenZFS keeps and a flushing writer can break:
every entry under a space map's debug word is of that word's type (`sm_form`: an ALLOC written as a FREE shows); no
debug txg of a metaslab's space map passes its U ≠ 0 (a flush sets U = its txg — an append past U is replayed again
from the logs, the import's "overlapping"); no log older than every U ≠ 0 (spa_cleanup_old_sm_logs). And the SM-append
deferral rule is gone, as in the kernel (k.108). Validated on every pristine fixture image first (all PASS; `makefs`
fails as before — no spacemap_histogram). `zfs-room-check.py --log LOG` takes a destroyed log's txg from its plan line
(the same ALLOC / FREE bytes) and holds every surviving log to its plan line — ⚠ (B10 review, k.123 (b)) so a
destroyed log's room figures are the kernel's own claim: only the surviving logs (≤ 16 a lane) are checked against an
independent source, and on a long lane most txgs' figures are self-referential; `zfs-sa-check.py` takes an object a drain
freed as FREED (`zfs-wtest: drain … freed`).

### i.3 Guest verify (`zfs-fixture-guest.sh` verify mode; each step prints `zfsfix: verify <lane> <step> PASS|FAIL
<why>` immediately, so a panic still leaves evidence)

- **A (userland only, before any kernel import — space-map bugs panic the FreeBSD kernel)**: A1 `zdb -lu`; A2
  `zdb -e -p DEV -u` — `ub_txg` == agnos's final txg; A3 `zdb -e -p DEV -bcc -d -m -s -y POOL` — exit 0, no signal,
  "No leaks (block sum matches space maps exactly)", no "leaked space", "block traversal size", "Error counts",
  "mismatch", "leaked" (MOS) or "DOUBLE" line; **(review fix)** plus `zdb -e -p DEV -mmmm POOL` with no
  "INCONSISTENT" line — the `smp_alloc` == Σ entries check runs only at `-mmmm`/`-dddddd` (`zdb.c:1582-1583` returns
  before it otherwise, `:1669-1673`), and nothing else ever reads a *log*'s `smp_alloc` (zdb's unflushed total and
  OpenZFS's import both sum the entries, `zdb.c:6725-6749`, `spa_log_spacemap.c:1251-1261`), so with plain `-m` a wrong
  log `smp_alloc` (§ c.4) or B10 metaslab `smp_alloc` delta is invisible. **(B0 review fix)** A3 also gates the `-s`
  vdev table's read/write/**cksum** error columns: `-cc` reads every block with `ZIO_FLAG_SCRUB`, so every DVA copy is
  read and a bad one is counted in `vs_checksum_errors` — but one good copy keeps `io_error` 0, so zdb prints no
  "Got error", no "Error counts", exits 0 and says "No leaks"; a normal read on a one-vdev pool always takes DVA[0]
  (`vdev_mirror.c:189-191`, `:459-473`) and a read-write import re-COWs the MOS root before C's scrub. The table is the
  only place a writer's bad DVA[1..2] (D6) shows. A4 the same at `-t T−1` and `-t T−2` (works on
  an exported pool: `-t` sets extreme rewind, which re-reads the label without the `txg ≤` filter, `spa.c:6313-6314`,
  `vdev_label.c:1647-1651`); A5 `zdb -P -dd POOL/DS` for every
  dataset: object count == the header's "N objects" (the ASSERT zdb compiles out), every plain-file / directory /
  symlink object reachable from the manifest or on `DELETE_QUEUE`; `-ddddd` of agnos's objects: no "fill … does not
  match", no type/level ERROR; **(B0 review fix)** `ds_referenced` (the header, exact under `-P`) == Σ dsize + the objset
  block's asize; "possibly leaked" objects only those the k.15 list names, by object id (`zdb -O`); A6 the MOS:
  `$MOS dd_used` − (Σ MOS dsize + the rootbp's asize) == the pristine residual.
- **B (read-only import, no `-f`)**: `zpool import -o readonly=on -R /mnt/m`; manifest diff against the expected
  manifest (`scripts/smoke/lib/zfs-expect.py`: fixture manifest + op list; deterministic content generator ported to
  Cyrius and Python); xattr listing == the fixture's adjusted by the op list (B0 review); strict dirsize for agnos
  dirs (zero directories checked is a FAIL); `zpool status -x` healthy with every READ/WRITE/CKSUM counter 0;
  accounting capture
  (`zfs get -Hp used,referenced,usedby*,logicalreferenced,written -r`, `zfs userspace|groupspace|projectspace -Hp`) with
  identities checked: `used = Σ usedby*`, parent `usedbychildren = Σ children used`, uid-0 `objused` = manifest count,
  `Δ zpool allocated == Δ root used` vs the pristine baseline (**B4 fix**: minus the unused reservations — § e.1's row);
  **(B0 review)** `referenced == usedbydataset` on every
  non-clone head, the root's hidden children == `$MOS` + `freeing` + `leaked` + the pristine residual; **(B4 review)**
  `usedbyrefreservation == max(0, refreservation − written)` on every head with a numeric `written` (identity 6:
  OpenZFS derives `written` from the previous snapshot's referenced and the deadlist, never from `ds_unique_bytes`, so
  REFRSRV is held to OpenZFS's own unique bytes); zero datasets checked is a FAIL. **(review fix)** *allocated* must NOT come from this
  read-only import: a kernel read-only open does not even open the metaslab space maps (`metaslab.c:2918-2929`,
  `spa_read_spacemaps` is set only by zdb, `spa.c:231`, `:1778`, `zdb.c:9749`) and skips the log space maps
  (`spa_log_spacemap.c:1141-1142`) — the fixture's own read-only oracle prints `allocated 0` for a pool `zfs list` puts
  at 19.3M (`main.summary` 33 vs 351), so the identity as written is red on a correct implementation. Take *allocated* from zdb (`zdb -bb` "Normal class:" + the unflushed total zdb adds, `zdb.c:7372-7380` — i.e.
  pass iff A3 printed "No leaks"), or from `zpool get allocated` on a *writable* import of a copy that is exported again
  immediately (export flushes every log, `spa.c:2060-2094` — by default: `zfs_keep_log_spacemaps_at_export = 1` skips it,
  `:2073`).
  **(B6)** Two more steps, for every object the op list CREATED (`<lane>.stat`, from the op list and the expected
  manifest): **B-stat** — OpenZFS's `stat(1)` mode, uid 0, the parent's gid, links (a directory 2 + its subdirectories)
  and size (a directory 2 + its entries, a symlink its target's length); **B-acl** — its `getfacl` == that of a
  FreeBSD-made twin of the same type and mode in the same dataset (k.56: the only reader of the ACL on the path).
- **C (read-write round trip on a copy)**: import without `-f`; `zpool scrub -w` → "repaired 0B … 0 errors" and every
  `zpool status` error counter 0 (a copy self-healed by any read since the import counts there); OpenZFS
  appends to / overwrites / `rm`s agnos files (frees agnos's DVAs through OpenZFS's own space maps — a missing agnos
  ALLOC panics here), `mkdir` + a few MiB copy, rename an agnos dir, `rmdir` an agnos dir, a ≥ 50-char name in an agnos
  microzap, a link + unlink of the new name (k.15 made by OpenZFS: `zdb -ddddd` must flag that object and nothing
  unexplained — C-k15), `zfs snapshot` + `destroy` — every op journalled; `zpool sync`, export, **A3 again**; read-only
  import → `<lane>.rt.manifest`, which must equal expected + the journal (B0 review: it was only counted).
- **D (destructive accounting, second copy)**: destroy every dataset but the root **one at a time** (clones first, then
  the deepest), each followed by `zpool wait -t free` with `freeing`/`leaked` unchanged — a dataset's `dd_used` error
  lands in `$FREE` and must not cancel against another's (B0 review); then OpenZFS empties the root (`rm -rf`, unmount,
  sync, wait free): `referenced == usedbydataset`, the uid-0 object count equals a fresh empty dataset's; final
  **`zpool get -Hp freeing,leaked` both 0**; final `zdb -bcc` with the `-s` table, the `ds_referenced` identity on what
  is left. ⚠ `leaked` is structurally 0: `zfs_free_leak_on_eio` defaults to `B_FALSE` (`spa_misc.c:302`), so a residual
  stays in `$FREE` (`dsl_scan.c:3905-3931`) — `freeing` is the signal.
- **(B9) B-snap / B-snapacct / D-scrub.** B-snap: every snapshot's OWN tree, mounted read-only (`snap_manifests`, the
  same function that wrote the fixture's RECIPE 7 baseline `<image>.snaps`), == the pristine one — a block freed
  instead of deadlisted reads back wrong once reused. B-snapacct: OpenZFS's own `dsl_dataset_space_wouldfree` over the
  deadlists agnos appended to (`zfs destroy -nvpd`: -d skips the dependent-clone refusal in a dry run) == what agnos
  keeps — every snapshotted filesystem's `usedbysnapshots` == the reclaim of all its snapshots, every snapshot's `used`
  == its own reclaim. D-scrub: `zpool scrub -w` after D-destroy (every snapshot destroyed, one at a time: OpenZFS
  processed the deadlists). B-acct's clone identity: a clone with no snapshot of its own holds `usedbydataset ==
  written` (OpenZFS derives `written` from its deadlist; an origin block's kill leaves HEAD alone while referenced
  drops) — "referenced − usedbydataset unchanged" stays for a clone that has snapshots. acct_capture gains
  `snaps <fs> <n>` (RECIPE 7). **(B9 review, k.106)** That last rule was FALSE — a clone with snapshots of its own that
  kills an origin block loses referenced and keeps HEAD (RECIPE 8 edgec: 204,800 → 167,936, the 36,864 B of origin
  blocks the op list killed). EVERY clone is now held to `usedbydataset == written@<origin>` (B-acct adds `wo` lines:
  OpenZFS derives it from the deadlists back to the origin, `dsl_dataset_space_written_impl`).
- **(B10) A3y** (REQUIRED): the LOG SET as OpenZFS's own zdb lists it — A3's `-m` ("Log Spacemap object O txg T",
  each metaslab's "unflushed txg=U") — exactly the host replay's count (`<lane>.logs`, which `zfs-fixture.sh --verify`
  writes from `zfs-wms-check.py --replay`), none older than the least U ≠ 0, every U ≠ 0 naming one, the newest the
  lane's own txg (every agnos commit writes log T). A3 already ran `-y` and `-m`, A3m `-mmmm` (INCONSISTENT); C's
  export → A3 again is the "export → zdb clean" the § h row asks.
- **E (host)**: agnos's `ZFS_SELFTEST` walk on `<lane>.rt.img` == `<lane>.rt.manifest` (`zfs-manifest-diff.py`).
- Verdicts: 0 PASS / 1 FAIL / 2 VOID; `panic:` in the guest serial log is **FAIL** (today a missing results tar is
  infrastructure, `zfs-fixture.sh:328-330` — wrong for this mode); zdb killed by a signal is FAIL. **(B0 review)** The
  guest's `verify.steps` file is authoritative once the results tar arrives (a console line can be lost or split by
  syslog sharing the console — a lost FAIL line read as PASS) and the serial lines must be a subset of it; a lane
  PASSes only with DONE PASS, no FAIL and every required step present exactly once. Judged per lane: a wrong-size
  image, an op list `zfs-expect.py` calls inconsistent, or no verifying uberblock FAILs that lane without a boot; a
  writer lane (`.ops`/`.expect`) without `<lane>.txg` is VOID (the default expected txg was read off the image itself,
  so a lost final uberblock passed A1/A2/H); a lane without either expects the pristine best; H gets `--final-txg`.

### i.4 Proving the oracle (once, at B12; ≈ 11 × 5 min)

Debug knobs that break one rule each, and the phase that must go red: M1 skip an ALLOC record → A3 abort / C panic; M2
skip a FREE → A3 "leaked"; M3 dir SIZE off by one → C `rmdir` / B dirsize; M4 zero a ZIL header → A3 leaked; M5
`ds_referenced` + 4096 → **A5** (`referenced` ≠ Σ dsize + objset block) **and B-acct** (`referenced` ≠ `usedbydataset`)
— (B0 review) NOT D: the async destroy charges `$FREE` from `dd_used_bytes` only; M6 corrupt one checksum → A3 rc 3 /
C scrub when every copy is bad; a bad copy of a multi-copy block (D6) → A3's `-s` vdev cksum column only (zdb exits 0,
"No leaks") and C scrub "repaired"; M7 bad ub eck → A2; M8 reuse a same-txg free → A4; M9 rewrite a label nvlist → h1;
M10 `obj-` count off → B; M11 cut + in-place overwrite → A3 on the rolled-back state. **(B3b, run — scratch mutants of
`zfs_write.cyr` applied with cyim, the `ZFS_WRITE_OPS` kernel built, the source restored byte-identical; each mutant
also silences the in-kernel read-back, which catches all four first; `ZWS_LANES` one lane → the guest):**
N1 = M1, the first ALLOC of the first null txg not logged → **A3 red** (zdb SIGABRT: "DOUBLE FREE … LOG_SM" — the next
txg's FREE of that block — and A4 at both rewinds, the second through `zio_claim`'s VERIFY0); N2 = M2, the first FREE
not logged → **A3 red** ("leaked space: vdev 0, offset 0x229600, size 5120", and A4); N4, log T's `smp_alloc` + 512 →
**A3m red** only ("space_map_object alloc (496640) INCONSISTENT with space map summary (496128)" — plain `-m` cannot
see it, as § i.3 said); N3, `U[i]` left 0 on disk for the metaslabs that received entries (`wr@u0`) → **every guest
step PASSED** (30/30, a writable import, scrub, round trip and destroy included): the oracle had a blind spot. Closed
by h6 (§ i.2): the same image now fails the verify in **H** ("log txg 78 holds an entry for ms 1, whose unflushed txg
is 0"), and the write smoke's per-lane `space` check fails it before the guest. **(B3b review) The driver.** That
per-lane `space` check fails a lane BEFORE its image reaches the guest, and a failed lane's image is not kept, so
`ZWS_LANES` alone stopped showing the guest phase of N1, N3 and N4. `ZWS_SPACE=advisory` (proof-only; the verdict is
PARTIAL, never a PASS) prints the red `space` and sends the image on. Re-run with it (`ZWS_SPACE=advisory
ZWS_LANES=<lane>`, the source restored byte-identical): **N1** — `space` red ("ms 1: an unflushed entry contradicts
the space map"), guest **H, A3, A4** red (A3's "DOUBLE FREE … LOG_SM" abort, A4's at both rewinds); **N2** — `space`
**PASS** (a missing FREE leaves every log self-consistent: only zdb's traversal sees the leaked block), guest **A3,
A4** red ("leaked space: vdev 0, offset 0x229600, size 5120"); **N3** — `space` red, guest **H only** (30 steps, every
other PASS); **N4** — `space` red ("log txg 25: smp_alloc 496640 != net 496128"), guest **H, A3m** red (A3m's
"INCONSISTENT" — still the only zdb phase that sees it). So h6 now catches N1, N3 and N4 before the guest, and N2
still reaches it by default. The B0 gate already forges M5
and M6 (both forms), a misattributed block (D), a root HEAD/CHILD shift (B-acct) and a lost final uberblock (H + A2)
with `scripts/tool/zfs-damage.py`.

#### i.4 (B12) the mutation table

**(B12) The proof, run once — every negative of the write cut in one table** (2026-10-13). Collected from the B0 gate
(forged copies, `zfs-damage.py`), every bite's § h gate / review / fix record and § k.103 / k.127 / k.129, then § i.4's
planned M1–M11 mapped onto it. A planned M was RUN NOW when it had never run, or when the code its mutant changes had
changed since its run (checked function by function against the current source): **M1, M2** (`zfs_wc_log` copies the
extent list since B4, and B10's hygiene flush now rewrites the space maps the logs feed), **M3** (`zfs_wc1`: 54 lines
changed since B6 — B8's fat ZAP, B11's UTF-8 names), **M4** (never run), **M5** (`zfs_wd_dsdelta`: B9's snapshot side),
**M6** in the KERNEL (only forged at B0), **M7** (never run), **M8** (`zfs_wdef_rel`: the merge's ghost release, k.133 (a)),
**M9** (never run), **M11** (never run). **M10** stands on B6's N4 (`zfs_wd_uacct` byte-identical since). How: a scratch
copy of the tree (`zfs_write.cyr` sha256 `4a94fc55…`, the fixture `r8-4ee74703473a-c579a04a`), each mutant applied with
cyim, the `ZFS_WRITE_OPS` kernel built, the source restored — sha256 identical — BEFORE the lane; one lane,
`ZWS_SPACE=advisory ZWS_CONTINUE=1`; every mutant also silences `zfs_wt_rb` (the in-kernel read-back, which would catch
most of them first). Phases: **kernel** = the kernel's own line or backstop; host = the smoke's checks (`space` h6,
`uacct` h7, `holes` h8, `fatzap` h9, `sa`, `room`, the walk and F lines, audit h1–h5); **H** = the guest's run of the host
audit; A1–A6 / A3m / A3y / B-* / C-* / D-* = § i.3. **Bold** = the phase the rule's own oracle is (the first that goes
red for the reason the mutant gives); the rest follow from it. (forged) = a forged copy of a pristine image, no kernel.
Scratch evidence: `b0/g-*`, the bites' scratch dirs; this run's `b12/runs/<m>.smoke.log`, `<m>-logs/`, `<m>-verify/`.

**Still unproven** (planned, not shown red by any run): **(1) M4's kernel form** — the mutant ran on main and wr and was never reached: the fixtures' only non-empty ZIL headers (one allocated, never-written block, class 3) belong to `agnostank/legacy` (`mountpoint=legacy`, which agnos grafts nowhere) and `agnoswr/vol` (a zvol), and no op list writes either. Closing it on disk needs
a fixture RECIPE change (a ZIL left on a dataset the op list writes: an `fsync` in, e.g., `agnostank/off` before the
export), which re-keys the fixture; the forged M4 proves the oracle's half. **(2) M8's literal same-txg form** — a
free in T is killed only after its successor was allocated (build, then kill: `zfs_wd_final`), and inside a txg the
allocator's cursor only moves forward, so a unit freed and reallocated inside ONE txg needs a cursor knob inside the
txg (B11's `zfs_wt_msonly` acts across txgs) and the release walk's own refusal disabled (a unit released that was
never deferred fails the pool, `zfs_wdef_rel`); the one-txg-early form reaches the same oracle phase (A4 at the rewind
that names the block) and is proven twice. Recorded as not proven by a mutant in the bites' own records, unchanged
here (none is a § i.4 M): B5's lock release, pre-scan refusal and quotas; B6 review's objset-growth refusal,
`*_COMPLETE` gates and `large_microzap` pre-check; B11 review's (b), (c), (e), (f), (g), (i) (k.132).
**What B12 found**: no oracle gap — every run mutant went red in the phase the plan named (M3's `rmdir` in D-root, M5
also in D's own identity). Two orderings changed since the plan was written, both for the better: since B10 the
KERNEL is the first to see a log record missing (M1 at its next reload, M2 at its next flush — the read-back silenced,
these backstops still fire), and `zfs_wr`'s ring / own-extent backstop refuses M9's and M11's writes before a byte
lands, so those two oracle proofs had to remove it. The table's host column also shows what the cut lane cannot do
on the host: it has no walk, so M6a / M6b / M8 / M11 are green on the host and red only in the guest.

## (j) Operator decisions

> **RULED 2026-10-10 (operator):** D1 **(b)** — durable at close/sync; D2 **(a)** — snapshot deadlists (B9);
> D7 **(a)** — exported pools only; D12 **full scope, and MAKE ROOM if a build would cross the wall** (not the cut
> order): first find why the `CYRIUS_DCE=1` kernel dies before its banner (162,672 B back), failing that move the BSP
> stacks out of region 1. Every other decision takes its recommendation (D3 a, D4 a, D5 b, D6 a, D8 a, D9 a, D10 a,
> D11 a, D13 a, D14 a, D15 a). The § g cut order is therefore NOT pre-agreed — a bite that would cross the wall stops
> and reports.
>
> **SUPERSEDED IN PART 2026-10-11 (operator standing rule, grow before refactor — recorded by B8 and the ROOM step,
> 2026-10-12):** D12's "a bite that would cross the wall stops and reports" no longer stands. When a build nears the
> wall the image GROWS its room — bulk is MOVED out of the image (never trim, `#ifdef`-out or dead-code-audit code;
> never stop to ask about room) — and the bites go on: a build that crosses the wall is reported with its number and the
> work goes on, and the rewrite comes later. § g's re-weighs stay as REPORTS of the margin, not as stop conditions. The
> 1.57.12 region-1 move of the BSP stacks was the first step; the ROOM step (2026-10-12, § g) is the first move of bulk:
> +285,040 to +285,056 B per build (after its second review).

Each changes the work; the recommendation is **bold**.

| # | Question | Options | Recommendation and why |
|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | Txg batching | (a) one txg per mutating syscall; (b) (a) + per-fd `write()` coalescing, commit on close/sync/exit/power/threshold/age and before any other ZFS entry; (c) OpenZFS open txg + sync thread + reader overlay | **(b)**, built as (a) first (B5) and switched in B11. Per-`write()` txgs cost ≈ 0.65 MiB of metadata and one log space map each; (b) is one txg per file with the same atomicity. (c) needs a kthread stack region 7 does not have and an overlay in every reader path. Cost of (b): `write()` is durable at close/sync, not at return (POSIX-legal; differs from ext2's write-through) |
| D2 | Datasets with snapshots | (a) deadlist insert + bpobj materialisation (B9); (b) refuse by name | **(a)**. ≈ 200 lines on top of the bpobj append B7 needs anyway for gang/dedup deferral. (b) makes the fixture root `agnostank` (`@snap1`) and nearly every real root (boot environments) read-only. Lazy-freeing snapshot-owned blocks to `free_bpobj` is **not** an option — it frees blocks a snapshot still uses |
| D3 | User/group/project accounting | (a) exact deltas; (b) refuse datasets with `userobj_accounting`/`project_quota` active; (c) clear `os_flags` COMPLETE and let OpenZFS rescan; (d) leave accounted bits clear on new dnodes, subtract only on modify/free | **(a)**. (b) refuses every fixture dataset (refcount 22 of 22). (c) repairs nothing (the upgrade re-dirties live dnodes only, WPA § 8). (d) still needs the ZAP writes and under-reports new files until OpenZFS touches them. ≈ 120 lines |
| D4 | Compression on write | (a) data OFF, metadata ZLE, zero blocks → holes when the dataset compresses; (b) + an lz4 compressor for data on `lz4`/`on` datasets (≈ 250 lines, ≈ 10 KB); (c) everything OFF | **(a)**; (b) with zstd as the follow-on. ZLE is legacy (no feature), already decodable everywhere, ≈ 40 lines, and turns the five 128 KiB indirect levels of a 6-level ZPL meta-dnode into ≈ 4 KiB each per txg. (c) writes ≈ 1.25 MiB of meta-dnode indirects per dataset per txg (5 × 128 KiB × 2 copies). Cost of (a): agnos-written file data is stored uncompressed on `compression=lz4` datasets |
| D5 | Checksum on write | (a) fletcher4 everywhere (makefs); (b) OpenZFS's choice (`dmu_write_policy`: data = property, metadata = property if metadata-capable else fletcher4) when agnos implements it and it needs no activation, else fletcher4 | **(b)** — same dispatch code (fletcher4, sha256, sha512 exist in the codec), keeps `checksum=sha256` datasets' integrity choice; sha512 only where already active; skein/edonr/blake3 datasets are not writable anyway |
| D6 | Copies | (a) OpenZFS: data `copies`, dataset metadata `copies+1` (`redundant_metadata=all`), MOS 3; (b) one DVA everywhere (legal, makefs) | **(a)** — matches what the pool owner chose and what the fixture shows (`triple`/`double`); costs ≈ 3× MOS write volume per txg, which D1 (b) amortises |
| D7 | Pool ownership | (a) EXPORTED only, labels and MOS config untouched; (b) (a) + an explicit operator force knob (build flag) for ACTIVE pools, still labels untouched; (c) full protocol: ACTIVE + agnos hostid at rw mount, EXPORTED at clean unmount (XDR re-encoder, two-phase labels, MOS config rewrite) | **(a)** now. (b) only if dual-booting a FreeBSD root pool is a 1.57.12 goal — it carries the "owner suspended with the pool imported" pool-destroying risk. (c) follow-on |
| D8 | Mirror pools | (a) refuse; (b) write every child (needs multi-device vdev assembly across backend tags, DTL/resilver gates) | **(a)** — agnos's block layer exposes one device per backend tag; writing one side is split-brain |
| D9 | Verb scope | (a) all of § a.1 incl. link + short symlink; (b) drop link/symlink; (c) (a) + an `ftruncate` syscall (new ABI number) | **(a)** — link/symlink are ≈ 60 lines on top of create/unlink; `ftruncate` is glue over `zfs_trunc` once an ABI number is ruled |
| D10 | Log space maps accumulating | (a) B10 flush/destroy (OpenZFS's own bound); (b) cap agnos txgs between OpenZFS imports (e.g. 1,000 → then read-only, named); (c) unbounded | **(a)** — ≈ 300 lines; without it every txg leaves ≥ one ashift block × 3 copies + a MOS slot + a ZAP entry until OpenZFS imports, and the log ZAP outgrows its microzap at 2,048 txgs. (b) is the fallback if the budget bites |
| D11 | Open fd on an unlinked file | (a) GEN check, −1 on mismatch; (b) refuse unlink/rename-over while any fd names the object (EBUSY); (c) POSIX: `DELETE_QUEUE` + free on last close | **(a)** now (cheap, safe); (c) when a cross-fork refcount exists |
| D12 | Budget | (a) full scope B0–B12 ≈ 193 KB (± 25 %); (b) cap at ≈ 150 KB by deferring B9, B10 and fat-ZAP splits; (c) move the BSP stacks first to raise the wall | **(a)** with a re-weigh after every bite and the pre-agreed cut order (§ g) if a bite runs > 15 % over. (c) is its own piece of work (CLAUDE.md: the next wall move needs a new home for the BSP stacks) — only if (a) measures over |
| D13 | Reservations / quotas | (a) implement `parent_delta` + EDQUOT checks (≈ 60 lines); (b) refuse datasets with them | **(a)** — a few lines, and refusing hits every dataset under a reserved parent |
| D14 | Large dnodes (`dnodesize=auto`) | (a) always one slot; (b) honour the property where `large_dnode` is active | **(a)** — legal everywhere; (b) only buys longer in-bonus symlinks/ACLs |
| D15 | `block_cloning` / dedup active | (a) defer every kill to `free_bpobj` (OpenZFS's own path); (b) refuse the pool | **(a)** — no BRT/DDT code; cost: freed space returns after OpenZFS imports. Today both are only *enabled* on the fixtures |

## (k) Open technical questions

1. **Is a ub-only commit (rootbp born before `ub_txg`) accepted by every OpenZFS path?** No check was found **ANSWERED (B0, 2026-10-10): yes** — a hand-forged ub-only commit (`main@ubonly`, txg 99 over 98's rootbp) passed zdb, read-only + read-write import, scrub, OpenZFS writes on top and destroy.
   (`uberblock_update`'s return only gates OpenZFS's *own* write, `uberblock.c:74`; `spa.c:10711` is a sync-pass test).
   B2 is the experiment; if any oracle phase objects, B2 folds into B3. **(review note)** Evidence it is accepted:
   OpenZFS itself writes such a ub — `vdev_config_sync` proceeds when `uberblock_update` reports "unchanged" as long
   as the config is dirty or multihost is on (`vdev_label.c:2069-2077`), and the load path has no rootbp-birth test
   (`spa_ld_select_uberblock`, `spa.c:4720-4880`: magic/txg/version/label/features only). Still run B2.
2. **Fixture SA facts** to read with `zdb -dddd` before B6: REGISTRY numbers (expected DACL_COUNT 16, SYMLINK 17,
   DACL_ACES 19, DXATTR 20, PROJID 21); LAYOUTS contents (expected `[5,6,4,12,13,7,11,0,1,2,3,8,21,16,19]` and the
   symlink form); whether the root directory carries `ZFS_PROJID`; which objects have `SPILL_BLKPTR` (`xattr.txt`,
   `sym-long`).
   **ANSWERED (B6, measured on the r4 fixture images — a host dump through `zfs-damage.py`'s parser, every ZPL
   dataset of ashift9 / wr / main / full / logs):** REGISTRY (a microzap in every dataset) is exactly the expected
   table — 0 ATIME 16 B, 1 MTIME 16, 2 CTIME 16, 3 CRTIME 16, 4 GEN, 5 MODE, 6 SIZE, 7 PARENT, 8 LINKS, 9 XATTR, 10 RDEV,
   11 FLAGS, 12 UID, 13 GID, 14 PAD 32, 15 ZNODE_ACL 88, **16 DACL_COUNT, 17 SYMLINK (variable), 18 SCANSTAMP 32,
   19 DACL_ACES (variable), 20 DXATTR (variable), 21 PROJID**. LAYOUTS (a FAT ZAP, 16 KiB, one leaf): in the pool ROOT
   datasets (agnos9, agnoswr, agnostank, agnosfull, agnoslogs) **2** = `[5,6,4,12,13,7,11,0,1,2,3,8,16,19]` — no
   PROJID: the root directory, made before the objset had project accounting — **3** = the same with **21** before 16
   (every file and directory FreeBSD made later), **4** = layout 2 + **17** (the symlink form — only agnos9, agnoswr and
   agnostank, which hold FreeBSD symlinks), **5** = `[17]` (a long symlink's spill: agnoswr, agnostank), and on
   agnostank **6** = layout 3 + 20, **7** = `[20]` (DXATTR in the bonus / its spill). Every CHILD dataset holds ONE
   layout, **2** = `[5,6,4,12,13,7,11,0,1,2,3,8,21,16,19]` (born after `project_quota`): **no symlink layout** — a
   symlink there needs a LAYOUTS add (a fat-ZAP add, B8), so B6 refuses it by name, as it refuses one on agnosfull and
   agnoslogs. **(B6 review, fixture RECIPE 5)** except `agnostank/custom`: one FreeBSD symlink (`custom-link`) gives it
   the symlink layout (with the intermediate `zfs_mknode` one before `sa_update` adds SYMLINK), so the ring-3 gate's
   `symlink`#63 succeeds there — the only writable dataset zfsx reaches. The root directories carry **no** `ZFS_PROJID` (flags `0x40800000144`); every later file / directory
   does (`0x840800000004` / `0x840800000144`), a symlink never (`0x40800000104`: VLNK gets no PROJID, `zfs_mknode`).
   The ACLs are the three-ACE trivial form: 0644 → owner@ (flags `0x1000`) `0x1e019f`, group@ (`0x2040`) and everyone@
   (`0x4000`) `0x120089`; 0755 → `0x1e01bf` / `0x1200a9` / `0x1200a9`; DACL_COUNT 3, 24 bytes. Modes: files `0100644`,
   directories `040755`, symlinks **`0120755`** (FreeBSD's `ACCESSPERMS & ~umask` 022 — not Linux's 0777). Dnodes:
   indirect shift 17, one level, one blkptr, bonus SA, checksum / compress 0, `dn_flags` **`0xa`** for an object that
   never had a block (USED_BYTES appears with `dnode_diduse_space`), `0xb` otherwise; a symlink's bonuslen is the last
   attribute's end, unrounded (`hello.txt`: 176 + 9 = **185**); a new directory's microzap is 512 B (`dblk` 512 — 1536
   once grown), its salt odd. `SPILL_BLKPTR`: `agnostank/xattr.txt`, `agnostank/sym-long`, `agnoswr/sym-long` (none in
   an object B6 creates or modifies: B6 builds bonus-only SAs, k.42).
3. **MOS shape on the fixtures**: MOS meta-dnode `nlevels` (174 objects → likely 2, so one 128 KiB L1 on the chain),
   whether `features_for_write` and the log ZAP are micro or fat, the number of logs alive after export. Decides the
   per-txg chain size (≈ 150 KiB × 3 uncompressed) and whether reserved-size ZLE is worth it.
   **ANSWERED (B3b, measured on ashift9 / wr / main / full):** the MOS meta-dnode has **2 levels** — 16 KiB dnode
   blocks (32 dnodes) under ONE 128 KiB L1 — with 47 / 134 / 175 / 49 objects (dn_maxblkid 12 / 12 / 16 / 14); the MOS
   objset_phys is 4 KiB; every MOS block is lz4 with 3 DVAs. `features_for_write` is a 1,536-byte **microzap** (object
   52, refcount `spacemap_histogram` 6 / 7 / 9 / 8); the log ZAP a 512-byte **microzap** holding **one** entry after the
   export (an embedded BP on disk; object 129, 133 on `full`); **one log** alive (txg T0); U[] is a 128 KiB
   `DMU_OTN_UINT64_METADATA` block (object 132 / 136) in which every metaslab with a space map reads `U = T0` — ~~the
   export flushed them all~~ **(B3b review)** because these pools had `log_spacemap` active from their first txg, so
   every metaslab got its U at its first log entry, and the export flushed exactly the metaslabs that have one (it
   flushes no `U = 0` metaslab: § c.3's correction). So a null txg never changes U[] on THESE pools; on an upgraded one
   (`wr@u0`) it does, exported or not. `$MOS`'s
   dsl_dir (35), the root's (32) and `features_for_write` share dnode block 1; the log ZAP and U[] share block 4; the
   first free MOS object is 2 (block 0 holds only object 1). A null txg is therefore **8 blocks**: non-chain
   `features_for_write`'s block (ZLE), the log ZAP's (512 B, OFF: ZLE cannot beat one allocation unit), dnode block 4
   (ZLE); chain (OFF) log T's block (1 / 4 KiB — B3b review: this said 2 KiB at ashift 9), dnode blocks 0 and 1 (16 KiB each), the L1 (128 KiB) and the objset
   (4 KiB) — **514,560 B written at ashift 9, 552,960 B at 12** (3 copies; the L1 alone is 384 KiB of it), 42–48
   extents, log T 232–392 bytes (12–21 entries). `$MOS` grows **once** by 496,128 / 479,232 B (the uncompressed chain
   replaces lz4 blocks), then by 3,072 / 12,288 B per txg (log T's own block, net). Reserved-size ZLE: k.25.
4. **Largest flag build's headroom** under gate 34 — measure before B1; the writer + its selftest must fit every flag **ANSWERED:** TEST+KTEST is binding (203,224 B before B1, 161,896 B after) — § g. **(B2 review: wrong — `RING3_SELFTEST` is, 127,960 B after B2; § g.)**
   build, not just the plain image.
5. **Does FreeBSD's base `zdb` compile `ASSERT`s out?** Assumed yes (NDEBUG); the harness therefore re-implements the **ANSWERED (B0): NO** — FreeBSD 15.1's zdb runs with ASSERTs live: a bad `smp_alloc` aborts in `metaslab_verify_space()` and a dropped log in `spa_ld_log_sm_metadata()` before "INCONSISTENT"/ENOENT can print; the oracle scores a signal-killed zdb as FAIL and quotes the assertion.
   object-count == root-fill and fill checks. Confirm once (a deliberately bad L0 dnode-block fill: does zdb abort?).
6. **`Δ zpool allocated == Δ root used`**: validate the identity across a pristine OpenZFS-only txg first (ZIL blocks **ANSWERED (B0): holds** exactly on every OpenZFS-only round trip (3,184,128 / 585,728 / 3,280,896 / 3,264,512 B).
   are allocated but in no dataset; `$FREE` contents are both) before it gates agnos. (review) Same for the `$MOS` line
   vs Σ MOS-object dsize; and never read *allocated* from a read-only import (§ i.3 B).
7. **ZLE vs OpenZFS's own compressed-size floor** (`zio_get_compression_max_size`): any psize < lsize is believed legal
   for reading; confirm with the oracle on blocks that compress by < 12.5 %.
   **ANSWERED (B3a, `zfs_wcomp` in `core/zfs_wenc.cyr`; recorded at the B3b review):** agnos applies OpenZFS's own
   floor — `d_len = lsize − lsize/8`, which for ZLE skips the gcd rounding (`zio_get_compression_max_size`,
   `zio.c:1783-1800`), then `zio_roundup_alloc_size` and compress OFF when that reaches lsize — so it never writes a
   ZLE block OpenZFS would not have written; gate 37 pins the floor's exact edge (output == / == + 1 of
   s_len − s_len/8) and a real OpenZFS ZLE block's psize and bytes (B3a, B3a review).
8. **Commit hold time with IF=0** on iron (archaemenid NVMe flush latency) — measure at B3; if a flush is tens of ms,
   the 4 MiB / 2 MiB thresholds come down.
   **(B2, measured on QEMU/KVM — B2 review)** One uberblock-only commit (flush, four slot writes, flush) holds
   `fs_lock` with IF=0 for **0.2–0.8 s**, against the 1–10 ms per flush § b.3 assumed: `zfs-wtest: begin` → `commit
   txg` was 431 / 414 / 198 ms (ashift9 / -torn / -grown, NVMe), 425 ms (AHCI), 418 ms (usb-storage), 781 / 779 ms
   (wr / main, 256 MiB images) in the post-review smoke; the review's own run read 419 / 233 / 490 ms (NVMe) and
   237 ms (AHCI); the read-back of four 128 KiB rings adds 200–300 ms more. ⚠ The QEMU flush is a host `fdatasync` of
   the raw image, which the smoke has JUST written (a 96–256 MiB `dd` into the lane disk), so the first flush pays for
   the host's dirty page cache — the larger images take longer and the numbers are an upper bound for QEMU, not a
   device measurement. B3 must not size its thresholds from them; measure on iron before B11 wires commits into
   close / sync.
   **(B3b, measured — zfs-write-smoke, the kernel's own timestamps from `begin` to `commit txg`: the plan, ≈ 0.5 MiB of
   writes, two flushes, four slot writes, under `fs_lock` with IF=0; QEMU/KVM, the flush a host `fdatasync`)** ub-only
   / the three null txgs: ashift9 NVMe 436 / 686 / 694 / 714 ms (-torn 452 / 699 / 697 / 708, -grown 445 / 690 /
   680 / 691), AHCI 455 / 853 / 857 / 842, usb-storage 442 / 856 / 862 / 849; wr 788 / 649 / 639 / 632; wr@u0 796 /
   709 / 699 / 697 (two rounds in the first); main 1,835 / 2,276 / 2,855 / 2,412 (the 256 MiB image the host had just
   written: its `fdatasync`); full (one bitmap slot, 32–38 metaslab loads per txg) 737 / 637 / 628 / 727 / 697 / 679 /
   692 / 713 / 722. A null txg holds ≈ 0.2–0.4 s longer than an uberblock-only commit on NVMe (the per-sector AHCI /
   USB paths ≈ 0.4 s): the write volume, not the plan. The in-kernel read-back after each commit (≈ 0.2 s) is outside
   the hold. Still an upper bound for QEMU; iron remains open.
   **(B3b review, re-measured — the review's run and this round's gate run, the kernel's own timestamps)** commit hold,
   ub-only / null txgs, ms — review: ashift9 468 / 730 / 738 / 707, AHCI 433 / 832 / 833 / 857, usb-storage 423 / 804 /
   798 / 813, wr 800 / 729 / 705 / 704, wr@u0 781 / 719 / 713 / 706, main 780 / 709 / 737 / 723, full 719 then 710–745;
   this round: ashift9 414 / 603 / 604 / 605, AHCI 416 / 727 / 750 / 748, usb-storage 438 / 871 / 827 / 826, wr 858 /
   716 / 699 / 710, wr@u0 220 / 700 / 698 / 717, main 792 / 724 / 695 / 709, full 729 then 691–712, full@pre 811 then
   651–734. B3b's main figures (1,835 / 2,276 / 2,855 / 2,412) did not reproduce in either run — host `fdatasync`
   variance on the 256 MiB image, not the writer. So **a null txg holds `fs_lock` with preemption off for 0.6–0.9 s**,
   the in-kernel read-back adds 0.22–0.34 s per null txg, and the op list — which holds `fs_lock` across all of it,
   IF=0 at its boot slot — **3.9–5.1 s** on the three-null-txg lanes and **11.0–11.3 s** on `full` / `full@pre`: 20–100×
   § b.3's 5–35 ms. Still QEMU upper bounds; **k.8 stays OPEN for iron**, and the chain's volume and its ENOSPC exposure
   (k.25) belong in the same ruling.
   **(B5 review, re-measured — the op list's own output sat inside every figure above)** The plan line the op list
   prints from INSIDE the commit (`zfs_wt_stats`, ≈ 250 characters to serial and the framebuffer console) costs
   ≈ 0.45 s of rendering on QEMU, and every begin → `commit txg` figure above includes it. The commit itself — the op's
   begin (or the txg before) → its plan line's own timestamp, which the kernel takes as the line starts — is **0.22–0.27 s
   a txg on NVMe** for null and small file-data txgs (`ashift9`: 9 txgs 224–266 ms) and up to ≈ 0.5 s for a 2 MiB data
   txg or the 3,553-extent truncation txg (`wr`: 103 txgs 205–507 ms, mean 405); a production build prints no plan line,
   so that is its hold. ⭐ **And the hold is ONE txg, never a request** (B5 review, finding 4): a `write`#1 or truncation
   of several txgs releases `fs_lock` between them (`zfs_wf_yield`) — `wr`'s 20 MiB `write`#1 through an fd is 11 txgs of
   ≤ 0.5 s with the lock free in between, where it was one ≈ 8 s hold (a 1 GiB write ≈ 2.5 min). The calling CPU stays
   IF=0 for the whole call, as in every syscall (k.48). Iron stays open.
   **(B11, measured by the commit itself — k.126)** Every figure above had the op list's own work inside it. `zfs_commit`
   now times itself (`zfs_wc_hold`: its entry → the publish, BEFORE the op list's plan line) and every commit prints it
   (`zfs-wtest: txg T b11: hold=… irqmax=…`): see k.126 for the figures and the interrupt windows B11 built. Iron stays
   open.
9. **OpenZFS import cost with many agnos logs** (before B10): time a writable import after 1,000 agnos txgs; decides
   whether D10 (b)'s cap is needed as a stopgap.
10. **Large unlink bound**: the per-txg log-entry cap that routes a big file to the lazy `DELETE_QUEUE` path (≈ 8 k
    DVAs keeps the log in one 128 KiB block); measure the fragmented worst case on the `gang` and `big1m` fixtures.
    **ANSWERED (B7, as built):** no separate cap — the B5 truncation's own bounds route it: an unlink whose object's
    non-hole BPs (every level, the spill) exceed the kill list (`ZWF_FRMAX`, 4,096 entries) or whose DVAs exceed the
    extent list's room (`zfs_wf_xfit`: 4,096 − 6 × (the txg's dirty blocks + `ZWF_CRSV` 96), ≈ 3,460 for a removal's ~10
    dirty blocks) goes the lazy way; nothing splits a removal across txgs (FreeBSD's `zfs_rmnode` does —
    `dmu_free_long_range` over several, then the dnode — which is the follow-on if the delete queue's drain-at-next-mount
    is not acceptable). A deferred (gang / dedup / clone) kill makes no FREE extent and is not counted against the
    extent list. Probed on `agnoswr/rs4k/r64k.bin` after B5's 20 MiB of 4 KiB records (`zfs_wt_lazyprobe`, in memory —
    the figures are its plan line's); on disk the lazy path runs under the op list's knob (§ f.5's B7 note);
    `gang/gang.bin`'s 8 gang L0s + L1 and every other fixture object free in their txg.
11. **Gang header size on free under `dynamic_gang_header`**: irrelevant while gang kills are deferred; noted in case a
    later bite frees gangs directly (header asize = `psize_to_asize(512)`, `include/sys/vdev.h:145-153`).
12. **Hole-birth fidelity has no oracle yet**: zdb checks type/level of hole BPs, not their birth. Add an incremental
    `zfs send -i` round trip in Phase C on a snapshotted lane once B9 lands.
    **(B5) A host oracle now: h8** (§ i.2, `zfs-hole-check.py`) — every hole of a changed object against the pristine
    image; proven by negative NB (§ h's B5 gate record). OpenZFS's own judgement (`zfs send -i`) still waits for B9.
13. **(review) Multi-LBA writes from the writer area.** The 8 MiB area is physically contiguous
    (`pmm_alloc_2mb_run`), so `blk_write_sectors_direct` with its *phys* address (`nvme_write_sectors` hands `buf`
    straight to the PRP builder, `nvme.cyr:990-998`, `:899-918`) would cut a txg from ≈ 165 commands to ≈ one per
    block. Open: does VT-d translation (on since 1.57.10) require an explicit DMA grant for that range, and is the area
    ever above the identity window the NVMe path assumes? Decides whether § b.3's thresholds can stay at 4 MiB.
14. **(review) `dn_used` has no oracle.** zdb never checks it, and the planned userspace recount takes per-object
    `dsize` from zdb, which *is* `dn_used` — a wrong `dn_used` and the matching wrong user delta agree with each other.
    Recount `dn_used` on the host as Σ asize of the object's BPs from `zdb -ddddd` (all levels + spill) and compare.
    **ANSWERED (B4): the host recount exists** — `scripts/smoke/lib/zfs-uacct-check.py`, the audit's **h7** (§ i.2):
    every dnode's `dn_used` == Σ dsize of every BP of its tree (+ its spill), read from the image itself (not from
    zdb's rows, which print `dn_used`), every head dataset's `ds_referenced` == Σ `dn_used` + its objset block, and the
    user / group / project-used ZAPs == a recount over every dnode — so a wrong `dn_used` and a matching wrong user
    delta no longer agree with each other anywhere. Validated on every pristine fixture image (main: 20 ZPL datasets,
    3,050 objects in the root; wr: 13; all exact), run per rw lane by zfs-write-smoke (`uacct`) and by the verify's H.
15. **(review) Stale `PARENT` after link → unlink-the-new-name.** `link A/f → B/g` sets `PARENT := B` (FreeBSD
    `zfs_link_create` writes PARENT on every link, `zfs_dir.c:640-641`); unlinking `B/g` never rewrites it, so `A/f`
    is live with a `PARENT` whose directory holds no entry for it. zdb `-ddddd` (five d — the check is gated on
    `dump_opt['d'] > 4`, `zdb.c:3790-3798`; WPA § 10 says `-dddd`) prints "path not found, possibly leaked" and counts
    it (`:4448`) on a pool OpenZFS alone would produce. Keep that sequence out of the selftest op list, or make A5
    tolerate exactly those objects (derived from the op list) — otherwise A5 is red on correct behaviour. **(B0, built)**
    `zfs-expect.py --leakok` derives the paths from the op list (PARENT := the directory of every link and rename's new
    name); A5 resolves them to object ids with `zdb -O` and requires flagged ⊆ that set — never a count, so a real
    orphan cannot stand in for an expected one. Phase C makes one such object with OpenZFS itself (C-k15), which proves
    the model on every verify run.
16. **(review) Branch B has its own identity.** A snapshot-owned kill moves its dsize HEAD → SNAP with `dd_used_bytes`
    unchanged (`dsl_dir_transfer_space`, `dsl_dir.c:1592-1613`), so the pool-wide `Δ allocated == Δ root used` cannot
    see a wrong split. Add a B9 identity: `Δ usedbysnapshots` of `agnostank` == Σ dsize of agnos's branch-B kills
    (computable from the op list's overwritten pre-`@snap1` blocks), and `Δ dl_used` of the head deadlist the same.
17. **(B3a → B3b) Metadata into the slop.** `zfs_walloc_bp` checks the slop only for user data (`ZWP_USER`); the
    commit's own metadata may use it, as OpenZFS's syncing context does (`spa_misc.c:354-393`). Read literally, § c.3
    ("refuse any allocation that would take pool free below slop") would refuse the null txg itself on the `full` lane
    (about half of a 128 MiB pool is slop). B3b's `full` lane must show the user write refused and the commit taken.
    **ANSWERED (B3b):** the `full` lane (112 MiB, 56.6 MiB allocated = past its slop) commits nine txgs, every one's
    metadata taken into the slop and OpenZFS judging the pool clean; the user-write refusal arrives with B5's writes
    (there is no user write before it — `zfs_walloc_bp`'s `ZWP_USER` refusal stays proven in memory by B3a).
    **Decided 2026-10-10 (orchestrator, technical; the operator may override): the commit's own METADATA may use the
    slop space, as OpenZFS's syncing context does; only user data is checked against it.** As built at B4: user data is
    checked TWICE — when it is dirtied (`zfs_wd_space`: OpenZFS's open-context predicates, the root's usage against the
    normal class − slop, and agnos's allocation rule, allocated + the txg's net + bytes × copies past the slop) and at
    its allocation (`zfs_walloc_bp`, `ZWP_USER`), the first strictly the stronger, so a write `zfs_wd_dirty` lets through
    cannot fail its commit on space. ⚠ The `full` fixture is NOT past its slop (B3b's answer above says it is): its
    normal class is 117,440,512 B, the slop half of it, 58,720,256 B; the root's usage is 56,651,776 B and the pool's
    allocated bytes 56,676,352 B ("56.6 MB" — decimal), so 2,043,904 B of user data still fit, and the `full` lane's
    dataset COW (one 128 KiB block, copies 1) commits — as OpenZFS's own predicate would let it. A refused user write
    on disk arrives with B5's appends (fill the pool, then one more).
    **(B4 review)** Both checks left out the DEFERRED frees, which OpenZFS's root predicate subtracts
    (`dsl_pool_unreserved_space` = the normal class − slop − `metaslab_class_get_deferred`, `dsl_pool.c:950-957`): agnos
    defers every free for the whole boot (≈ 120–280 KiB per fixture txg), so user data could eat into the slop by the boot's
    deferred total and leave the commit's metadata short. Now `zfs_wroom()` = the normal class − slop − `zfs_w_defer`
    (the FREE bytes of every log with txg ≥ T0 − 2: the mount's deferral window, summed by the log scan, + each
    published txg's — equal to the deferred bitmaps' popcount on every lane, `zfs-wpool`), and both checks count
    allocated + the open txg's allocations (its frees stay busy) against it; the read-back re-derives `zfs_w_defer`
    from the device. Stricter than OpenZFS (which defers two txgs), never looser. The plan line prints `defer=`.
18. **(B3a → B3b) What B3b must maintain at each commit** (the allocator assumes it): log T into the log table
    (`ZWA_LOG`), every touched metaslab's `U` (+8, set to T if 0) and unflushed net (+56, ± the txg's extents),
    `zfs_w_alloc += alloc − free`, then `zfs_dirty_reset`. Without the +8/+56 update a reload of a touched metaslab
    fails its strict `smp_alloc + net` check once log T is on disk. **DONE (B3b):** `zfs_wc_pub`, § c.2 note.
19. **(B3a → B3b) Chain blocks are OFF, uncompressed.** The MOS meta-dnode L1 (128 KiB) and its peers go out
    uncompressed × 3 DVAs every txg (§ d.2.4); ZLE would need the reserved-size re-plan of § d.2.4's MAY. Decide on the
    measured MOS shape (k.3) — the BP builder already writes any block OFF when given neither `ZWP_ZLE` nor `ZWP_HOLE`.
    **MEASURED (B3b):** OFF it is — 5 of a null txg's 8 blocks, ≈ 168 KiB × 3 per txg; the cost and the way out are
    k.25.
20. **(B3a) No embedded BPs.** Where OpenZFS embeds a compressed L0 of ≤ 112 B (`zio.c:1993-2008`) agnos writes a
    ZLE/OFF block of one allocation unit. Legal on every reader (WPA § 4: MAY); it costs one unit per small ZAP.
    **MEASURED (B3b):** the log ZAP (≤ 15 entries) goes out as a 512-byte OFF block × 3 copies where OpenZFS embeds it —
    1.5 KiB (ashift 9) / 12 KiB (ashift 12) per txg; OpenZFS reads it, rewrites it on top (phase C) and destroys it
    (phase D) without a remark.
21. **(B3a) The free estimate of a non-resident metaslab** is size − allocated (its deferred bytes are known only
    once loaded), so a pick can load a metaslab whose deferred space then fails the fit; the next pick follows (at most
    one load per metaslab per DVA). Measure the load count per txg at B3b on a pool with frees.
    **MEASURED (B3b, `loads=` on every plan line):** 0 on every lane whose metaslabs all stay resident; **32–38 per
    txg** on `full` held at ONE slot (7 metaslabs, 42–45 extents: every DVA of a 3-copy block and every FREE reloads —
    a replay of the space map and every log, 9 of them by the end). Correct (OpenZFS accepts every one), but a ≥ 3 TB
    disk (one slot) would pay it for real: B4 should group a txg's allocations and frees by metaslab (k.26).
22. **(B3a review → B3b) Eviction on disk.** Every fixture lane keeps all its metaslabs resident (resident = 1 MiB /
    (2 × bitmap) ≥ metaslab count), so the no-pin rule runs only in `zfs_wst_b3`'s one-slot evict stage. B3b's write
    smoke should add a lane whose metaslab count exceeds the slots (a pool whose bitmaps total > 1 MiB — e.g. a sparse
    4 GiB ashift-9 pool: OpenZFS makes 512 MiB metaslabs there, `vdev.c:3002-3007`, so 128 KiB bitmaps and 4 slots for
    ~7 metaslabs — or a `ZFS_WRITE_SELFTEST` knob capping the slots for the whole boot) so a commit's log T is written
    from a txg whose metaslabs were evicted and reloaded mid-txg.
    **DONE (B3b):** the op list holds the `full` fixture at ONE bitmap slot for the whole list (`zfs_wtest`, keyed on
    the fixture pool's name like the list itself); its eight null txgs load, evict and reload metaslabs 32–38 times
    each, mid-txg, and OpenZFS verifies the pool (A1–A6, the rewinds, import, scrub, round trip, destroy).
    **(B3b review)** The overlap rule (§ c.2) — a FREE of units already deferred, `zfs_xl_pre` > 0 — had not run on
    disk on any lane (0 such units on every final image); it does now: `full@pre` re-applies them at its one slot
    (`reapply=24`), `wr@u0` evicts on the undo of its first round (`loads=3 pre=3`) — § c.2's note.
23. **(B3a review) Contracts the commit loop must keep.** `zfs_bp_build` / `zfs_walloc_bp` / `zfs_wfree_bp` can load a
    metaslab: slots are evicted, the `zfs_ws_*` walker globals change, and the READER's metadata cache may drop any
    block, so the commit holds no `zfs_obj_blk` pointer and no resident slot number across them (the loader now has its
    own dnode slots, `ZWA_LDN`). The mount scratch (`ZWA_OS`/`STK`/`ZIL`/`CFG`) is the dirty-data arena: every function
    that writes it refuses while the open txg holds a dirty block (`zfs_wscr`). Block-clone kills cannot be detected
    from a BP: B4's kill routes every kill to `free_bpobj` while `block_cloning` is active (`zfs_wfree_bp` refuses gang,
    dedup and encrypted BPs by bit).
24. **(B3b → B4) The rounds and the dataset phase.** `zfs_commit` plans in rounds for U[] (§ c.3) and refuses a dirty
    set it does not own; a round undoes every extent and DROPS the dirty set. B4's dataset blocks are finalised into
    their parents' copies before the MOS phase, so a second round would have to replay the dataset phase from
    pre-finalisation copies. Simpler and recommended: on the FIRST commit after mount, preset `U = T` for EVERY
    metaslab with a space map and `U == 0` (legal — a `U` naming a log the metaslab has no entry in is what
    `metaslab_unflushed_bump` writes for clean metaslabs; it costs one U[] block once per mount on an upgraded pool —
    ~~nothing on an exported one~~ **(B3b review)** exported or not: an export leaves `U = 0` on every metaslab nothing
    touched since the upgrade (§ c.3's correction) —, nothing on a pool created with `log_spacemap` active); then no
    later txg can need a round, and the round loop becomes a guard that fails the commit by name. The correction makes
    the preset MORE necessary, not less: rounds = 2 is the normal first commit on any upgraded pool, so without it B4's
    dataset phase would be replayed on exactly those pools. Ruling wanted.
    **Decided 2026-10-10 (orchestrator, technical; the operator may override): preset U = T where U == 0 on the first
    commit after mount** (every upgraded pool needs it). **BUILT (B4):** `zfs_commit` presets `zfs_wc_pre` for every
    metaslab with a space map and U 0 when `zfs_wc_first` (set by the mount, cleared by the first publish); the round
    loop is gone — a metaslab with U 0 that still gets an entry (only one without a space map could: never allocated
    from, a free there is refused) fails the commit by name ("U[] preset incomplete"). `wr@u0` now plans ONE round
    with U = T for metaslabs 1, 3, 5 in its first commit (a dataset COW). ⚠ Lost with the rounds: the on-disk run of
    `zfs_wxl_undo`'s evict-on-undo (round 1 of `wr@u0` freed already-deferred units, § c.2's note) — it is proven in
    memory only now (`zfs_wst_b3`); a chain re-plan (k.25) undoes chain extents only, whose predecessors the
    `pre-defer` forge does not touch (open, k.30).
25. **(B3b → operator) The chain's write volume.** OFF chain blocks cost ≈ 0.5 MiB per txg (the 128 KiB L1 × 3 is 384
    KiB of it) and grew `$MOS` by ≈ 0.48 MiB once on every fixture (k.3); policy B (D1) makes it once per file. The way
    out is § d.2.4's MAY: **reserved-size ZLE** for chain indirects — allocate psize = roundup(2P + ⌈(lsize − P)/192⌉
    + 2), P the bytes up to the last non-hole BP, a PROVABLE bound on ZLE's output (a literal costs ≤ 2 bytes per input
    byte, a zero run ≤ 1 per 192), write the real output zero-padded to it. ≈ 5 KiB instead of 128 KiB for the MOS L1.
    Every reader decodes it (ZLE stops at lsize); but psize would then exceed roundup(c_len) — a BP OpenZFS itself never
    writes for that content (it rewrites in place only when psize is EQUAL, `zio.c` pass ≥ `zfs_sync_pass_rewrite`).
    Ruling wanted: take it (B4, ≈ 15 lines) or stay OFF.
    **(B3b review) Fragmentation, too.** Staying OFF also means every txg — a null one included — needs three
    CONTIGUOUS 128 KiB runs for the MOS L1 (3 DVAs) plus 16 KiB ones per dnode block, with no gang fallback (§ c.3:
    ENOSPC): on a fragmented pool a null txg fails its allocation and the pool goes read-only until reboot
    (`zfs_wfail_now`), where OpenZFS would compress the same L1 to a few KiB (or gang it). Reserved-size ZLE takes the
    run down to ≈ 5 KiB. Weigh it in the ruling beside the write volume and the hold time (§ b.3, k.8).
    **Decided 2026-10-10 (orchestrator, technical; the operator may override): compress the self-referencing chain
