    indirects into a RESERVED psize** — reserve a size, fill, ZLE-compress, and if the result does not fit the
    reservation re-plan with a larger one (bounded); DATASET chain blocks (not self-referencing) compress after their
    content is final per D4. **BUILT (B4, `zfs_wc_chrun` / `zfs_wc_try` / `zfs_wc_rsv` / `zfs_wc_cfin`):** before
    step (2) the MOS chain blocks' bytes, the rootbp and the MOS's space are snapshotted (`ZWA_CSN`, 448 KiB; a bigger
    chain goes out OFF unreserved); step (2) gives each chain INDIRECT the psize of its ZLE size at that moment (its
    non-chain children final, its chain children allocated with fill and checksum still 0) + 128 B per such child (40
    of its bytes still change; ZLE spends ≤ 3 output bytes per changed byte) + 64 (a literal run needs 64 bytes of
    room), rounded to the allocation unit (OFF when that reaches lsize); step (4) compresses the final content into it
    zero-padded, and an output that does not fit records its real size + 256 (`ZWA_RSV`) and RE-PLANS steps (2)-(4):
    their extents undone to the mark (`zfs_wxl_undo`; log T is encoded from a SORTED COPY of the extent list, `ZWA_XLS`,
    so the list still undoes by position), the snapshot restored, at most `ZWK_RPLMAX` = 3 times, the attempt after
    that OFF. The other chain blocks (log T, the dnode blocks, the objset_phys) stay OFF. On disk: the op list's
    `zfs_wc_tight` reserves 512 B on the first attempt of every rw lane's second null txg — always too small — and
    every lane's plan line shows `replans=1` there (the re-planned txg is T − 1, one of the guest's A4 rewinds); no
    other txg re-planned on any lane. **Measured:** a null txg allocates **125,952 B at ashift 9 / 172,032 B at 12**
    (B3b: 514,560 / 552,960 — the MOS L1 is one allocation unit × 3 now, not 384 KiB), and the MOS grows ONCE by
    ≈ 96–108 KiB (B3b: ≈ 485 KiB) when its chain first replaces OpenZFS's lz4 blocks. The txg no longer needs three
    contiguous 128 KiB runs — the largest chain run is a 16 KiB dnode block. As k.25 said, the reserved psize may
    exceed roundup(c_len) — a BP OpenZFS never writes for that content but reads (ZLE stops at lsize): every OpenZFS
    phase of the verify accepted it.
26. **(B3b → B4) Metaslab loads at low residency.** 32–38 loads per null txg at one slot (k.21). B4's dataset txgs
    allocate and free far more; sort each phase's frees by metaslab and prefer, for every DVA, a metaslab already
    resident (the pick does) — and measure again on `full`.
    **MEASURED (B4, `full` at one slot):** the dataset COW's txg made **55** loads (20 blocks, 82 extents), its null
    txgs 38 (58 in the re-planned one: an undone attempt reloads). Not regrouped yet: a txg's allocations follow the
    walk order and each FREE its predecessor — open for B5, whose writes multiply both (k.31).
27. **(B3b → B7) MOS gang / dedup predecessors.** A null txg kills MOS blocks with `zfs_wfree_bp`, which refuses a gang
    or dedup BP: on a fragmented pool whose MOS has a gang block the commit fails by name and the pool goes read-only
    until B7's `free_bpobj` (OpenZFS frees a MOS gang through `spa_free_bplist` in the same txg, § e.2).
    **(B7) STILL OPEN — not built:** B7 defers DATASET kills (§ e.2's B7 note); a MOS gang / dedup predecessor still fails
    its commit by name. Deferring it would append to `free_bpobj` from the MOS phase's steps (1) AND (2), so the
    `free_bpobj` block, its dnode block and `$FREE`'s dnode block would have to be CHAIN blocks (planned after every
    kill, written at step (4)), with the predecessors pre-scanned before step (1). No fixture has a MOS gang (the forced-
    ganging txg made none); a fragmented real pool would. For B8+ or a ruling.
28. **(B3b → B4) Log T's size bound.** One 128 KiB block holds the bound `8·(12N + 2·min(ms_count, 6N) + …)`; with the
    dirty set full (N = 1,024) that is ≈ 98 KiB on a 15-metaslab pool but ≈ 131 KiB on a 2,048-metaslab one — refused
    by name ("log space map over 128 KiB"). B4 must either tighten the bound (debug words per metaslab actually
    touched) or write a two-level log (`dn_nlevels` 2 under the same rules).
    **(B4) Still open — moved to B5**, whose data writes are the first to approach it: a B4 txg has 18–20 dirty blocks
    (bound ≈ 2 KiB); 1,024 dirty blocks — B5's 4 MiB of data at 4 KiB records — would reach it.
    **ANSWERED (B5) by the policy, not a two-level log:** a write or truncation stops dirtying at `ZWF_TXGBLK` = 512
    blocks and commits (the rest goes to the next txg), so N ≤ 512 + the commit's own ≈ 60: the bound is 8 · (12 · 573 +
    2 · min(ms_count, 3,438) + …) ≤ 87.8 KiB even at 2,048 metaslabs, and the extent list (≤ 6 a block) stays under
    `ZWK_MAXXL`. B5's txgs hold 17–22 blocks (log T 272–696 bytes). B11's thresholds sit below it.
    **(B5 review) The answer held for writes and FAILED for truncations — both fixed.** A truncation's kills belong to no
    dirty block, so (1) its kill list was never measured against `ZWK_MAXXL`: 4,096 freed 4 KiB L0s of one 20 MiB file
    overflowed the list INSIDE the commit (`txg extent list full: extents 4096`, the pool read-only); now every listed
    kill counts its DVAs and every dirty block 6 extents, with `ZWF_CRSV` (96) blocks more for what the commit and the
    walk's dnode_dirty_l1 / boundary paths add (`zfs_wf_xfit`) — `wr`'s AO_TRUNC of 5,137 blocks went in 2 txgs (3,553 and
    1,790 extents); and (2) log T was sized from the dirty-block count alone, so ≈ 230 non-adjacent freed blocks
    overflowed a 4 KiB log (`log space map over its planned size`); now from the extents already recorded + 6 per MOS
    block still to finalise, capped at `ZWK_MAXXL` — which bounds the encoding at 8 · (2 · 4,096 + 2 · 2,048 + 1) =
    98,320 B, one block at any metaslab count — `main`'s truncation of 250 appended blocks (each its own txg, so none
    adjacent) planned 345 extents and a 4,736 B log in one txg. Both on disk in `zfs-write-smoke`, OpenZFS 30/30.
29. **(B3b → B10) Logs accumulate.** Every txg leaves one log + its block (×3) and a log-ZAP entry until OpenZFS flushes
    them at its next import (C-zdb proves it does). The `full` lane already runs 9 logs and a grown log ZAP; B10's
    hygiene bounds it for long agnos runs.
30. **(B4 → B5) Evict-on-undo is back to in-memory only.** k.24's preset removed the second round in which `wr@u0`
    undid FREEs of already-deferred units on disk (`zfs_wxl_undo` evicting); a k.25 re-plan undoes only the chain's
    extents, and the `pre-defer` forge pre-defers `features_for_write`'s block — non-chain. Either forge the MOS L1
    instead (the first block every commit kills inside the chain) on one lane and let the tight re-plan undo it, or
    accept `zfs_wst_b3`'s in-memory proof. Ruling wanted (no code change either way in B4).
31. **(B4 → B5) Allocation order at low residency.** `full` held at one slot: 55 metaslab loads for one dataset COW
    (k.26). B5's writes multiply the blocks of a txg; group a txg's allocations and its FREEs by metaslab before B5's
    thresholds are sized.
    **(B5) Measured, still open → B11:** the `full` fill's txgs (2, 1, 1 data blocks of 128 KiB, 20–21 blocks each)
    made 61–63 metaslab loads apiece at one slot, the null txgs after them 36–59. Not regrouped; B11 sizes its thresholds
    with it (and with k.8's iron numbers).
32. **(B4 → B5) Indirects that do not compress.** A dataset indirect is ZLE (D4): one with many live children (a full
    L1 of a large file: 1,024 BPs) does not beat the 12.5 % floor and goes out OFF — 128 KiB × (copies + 1) per
    level rewritten, where OpenZFS's lz4 writes a fraction. B4's targets have ≤ 16 children per L1 (all compress);
    B5's appends to big files will pay it per write. D4 (b) (an lz4 compressor, ≈ 250 lines) is the remedy — to be
    weighed against the budget (§ g: B4 measured +76 %).
    **(B5) Still open:** the op list's files keep ≤ 25 live children per L1 (all compress); no B5 lane appends to a
    1,024-child L1. Its cousin is new and measured (k.38): DATA is written OFF too (D4 a).
33. **(B4 → B5) Reserved ZLE for chain dnode blocks.** The MOS chain's dnode blocks (2–3 per txg, 16 KiB × 3) stay
    OFF; the same reservation would compress them (their content changes more between steps (2) and (4): dn_used,
    the log dnode's bonus, the dsl_dir bonuses), at the price of more re-plans. Measure the miss rate first.
34. **(B4) A guest cross-check of the user-space bytes.** The guest's B-acct checks the uid-0 object count as a delta;
    the BYTES and every group / project are checked only by the host recount (h7) — which reads the ZAPs with the
    image tool's parser. A guest step comparing `zfs userspace / groupspace / projectspace -Hpn` with h7's recount (the
    host would ship it in the seed) would make OpenZFS's own reading of those ZAPs the judge. Cheap; B5 or B12.
    **DONE (B4), host side:** after the verify, zfs-write-smoke holds phase B's capture (`space` lines of
    `<lane>.B.acct` — OpenZFS's own `zfs userspace / groupspace / projectspace -Hp`) to the recount of the same image
    (`zfs-uacct-check.py --space`, `uspace` per lane; owner names map root / wheel → 0, the fixtures' only owners —
    a fixture with other owners needs `-n` in the capture, which is in the fixture's build half). Negative N3 is red
    there too.
35. **(B4 → cyrius) A stray top-level `}` is not diagnosed.** During B4 an extra `return 0; }` after a function made
    cycc 6.6.6 report 62 "undefined variable" errors in OTHER files (proc.cyr, elf.cyr, klug.cyr — every global and
    function declared after the stray brace's file vanished from pass 1) and nothing at the stray line. Found by a
    brace-balance scan, not by the compiler. To be filed in the cyrius repo (agnos never edits it; other-repo issues
    never block).
36. **(B4 → B5) The quota predicates count no in-flight bytes.** `zfs_wd_space` holds the COMMITTED usage to
    refquota / quota / the pool (OpenZFS's `used_on_disk + est_inflight >= quota`, with this txg's tempreserved bytes
    as `est_inflight`); B4 dirties one block per txg, so in-flight is 0. B5's policy-B coalescing (many `write()`s
    before one commit) must add the open txg's dirty data per dataset and dsl_dir as in-flight, or a burst of writes
    can pass a quota the first byte of the burst already met.
    **DONE (B5):** `zfs_wd_space(dse, bytes, infl, inflr)` — infl = Σ bytes × 24 the open txg reserved for the dataset
    (OpenZFS's tempreserved, worst-case inflated), inflr = Σ their asize × copies. refquota: referenced + infl ≥ refquota
    → ERESTART when infl > 0 (`dsl_dataset_check_quota`); each dir: used ≥ quota → EDQUOT / ENOSPC, used + infl ≥ quota
    + quota/32 → ERESTART (`dsl_dir_tempreserve_impl`), `parent_delta` taken at used + infl; agnos's allocation rule with
    inflr. ERESTART is OpenZFS's wait-and-retry: the write COMMITS what is in flight and asks again with none, so the
    committed usage decides. On disk: `full`'s fill was cut 2 / 1 / 1 blocks a txg by it and ended SHORT (524,288 of
    4,194,304 B) at ENOSPC with nothing in flight; the next write refused. EDQUOT on disk needs a small-refquota child
    (k.39). Policy B (B11) keeps the rule: its in-flight is the coalesced dirty data.
37. **(B4 review → B5) What no fixture COW reaches.** (1) A non-zero change into a dsl_dir with `dd_reserved`, or across
    a refreservation: `wr`'s `resv/r.txt` is absorbed whole by its refreservation, so the dir-level `parent_delta` and
    the parent's CHILD_RSRV transfer only ever see 0. (2) An accounting key ADDED, REMOVED at 0 or a microzap GROWN:
    B4's verb keeps a dnode's ids, so both sides share their keys; and every fixture file is uid / gid 0. (3) The k.25
    re-plan in a txg with a dataset phase — **now on disk**: the op list's first dataset COW of every pool is
    `zfs_wc_tight` (`replans=1` on its plan line; on `full` at one slot). (1) and (2) need a fixture RECIPE 4 — in `wr`
    a child with `reservation` = its used + 4 KiB and no refreservation, one with `refreservation` = its referenced +
    4 KiB (a COW of an embedded file then crosses both: ≈ 28 KiB of dsize), a file `chown`ed to 1001:1002, and `-n` in
    `acct_capture` (k.34) — and a fixture build sweeps EVERY stale work dir in the cache, an earlier agent's
    `r3-nopayload-8dc65342.work.3000703` among them, which this review was told to leave. Until then both run IN MEMORY
    on every rw lane at mount (ZFS_WRITE_SELFTEST, no write): `zfs_wst_resv` (the space step across both boundaries,
    absorbed and wholly above, there and back, judged by the identities in delta form) and `zfs_wst_uacct` (a dnode
    given uid / gid 1001 / 1002 + more user keys than the ZAP has room for: new keys, a 512 → 1,024 B growth, then
    every key removed at 0, judged on the blocks themselves). Fold RECIPE 4 into B5's fixture change.
    **What can still fail INSIDE a commit** (the pool then read-only until reboot): I/O errors, a metadata allocation
    that finds no contiguous run (no gang fallback, k.25), and the dirty arenas filling with the blocks the commit
    itself dirties (the accounting ZAPs, the MOS phase) — the policy's thresholds (B5, § b.2) must leave that room.
    Every POLICY refusal (gang / dedup / snapshot-owned / encrypted predecessors, `block_cloning`, fat or full accounting
    ZAPs, the delta table, space and quotas) is decided when the operation is dirtied.

38. **(B5 → B6/B11) Data written OFF (D4 a), measured.** A 10-byte write to a file whose block size is 128 KiB writes a
    whole uncompressed 128 KiB block (ring-3's `custom.txt` after its `AO_TRUNC`: OpenZFS would lz4 it to a few hundred
    bytes, or embed it). Text files rewritten by agnos lose their compression ratio. D4 (b) (lz4, ≈ 250 lines, ≈ 10 KB)
    stays the remedy; the budget after B5 has room for it at B5's ratio (§ g).
39. **(B5 → RECIPE 4) EDQUOT on disk.** The refquota / quota predicates (with in-flight, k.36) run on every write, and
    `wr`'s `quota/q.bin` write passes them in quota, but no lane reaches a quota: `agnoswr/quota`'s refquota is 32 MiB
    with 1 MiB used, and filling it costs ≈ 31 MiB of writes. A RECIPE 4 child with `refquota` ≈ its used + 256 KiB
    (and one with `quota` on a parent) would put the short write / EDQUOT on disk for OpenZFS to judge.
40. **(B5 → B11) The boot-long deferral shrinks the room.** `zfs_wroom` subtracts every free this boot (`zfs_w_defer`),
    so a long agnos boot loses user space txg by txg: every txg defers ≈ 250 KB more (its MOS chain's and its rewritten
    blocks' predecessors — `full`'s fill txgs moved `defer=` 770,048 → 1,269,760 B), none of which an allocation may take
    this boot, and the fill fit 524,288 B where B4 measured ≈ 1.8 MB of user room at mount. Stricter than OpenZFS (2
    txgs), never looser; B10's flushes do not change it. A ruling on releasing deferred frees after
    T + 2 (OpenZFS's rule — the frees are durable in log T) belongs with B11's thresholds.
    **(B5 review) It degraded into a COMMIT failure, not ENOSPC — fixed; the ruling is still open.** The room was OpenZFS's
    figure over the whole class while the allocator uses only the metaslabs with a space map (4 of 15 on `wr`): user
    data passed every predicate until a commit found no metaslab (`no space: no metaslab holds bytes 4096`, the pool
    read-only, after 212 one-block txgs). `zfs_wroom` is now min(OpenZFS's figure, `zfs_wphys` = the synced metaslabs
    less `zfs_w_defer` less `ZWK_MDRES`) and a txg opens only while `ZWK_MDRES` is left (`zfs_wf_begin`); OpenZFS's quota
    model on `dd_used` keeps OpenZFS's figure (`zfs_wroomz`). On disk: `wr`'s burst stopped at its 82nd one-block write,
    `no space: the metaslabs with a space map are full`, a SHORT write; the host's room check (`zfs-room-check.py`)
    holds where it stopped to the space record. Releasing deferred frees after T + 2 (this entry) and giving a
    never-synced metaslab a space map (k.46) would each give that room back.
    **DECIDED (operator ruling for B11 + prior art) and BUILT at B11 — k.125:** release after TXG_DEFER_SIZE = 2 txgs,
    exactly OpenZFS's `metaslab_sync_done` rotation (a free of T allocatable from T + 3).
41. **(B5 → B7) The generation check is built but cannot fire yet.** Every ZFS fd carries `ZPL_GEN` and every op compares
    it (D11), but nothing frees an object before B7: B7's gate must open an fd, unlink the file, create one that reuses the
    number, and see the old fd's read / write / `SEEK_END` answer -1.
    **ANSWERED (B7):** it fires — on disk in the op list (`agnos9`: an `AO_RDWR` fd across the unlink of `b6/many/f03`,
    then the create of `b6/many/r03` that takes the same object number; `agnoswr`: an `AO_DIRECTORY` fd across `rmdir
    b6empty` and the `mkdir b6e2` that takes its number; a fd across the LAZY unlink of `rs4k/lazy.bin` — links 0) and from
    ring 3 (zfsx 138-139): `read`#5, `write`#1 and `SEEK_END` -1 each time, `getdents`#29 -1 for the directory fd.
42. **(B5 → B6/B7) Spill-held SA attributes.** A write refuses an object whose `SIZE` / `MTIME` / `CTIME` / `FLAGS` are
    not in the bonus (an SA in a spill block; named). No fixture object has them there — `dnode/n00` keeps its xattrs in
    the spill and its core attributes in the bonus — so the refusal has not run on disk; B6's create builds bonus-only SAs
    anyway. Spill COW (an in-place update of a spill-held attribute) is the follow-on if a real pool needs it.
43. **(B5) Truncations larger than one txg** are cut from the TOP, txg by txg (each a `trunc` of the remaining range, its
    `dn_maxblkid` lowered; the SIZE moves in the last — OpenZFS's own between-chunks state of `dmu_free_long_range`), and
    the first / last L1 are dirtied per chunk. What binds is the metadata arena (each dirtied L1 is 128 KiB beside the
    dataset's ≈ 800 KiB meta-dnode chain and `ZWF_MRSV`): ≈ 4–5 L1s a txg, i.e. a freed range past ≈ 512 MiB of 128 KiB
    records or ≈ 16 MiB of 4 KiB ones. No fixture file comes near (the largest freed range is 16 L0s under one L1), so
    the multi-txg path has not run on disk; a lane with such a file would put it there (B7's unlink of a big file needs
    the same machinery, k.10).
    **(B5 review) On disk now** (`wr`: 20 MiB of 4 KiB records through an fd — 5 full L1s — then AO_TRUNC: 5,137 kills
    in 2 txgs, the extent list binding before the arena), with two more rules: every block the truncation will free or
    rewrite is judged BEFORE its first txg (`zfs_wf_okscan`, read-only over the indirects), so a refusal by name changes
    nothing; and the object's `ZPL_GEN` is re-checked after each `fs_lock` release. A LATER txg can still fail (ENOSPC
    for its commit's metadata — `zfs_wf_begin` — or an I/O error): the file then reads zeros past what the earlier txgs
    freed with its SIZE unchanged, OpenZFS's own between-chunks state (ABI § 3.6 says so). No lane has a refusable block
    deep in a big file, so the pre-scan's refusal path runs only on `gang/gang.bin` (whose first txg refused anyway).
44. **(B5) `AO_TRUNC` on a read-only open truncates** (ext2's rule, POSIX-unspecified); the fd is then read-only. Keep, or
    refuse `AO_TRUNC` without write access on ZFS? A one-line change either way; the ABI table should say which.
    **DECIDED 2026-10-11 (operator): keep** — `AO_TRUNC` without write access truncates, ext2's ABI rule; the fd is
    then read-only. Recorded in ABI § 3.6 (`open`#7's row) and at `zfs_open` (B6). A file `AO_CREAT` has just created
    is not truncated (nothing to free; OpenZFS's `zfs_create` truncates only an existing file).
45. **(B5 review → RECIPE 4) User / group / project quotas.** Enforced now (`zfs_wd_idq` before each txg, as zfs_write
    before each chunk: the owner's key in the master node's `userquota@` / `groupquota@` / `projectquota@` ZAP, else
    `default…quota`, against the committed `*used` ZAP; EDQUOT → a short write). Every write runs the lookups and finds
    no quota on any fixture, so EDQUOT itself has not run on disk: a RECIPE 4 child with `userquota@root` (or
    `defaultuserquota`) ≈ its used + 256 KiB, written past it, would put it there for OpenZFS to judge (with k.39).
46. **(B5 review → B10 / B11) Never-synced metaslabs.** agnos allocates only in metaslabs with a space map (`zfs_wpick`),
    so `wr` gives user data ≈ 51 MiB of OpenZFS's ≈ 113 (4 of 15 metaslabs). OpenZFS's `metaslab_sync` creates a space
    map at a metaslab's first allocation (`space_map_alloc`); agnos would need the same MOS object creation it already
    does for log T, plus the metaslab array entry and the vdev ZAP's histogram — B10's space-map machinery. Rule with
    k.40. **(B6 review, measured)** It made a gate depend on luck: which metaslabs the fixture recipe touches varies
    between builds (main: 8 of 15 on r4, 7 on the first r5 build), and on 7 the `main` lane's op list (≈ 95 MB deferred
    over ≈ 330 txgs) ran out in its burst. RECIPE 5 spreads main (15 of 15 with a space map, held ≥ 12); `wr` keeps its
    4 of 15 on purpose (its burst is MEANT to run out).
47. **(B5 review) `ZWK_MDRES` is a worst case.** One commit's metadata is bounded by the metadata arena × 3 copies
    (5,898,240 B); measured, a B5 txg allocates 0.13–0.3 MB of metadata. A bound from the txg's own dirty metadata at
    plan time would give small pools that room back, at the price of a predicate that changes as the txg grows.
48. **(B5 review → operator) IF=0 for the whole `write`#1.** `fs_lock` is now released between txgs, but the calling CPU
    runs the whole syscall with interrupts off, as every agnos syscall does (SFMASK): a 1 GiB write keeps ONE CPU's
    timer and devices masked for ≈ 2 min (other CPUs proceed). Returning SHORT after one txg (POSIX allows it only at
    ENOSPC / a signal; agnos userland callers would have to loop) or re-enabling interrupts between txgs (the AC flag,
    the kernel-stack rules) would bound it; either is a ruling, with B11's thresholds and k.8's iron numbers.
    **(B11)** Inside the COMMIT the CPU now takes its interrupts (k.126's windows: AC cleared, IF / AC restored exactly,
    preemption off); between txgs and while a txg is dirtied it is still IF=0 — the rest of this entry stands.
49. **(B5 review) The op list's plan line costs ≈ 0.45 s of console rendering per txg** (k.8) — 250 txgs of `main`'s
    burst spend ≈ 2 min printing. A quieter op list (the plan line to serial only, or one line per op) would cut the
    smoke's time; the plan line's fields are what the smoke checks, so it moves, not goes.
50. **(B6 → B7) Object numbers come back.** `zfs_wc_slot` is first fit: once B7 frees an object, a later create reuses
    its number — in the same boot. OpenZFS's own allocator moves through 128-object chunks and rescans for holes only
    after an L1's worth of objects or enough frees (`os_rescan_dnodes`, `dmu_object.c:117-160`: "dmu_traverse depends
    on the behavior that we use multiple blocks of the dnode object before going back to reuse objects"). Reuse is
    legal — `traverse_visitbp` sets `td_realloc_possible` whenever the meta-dnode's `dn_maxblkid` > 0, true of every
    fixture dataset — and the fd's generation check (D11) is what makes it safe for agnos's own fds. B7's gate (k.41)
    must create into a number its unlink freed and see the old fd fail. Ruling wanted only if `zfs send -i` of an
    agnos-written range ever matters: skip numbers freed in this boot (cheap: the boot's freed-object list).
    **(B7, measured)** Numbers come back at the next create: `agnos9`'s freed `b6/many/f03` (a B6 object, below every
    other free slot) went to the next create; `dir20/e06`'s 134 did NOT — the first fit took slot 24 below it — so the
    gate targets the lowest free number. The `zfs send -i` ruling stays open.
51. **(B6 → B8) Symlinks in child datasets, and REGISTRY gaps.** Every fixture CHILD dataset but `agnostank/custom`
    (RECIPE 5) holds only the file / directory layout (k.2), so `symlink`#63 there is refused by name until B8 can add a
    LAYOUTS entry (count + 2, a
    decimal key, a BE u16 array, in the fat LAYOUTS ZAP, in the same txg as its first user). B8's LAYOUTS add is its
    first fat-ZAP add with a consumer; REGISTRY registration (an attribute a dataset never used — PROJID on a pre-project
    pool) stays refused (no fixture needs it). **(B8: BUILT, k.79)** — the child-dataset symlinks of the op list and
    zfsx now succeed; REGISTRY registration is still refused.
52. **(B6) A new directory's microzap is written OFF** — 512 B, one allocation unit × (copies + 1) — where OpenZFS
    would EMBED it (an empty microzap compresses far below 112 B): ≈ 1 KiB per `mkdir` at ashift 9, 8 KiB at 12, until the
    first growth. Legal (D4 a); lz4 (D4 b) would let agnos embed too.
53. **(B6) Every add rewrites the whole microzap** — up to 128 KiB × (copies + 1) — plus the parent's and the new
    object's dnode blocks and the five meta-dnode indirects: `main`'s 56-file batch in `coff/kid` ran 56 txgs of
    225–276 ms each on QEMU/KVM, `b6`'s microzap growing 512 → 4,096 B on the way. Policy B (B11) coalesces a burst of
    creates into one txg; until then a big directory fill is one commit per name.
54. **(B6 → B7) What B7 removes, B6 counted.** `rmdir`#10 needs SIZE == 2 and LINKS 2 (FreeBSD's phase C already
    `rmdir`s agnos's `b6empty`, and its `rm -rf` in phase D empties agnos's trees — both pass); unlink / rmdir must undo
    exactly what a create did (the parent's SIZE − 1, LINKS − 1 for a directory, MTIME / CTIME, the object's slot
    zeroed, its `obj-` counts − 1, its blocks killed) — `zfs-sa-check.py`'s parent rule and h7 extend to removals.
    **ANSWERED (B7):** `zfs-sa-check.py` is one namespace model now (header ⭐ B7): every op in order — write, create,
    unlink, rmdir, rename, link — moves the model's SIZE / LINKS / PARENT / MTIME / CTIME / FLAGS of each object and
    parent, and every object any op touched is held to it in EVERY SA attribute; a freed object's slot must hold no
    dnode (unless a later create took the number), a lazy one LINKS 0 and its DELETE_QUEUE entry. h7 needed no removal
    rule — it is a recount (a freed object's `obj-` and bytes leave the ZAPs only if the commit's diff subtracted them) —
    and gains the `free_bpobj` identity (Σ entries == header == `$FREE` == the kernel's Σ `fbpo=`); h8 skips a reused
    number by its `ZPL_GEN` and allows meta-dnode hole births in removal txgs.
55. **(B6) `utf8only` and mode.** A non-ASCII name on a `utf8only` dataset is refused (no `u8_validate`); the ABI has no
    mode argument, so agnos creates FreeBSD's umask-022 modes only (`0100644` / `040755` / `0120755`). A mode argument
    or `chmod` would need `zfs_acl_chmod`'s full form (DENY entries when a class has more than the one before it, e.g.
    0604) — an ABI ruling first. **(B6 review)** The mask helper is now `acl_trivial_access_masks`' for every mode (it was
    right for 0644 / 0755 only), and a mode that needs the allow0 / deny entries is refused by name (`zfs_wc_trivok`), so
    a mode argument would fail closed until that form is built; the host oracle derives its ACL from OpenZFS's C, not
    from agnos's helper.
    **(B11) The `utf8only` half is BUILT — k.128:** u8_validate's rules (`zfs_wenc.cyr` `zfs_u8_valid`); a valid name is
    created, an invalid one refused by name with its class; normalised datasets keep refusing non-ASCII names.
56. **(B6) What only FreeBSD's ACL code sees.** A wrong `DACL_COUNT` (4 for 3 ACEs) passed all 31 guest steps — stat,
    the manifest, zdb, scrub and the round trip never read an ACL — and PANICKED FreeBSD's kernel the first time the ACL
    was read (`getfacl`: "acl_from_aces: a_type is 0xffff"). The verify now reads every created object's ACL in phase B
    (B-acl: `getfacl` == a FreeBSD-made twin's); any later bite that writes an ACL (chmod, inheritance) must keep it.
57. **(B6 review → B8) A microzap past 128 KiB cannot be READ either.** `large_microzap` is on the writer's list and a
    create there is refused by name — judged on the parent dnode's block size BEFORE the lookup (`zfs_wcreate`), since
    the reader's block cache (`ZK_METAMAX`, one 128 KiB slot) cannot load the block: so on such a parent even an
    existing name answers the `large_microzap` refusal, not EEXIST, and `open` / `stat` / `getdents` of anything in that
    directory fail (-1, "path unresolvable") — a reader gap since 1.57.11. No fixture has one (FreeBSD makes one only past
    `zap_micro_max_size`, a tunable up to 1 MiB with the feature). B8 (fat / large ZAPs) is where a streamed microzap read
    and a fixture for it belong; ruling wanted on whether the reader gap is 1.57.12's or later. **(B8)** still open —
    k.83.
58. **(B6 review) The refusal log.** Every refused ZFS mutation syscall (`open`#7 with a write flag, `mkdir`#9,
    `symlink`#63, `write`#1) prints `zfs: <verb> refused (<why>)` (`zfs_wr_say`, under `fs_lock`), so the ring-3 gate
    holds each refusal to its NAME, in order (`EXPECT_REFUSED`). A ring-3 program can make the kernel print one line per
    failing call — unthrottled, ≈ 60 characters (≈ 5 ms of serial at 115,200 baud, inside the lock). Fine for a gate and
    for an operator; a rate limit (or a quieter default) is a ruling if it ever matters. B7's `unlink` / `rmdir` /
    `rename` / `link` arms should log the same way, and the gate's list grows with them.
59. **(B6 review → B7) Object and project quotas wait for OpenZFS's upgrade.** As OpenZFS, an object quota is enforced
    only once the objset's `os_flags` carries `USEROBJACCOUNTING_COMPLETE`, a project quota (bytes or objects) only once
    it carries `PROJECTQUOTA_COMPLETE` (`zfs_id_overobjquota` / `_overblockquota`) — and agnos never runs the upgrade
    (`dmu_objset_id_quota_upgrade`) OpenZFS starts at that point, so on an upgraded pool those quotas stay unenforced
    until an OpenZFS mount completes it: OpenZFS's own answer meanwhile. No fixture sets a quota (k.45) or carries an
    incomplete flag (measured below, § h B6 review).
60. **(B6 review → B8) The 2 KiB objset.** A create on a dataset whose objset_phys is 2 KiB on a `project_quota` pool (or
    smaller than 2 KiB) is refused: FreeBSD grows it to 4 KiB in memory at open, so its creates carry `PROJID` and its
    sync writes the 4 KiB objset with a new projectused ZAP. B4 / B5's writes there keep § e.3's rule (the objset stays
    2 KiB; the objects match FreeBSD's) — the only difference is the objset's size and that ZAP, which OpenZFS creates on
    its next sync. Building the growth (a 4 KiB objset_phys + `zap_create_claim` of object −3 with the deltas) lifts both;
    no fixture has such a dataset (every head objset on the five rw images is 4 KiB) — a `zpool upgrade` recipe would.
61. **(B6 review, measured) The production clock lags and coarsens.** `zfs_wf_now` is `ntp_now()` — the boot's RTC read
    + `timer_ticks / 100` — and ticks are lost while a commit holds IF=0 (≈ 200 ms a txg): in the ring-3 gate every one
    of zfsx's eleven txgs (≈ 3 s of kernel time, serial 59.4 → 62.5 s) stamped the SAME second, ≈ 20 s behind the wall
    clock (QEMU started 15:34:44, the stamps read 15:35:32, the run sat ≈ 70 s after QEMU's start). Legal ZPL times —
    OpenZFS keeps whatever it is given — and inside the gate's window, but FreeBSD would stamp nanosecond wall time.
    A TSC-based clock for `zfs_wf_now` (the kernel already timestamps its log from the TSC) is the fix; ruling wanted on
    whether it is ZFS's or the kernel's timekeeping item (`ntp_now` serves every caller).

62. **(B7 → B8) Removals in fat ZAPs, and a fat DELETE_QUEUE.** Every B7 verb refuses a fat-ZAP parent by name
    (`agnoswr/fat`, `agnostank/dir3000` — the latter in the snapshotted root anyway), and the lazy path refuses a
    DELETE_QUEUE that is not a one-block microzap. B8's fat remove (free the chunks, `zap_num_entries` − 1, empty leaves
    kept) lifts both; the DELETE_QUEUE grows fat only past ~2,000 lazily unlinked objects (FreeBSD's own comment,
    `zfs_dir.c:262-269`). **(B8: BUILT)** — both refusals lifted (§ f.2, § f.5; k.76, k.85).
63. **(B7) agnos never DRAINS a delete queue.** Objects on a DELETE_QUEUE — agnos's lazy unlinks, and any FreeBSD left
    (an fd open across its last unlink at export time) — stay allocated and charged until OpenZFS's next read-write
    mount (`zfs_unlinked_drain`). FreeBSD drains at every mount; agnos could free them at its rw mount (or in idle txgs)
    with the same `zfs_wu_free`, one per txg — or split a big one across txgs (`zfs_rmnode`'s shape). Ruling wanted
    on whether 1.57.12 needs it (the space returns at the next OpenZFS import either way).
    **DECIDED (operator ruling with the B10 / B11 split; FreeBSD's `zfs_unlinked_drain` at every rw mount):** agnos
    drains its own DELETE_QUEUE — bite B10's track.
64. **(B7, measured) The boot-long deferral now refuses NAMESPACE ops late in a long boot.** `rename` and `link` charge
    the dataset's quota / the pool's room for one microzap growth (`zfs_wd_space` 512 B, as OpenZFS's
    `dmu_tx_hold_zap`), and after `main`'s ~600 txgs the deferred frees (~0.27 MiB each) put the root dsl_dir past the
    room: `rename … refused (no space: the pool is at its slop)` — measured on the first B7 run, the ops then moved before
    B5's burst. Unlink / rmdir charge nothing (netfree). OpenZFS clears its deferral every 2 txgs; this is k.40's rule
    (B10's flush / B11) showing up outside file data. On `full` it ran the other way (measured): the three B7 ops
    BEFORE B5's fill deferred enough that the fill got nothing (`write FAIL no space: the pool is at its slop`) and the
    rename was refused — so `full`'s B7 ops run AFTER the fill, where the pool sits at its slop: the unlink and rmdir
    commit (netfree, metadata into the slop) and the rename is refused by name, which the lane now requires.
    **DECIDED and BUILT at B11 (k.125): the refusal is gone.** With the frees of two txgs back released at every commit,
    `full`'s rename after the fill COMMITS (the lane now requires `rename /agnosfull/dir/s1.txt … moved` and its op), and
    `main` gains a rename after its 250-txg burst and the truncation (`zfs_wt_b11late`: `coff/kid/f01 → f01-late`,
    committed — the long-boot case this entry measured).
65. **(B7) The oracle's FreeBSD twins can be removed.** B-acl holds every created object's ACL to a FreeBSD-made object
    of the same type and mode in the same dataset AFTER the ops — an op list that unlinks the last FreeBSD symlink of a
    dataset where it also created one makes B-acl FAIL (measured: `agnos9/sym`, the first run). The list keeps one
    (`agnos9/sym`, `agnoswr/sym-short`); a B-acl that took its twins from the pristine copy would not depend on it.
66. **(B7) A rename / link's `PARENT` tolerance in phase C.** The round trip's `rm` of a name the WRITER linked leaves its
    other name with whatever PARENT the last link / rename set — OpenZFS's own k.15 — so C-k15 now tolerates such an
    object by id (`c.k15x`: `stat`'s inode when the removed name had links > 1). Measured: `agnos9/dir20/e05` (linked to
    `b6/e05`, the round trip removed `b6/e05`) was flagged "possibly leaked" and C-k15 went red before the rule.
67. **(B7) Directory renames move the namespace, not the grafts.** A directory with a dataset grafted below it is
    refused (EBUSY) as a rename source or target and as an rmdir target — the graft table is keyed by path and would be
    left pointing at the old name. OpenZFS moves a mountpoint only through `zfs rename`; agnos has no such verb.
68. **(B7, found by the gate — DECIDED, built) The space map histograms of the metaslabs a txg allocates in.** § e.4 left
    them to B10 on the premise that OpenZFS lets them drift; it lets them drift DOWN only (see § e.4's note), and
    `metaslab_load_impl` ASSERTs the stored segment weight (the highest nonzero `smp_histogram` bucket, then its count)
    is never above the loaded one's (`metaslab.c:2670`) — on a debug build an import that loads such a metaslab
    panics, and zdb `-mmmm` after an export aborts. Measured: `wr`'s B5 write over the three metaslabs with a space map
    (the B7 payload's fixture build — the B6 build had more, and the round trip's own allocations re-synced them) left
    every one at bucket 23 / count 1 with bucket 20 real; C-zdbm red (`VERIFY3U(weight, <=, msp->ms_weight) failed
    (414331165718085633 <= 396316767208603649)`: index 23 vs 22 after OpenZFS's frees). **Built** (`zfs_write.cyr`
    § "B7 (design k.68)"): after step (1) of the MOS plan — every allocation but the chain's made — `zfs_wh_plan` takes
    the txg's HISTOGRAM SET: each metaslab T allocated in whose STORED histogram (T − 1's, the reader's MOS) now has a
    segment weight above the exact one of its ALLOCATED bitmap (the true state; one segment per run of clear bits,
    bucket `highbit(run) − 1` in `sm_shift` units, the last bucket scaled as `space_map_histogram_add`); each member's
    space map dnode is dirtied and its dnode block made a CHAIN block — a dnode block step (1) already finalised and
    wrote is DEMOTED (`zfs_wh_demote`: its DVAs taken back out of T — never logged, as an allocate-and-free inside one
    txg never is — its born space returned, its BP a hole until the chain allocates it); log T's block grows for the new
    blocks' entries if its bound needs it, and `zfs_wh_fill` — once the chain is allocated — writes the exact histogram
    in place of the stored one. The chain's own allocations are checked too (`zfs_wh_check`): a metaslab one of them
    took below its stored weight joins the set — directly when its space map dnode is already in a chain block, else
    through a re-plan (k.25's machinery: the snapshot restored, the walk order and snapshot rebuilt; the set only grows,
    ≤ 8 such re-plans, then the commit fails by name).
    A weight is never RAISED: the stored histogram also bounds what a read-only
    zdb loads (the space map alone, the logs not replayed — the census's load probe, § i.3), and a lower one costs
    OpenZFS only an allocation preference until its first load. A txg whose allocations leave every stored weight
    standing — almost all of them: a weight drops when a metaslab's largest segment crosses a power of two — dirties
    nothing more. Plan line `hist=` (the histograms lowered). **First built EAGER** (every txg's set topped up to three
    metaslabs, their space map dnodes always in the chain): two more 16 KiB chain blocks per txg, written OFF ×3,
    +≈ 90 KB deferred per txg for the boot — `main`'s 250-txg burst stopped at write 125 and `full`'s fill got nothing
    (measured); a second cut kept the chain out of metaslabs it would break (an avoid set) and ran out of room at the
    end of `main`'s and `wr@u0`'s lists (`no metaslab holds bytes 4096`) — the set that GROWS instead is the fix. **Oracle**: the host replay (h6, `zfs-wms-check.py`) holds EVERY metaslab of
    every image to the rule — red on the first B7 `wr` image, green on every pristine fixture image (OpenZFS never
    breaks it); `wr` must lower at least one (`--hist`). The rare paths run ON DISK through the op list's knob
    (`zfs_wh_force`, `zfs_wc_tight`'s pattern): agnoswr's first COW puts every metaslab it allocates in into the set at
    plan time — on `wr@u0` the U preset has dirtied the dnode block holding the space map dnodes, so step (1) wrote it
    and it is DEMOTED — and its second only after the chain, which grows the set and RE-PLANS (`zfs-wtest: histogram set
    forced on 2 COWs: <d> step-(1) block(s) demoted, <r> chain re-plan(s) ok`, required on both lanes with d ≥ 1 on
    `wr@u0`); OpenZFS then judges both pools. B10's flush recomputes the histograms anyway; until then this is the rule.
69. **(B7) `wr@u0`'s forge on a three-metaslab build.** `zero-u` needs a metaslab with a space map and no log entry;
    when none exists it FLUSHES one's entries into its space map first (B5, `flush_for_u0`), never one holding a copy of
    `features_for_write`'s block (pre-defer's overlap lives in those entries). The B7 payload's `wr` build has exactly
    three such metaslabs, each with one of the block's three copies — the forge REFUSED. Now it flushes the highest of
    them when another copy's metaslab keeps its entries (that copy's FREE + ALLOC go into the space map, net 0, no
    longer deferred; the other two still are — the lane's `pre>0` holds). A recipe promise (≥ 4 metaslabs with a space
    map on `wr`) would remove the luck; not done (no B7 object needs it). **(B9: done another way — k.97: RECIPE 7
    spreads `wr` to every metaslab and the op list caps agnos's allocation there at the first 5.)**
70. **(B7 review, finding 3) `free_bpobj`'s appends are budgeted in the metadata arena.** A deferred kill (§ e.2) appends
    a BP to `free_bpobj`, whose 128 KiB L0 blocks — and, past `dn_nblkptr` × 1,024 BPs, its indirects — the commit
    dirties. Nothing counted them: the truncation walk filled the arena to `ZWK_MARENA − ZWF_MRSV` with its own
    indirects (on `wr`'s `rs4k`: a six-level meta-dnode, 656 KiB, + the file's L2 and L1 — 916 KiB measured) and the
    appends of its ~3,500 kills (4-5 blocks) overflowed inside the commit — `dirty metadata full`, the pool read-only until
    reboot — on ANY pool with `block_cloning` or `fast_dedup` active (every kill defers: agnos reads no BRT), and on
    gang / dedup objects. Found by forcing that deferral on disk (finding 3's free_bpobj coverage). The rule:
    `zfs_wfb_q` counts the kills the open txg will defer — each kill-list entry (`zfs_wf_kill`, `zfs_wu_kbp`) and each
    dirtied dataset block whose predecessor defers (`zfs_wblk`, outside the commit: `zfs_wc_in`) — and `zfs_wfb_meta(q)`
    is the exact bytes their appends dirty: the L0 blocks from `bpo_num_blkptrs` to its new end and, at every indirect
    level that end needs, the blocks its path spans, for q + `ZWF_FBM` (32: the commit's own deferrals and what one
    admission's dirtying counts). Every metadata admission adds it (`zfs_wf_mfit`: the write path, the walk, a create, a
    B7 op); a kill-list entry that would not fit ends the txg (a truncation goes on in the next; an unlink takes the LAZY
    path). The first deferral of a txg on a pool where nothing defers otherwise is the one unreserved append — refused by
    name when the arena could not hold it (`txg full: a deferred kill's free_bpobj blocks (commit first)`), never a
    commit that runs out (a first version checked it inside the commit's own COWs too and failed a commit by name:
    measured, hence `zfs_wc_in`). On disk: `wr`'s truncation in six txgs (fbpo 8.5 / 4.4 / 0.3 / 4.4 / 4.4 / 0.2 MB — the
    third and sixth stop at the level growth and the end), `free_bpobj 5212 BPs, 6 block(s), nlevels 2`. 2,624 B.
71. **(B7 review, finding 2) Path operands are read once.** The syscall arms pass ring-3 pointers in (AC=1, validated
    ranges), and B6 / B7 read the path several times — the name's checks, the parent's resolve, the copy kept, the name's
    resolve — so a second thread of the caller rewriting the buffer could make the KEPT name differ from the one checked
    and looked up (`..`, a `/` entry). `zfs_upath(k, path, len)` copies operand k into `ZR_UP` (2 × 1 KiB of the reader's
    area, under `fs_lock`) first: `zfs_wu_arg` (both operands of rename / link), `zfs_wcreate` and `zfs_open` (whose
    `AO_CREAT` create and re-resolve now see one copy). A path of 1,024+ bytes is refused as the resolver did (`path
    unresolvable: 36`). Not provable by a single-threaded test; the gates prove the copy changes nothing else.
72. **(B7 review, finding 3) The verify knows an xattr directory's tree.** Fixture RECIPE 6 gives `agnos9` `xd.txt`,
    whose xattr lives in an extended-attribute DIRECTORY (`xattr=dir` for it alone; checked by zdb at the build). Its
    unlink is LAZY by `ZPL_XATTR` (§ f.5); zdb `-ddddd` then calls the xattr directory and the xattr in it "possibly
    leaked" — `zfs_obj_to_path_impl` answers ESTALE for the queued owner only, the walk from its xattr tree dies at the
    owner's removed name — exactly as on a pool FreeBSD itself unlinked such a file on. The guest's `ds_check` (A5, C-k15,
    D-zdb) sets aside precisely those objects: `ZFS_XATTR` in its pflags and in every parent's up to an owner zdb lists
    "on delete queue" (`FLAGGED.xt`, an A5 INFO line); an xattr directory whose owner was FREED stays flagged — the
    orphan a misfiring `ZPL_XATTR` rule would leave. Measured on `ashift9`: objects 266 and 267 set aside, A5 PASS.
    Also, A5 is now EXACTLY the k.15 list: a predicted object zdb does not flag is a FAIL (it was INFO).
73. **(B7 review, finding 3) The namespace model learns the lazy paths.** `zfs-sa-check.py` took a rename's target as
    numberless (its line names the source) — a lazily removed target the ops created could not be found — and treated a
    LAZY object's slot as free, so an earlier freed object whose number it took read as "still holds a dnode". The
    rename's where-line now names the target (`moved (the target object <t> freed | on the DELETE_QUEUE)`), and a lazy
    object keeps the slot it took. The FreeBSD twin also excludes objects with a `ZPL_XATTR`.

    `ext2_write_at_dma` → `blk_write_sectors_direct`) has NO caller since the room: klug's spill and its 64 KB prealloc

    room 0`, -smp 1 + 4), VT-d, HDA — 21 passed. Not re-run: the three ZFS rows, lifecycle, the TCP / socket / ICMP

    **The second ROOM review (2026-10-12) — six findings, all taken:** **(p)** (finding 3) (a) said the new line is

75. **(B8) The pointer table's growth is reached by a SALT KNOB, not by size.** An embedded table (2^10 entries at 16 KiB
    blocks) grows only when a leaf whose prefix is 10 bits long must split: under a random salt ≈ 1,024 full leaves,
    ≈ 10^5 names — at one create per txg (≈ 2 s of boot each on the op list's console) days, and more room than any
    fixture pool has. So the op list makes `b8/` under ITS OWN salt (`zfs_wc_fsalt` 0x5A17B9 — odd and non-zero like
    every OpenZFS salt; the knob is 0 in production, where `zfs_wc_salt` derives it as B6 does) and CHOOSES its names by
    their hash, 255 bytes each (15 chunks: 42 to a leaf — fewer txgs than short names, and 13-chunk name arrays on every
    split): 42 `b` names in the top quarter (leaf 1 splits: 2 leaves, 49 entries), then `a` names sharing the top 12
    bits 0x5A3 until `zt_nextblk` is set — 79 of them: their leaf splits down to prefix 10, the table moves to a block
    of its own (2^11) and doubles as the same prefix keeps splitting (2^12, 2^13 in 4 blocks), and the doubling to 2^14
    is LEFT UNDER WAY (`1 copied`, 14 leaves, 128 entries) — the next ops copy one block each, 2 `b` names split a leaf
    mid-grow (`zap_table_store`'s dual writes, `3 copied`, 15 leaves), and 10 more names switch the table (`2^14 in 8
    block(s) at 22`, the old four blocks freed as holes of birth T). Every checkpoint is the host model's prediction
    (`zfs-fz-ops.py`, scripts/check/zfs-fatzap.py), and gate 37 replays the same shape against the Python model. The
    code path is the salt-independent one OpenZFS takes for any ZAP; only the names are chosen.
76. **(B8) `zap_shrink` is not built.** OpenZFS 2.4 merges an emptied leaf with its sibling (`zap_shrink`,
    `zap_shrink_enabled` 1) inside `fzap_remove`; agnos keeps it — the format allows empty leaves (every release before
    2.3 left them; the reader and zdb read them) and OpenZFS only shrinks on the remove that empties a leaf. On disk:
    `dir3000`'s smallest leaf emptied by its unlinks, `longn`'s two leaves emptied before its `rmdir`; h9 counts them
    (`N empty`), zdb `-dddd` reads them, the round trip's mount, writes and scrub are clean. Building it (a sibling
    merge, the table's entries repointed, `zap_num_leafs` − 1) is a follow-on, not a correctness gap.
77. **(B8) An accounting ZAP with an EXTERNAL pointer table refuses the dataset.** The user / group / project-used ZAPs
    are written INSIDE the commit, whose metadata arena is sized at admission (k.70's rule): `zfs_wz_urs` reserves a new
    leaf and a table block per new key — an embedded table's move to its own block included — but not an external
    table's doubling (2 × `zt_numblks` new blocks and a copy that runs over later txgs). Such a ZAP needs > 1,024 leaves,
    ≈ 10^5 owners; refused by name (`a user accounting ZAP with an external pointer table`). No fixture comes near.
78. **(B8) Accounting microzaps are upgraded at ADMISSION, on a conservative count.** OpenZFS upgrades a full accounting
    microzap inside the sync, in the `zap_increment` that finds it full; agnos's commit cannot grow its arena, so the op
    whose new keys the microzap cannot take (dse +120's count) upgrades it when it is admitted (`zfs_wz_urs` →
    `zfs_wz_upgrade`), and the commit then updates a fat ZAP. The count is B4's: two new keys per ZAP for a created
    object (and for a dnode without the accounting flags), not looked up — so agnos upgrades a microzap with fewer than
    two free chunks where OpenZFS, finding the keys present, would have left it micro (a create is uid 0, which every
    ZPL dataset's hidden shares directory already owns). Legal — OpenZFS reads and writes a fat accounting ZAP like any
    other — and the price of never running out inside the commit; a txg dropped before its commit drops the upgrade with
    it (the dse entry and the dirty set go together). On disk: `uidm`, whose 128 KiB userused microzap holds 2,046 keys
    in 2,047 chunks, upgraded by `create /agnosfz/uidm/new.txt` (`1 ZAP upgrade(s)`; h7's recount and the guest's
    `zfs userspace` agree). The fixture's first build taught the count: 1,022 files there made 2,048 keys — the shares
    directory's uid 0 is an owner too — and OpenZFS upgraded it itself (k.81).
79. **(B8) A new SA layout, and its oracle.** `zfs_wc_layout` registers a missing layout as `sa_add_layout_entry` does
    (§ f.1). The oracle learnt it: `zfs-sa-check.py` accepts layouts beyond the start image's only as NEW ones, numbered
    `len(start layouts) + 2` on, each array distinct from every other, the start layouts byte-identical, a created
    object's SA held to its layout; the FreeBSD twin (B-acl, the SA check) falls back pool-wide when the object's dataset
    holds no FreeBSD object of its kind (fixture RECIPE 7 gives `fz` one FreeBSD symlink, `uids/fbsym`, the twin for
    agnos's first symlinks in the root). `agnosfull` and `agnoslogs` hold no symlink anywhere, so their B6 symlink ops
    (refusals until B8) were DROPPED from the op list rather than kept twinless; the layout add runs on `wr`, `main` and
    `fz` (and `sym2` matches the layout `sym` registered).
80. **(B8) The fat ZAP is one implementation, proven on the host first.** `zfs_wenc.cyr`'s fat-ZAP code reads and
    writes through three resolvers, so `tests/zfscodec` runs it over a flat image (gate 37): OpenZFS's own histories of
    `agnoswr/fat` (2 blocks) and `agnostank/dir3000` (17 blocks, the microzap → fat upgrade at the 2,048th name and its
    splits) replayed name by name and compared BYTE FOR BYTE with the blocks OpenZFS wrote (captured, SHA-256-pinned),
    then 1,000 removes, a leaf emptied, re-adds, 255-byte names, updates and lookups against an independent Python model
    (`scripts/check/zfs-fatzap.py`, itself held to the same captures); a shared 12-bit prefix (external table, doubling,
    a mid-grow across ops); TOUPPER normalisation (CDSORTED leaves, case-folded collisions); makefs's 128 KiB blocks;
    u16 arrays (LAYOUTS); an upgrade with a 28-bit collision; a resolver failure; five hostile leaves / headers — each
    `ZZ_CORRUPT` with nothing written outside its blocks (guard pages). 13 cases; mutation-proven (§ h B8).
81. **(B8) Fixture RECIPE 7, and the B9 track's RECIPE 7.** B8 needed a writable dataset holding every fat ZAP the
    writer touches and room for ≈ 600 txgs: `fz` (`agnosfz`, 256 MiB, ashift 9) — `dir3000` (3,000 names), `longn` (60
    names of 255 bytes, 2 leaves), `micro` (20), `uids` (1,100 files of their own uid and gid: fat accounting ZAPs of
    1,102 owners), `uidm` (2,046 userused keys in a full-but-one 128 KiB microzap). Its first builds measured OpenZFS
    UPGRADING `uidm`'s microzap — 1,022 files made 1,024 owners, 2,048 keys: every ZPL dataset's hidden shares
    directory (`zfs_create_share_dir`, uid 0, 512 B) is an owner too — so the recipe makes 1,021. The
    B9 worktree bumped RECIPE to 7 too (its `.snaps` files); the two caches differ by build hash, so neither satisfies the
    other — the merge must make ONE recipe (8) holding both additions and rebuild once. The guest verify now boots 8 rw
    lanes, `VERIFY_MAX_LANES` (q35's root-bus slots): a ninth needs a second verify run or a PCIe bridge. **(The merge,
    2026-10-12, k.107)** Done — and B9's review had meanwhile moved its own track to RECIPE 8 (`edge` / `edgec`, k.103):
    the merged history has ONE RECIPE 7 (B8's `fz` and B9's `snap2`, spread and `.snaps`) and B9's 8 on top, `RECIPE=8`
    under a build hash neither track's cache holds — one cold build.
82. **(B8) A FULL microzap is not grown by an UPDATE.** OpenZFS's `zap_update` locks a microzap `adding`, so an update
    of an EXISTING key in a full microzap still grows it by 512 B (or upgrades it at 128 KiB); agnos updates in place
    (B4's `zap_increment` did already). The result is a valid microzap and any later OpenZFS add does its own growth;
    only the bytes differ from what OpenZFS would have written. Not changed.
83. **(B8) k.57 stays open.** A microzap past 128 KiB is still neither read nor written (the reader's one 128 KiB cache
    slot); agnos never makes one (its upgrade runs at 128 KiB, `zap_micro_max_size`'s default), FreeBSD does only past a
    raised tunable with `large_microzap` active.
84. **(B8) Normalised fat ZAPs: host-proven, unreachable.** The fat code folds ASCII under `U8_TEXTPREP_TOUPPER`, keeps
    CDSORTED leaves and refuses a non-ASCII name (`u8_textprep` not built); gate 37 proves it. On disk every
    normalised / case-insensitive DATASET is still refused at the gate (§ a.3), and a normalised microzap is refused by
    name (its sorted-cd add is not built), so none of it runs on a pool. Lifting the dataset refusal needs the UTF-8
    normalisation tables first.
85. **(B8) A fat DELETE_QUEUE and a fat MOS log ZAP.** Both go through `zfs_wz_op` like a directory, so their adds and
    removes are the code `dir3000` exercises on disk; no fixture holds either fat (a queue grows fat past ≈ 2,000 queued
    objects, the log ZAP past 2,047 unflushed txgs — B10 bounds it far below). **(B8 review — corrected)** A
    pointer-table growth of EITHER kind in a MOS ZAP is refused before anything changes (`zfs_zf_gok` → `ZZ_NOGROW`:
    the maybe-growth is skipped, as OpenZFS skips a failed one; a split that needs a growth fails the commit by name),
    and the write gate refuses a log ZAP whose table is already external or mid-grow (`zfs_wgate_zap`) — a doubling
    would free MOS blocks inside the commit, which its own MOS kill path does not do. As first written this item said
    the growth was refused: only the doubling's FREE was (`zfs_zf_free`), the embedded table's move out was not refused
    at all, and the free's refusal was swallowed when the maybe-grow made it (k.88).
86. **(B8) A fat accounting ADD is unreachable from agnos's ops on an accounted pool.** agnos creates as uid 0 with the
    parent's gid and the default project — owners every ZPL dataset already has (uid 0 the hidden shares directory, the
    gid the parent itself) — and no verb changes an object's ids; so its txgs only UPDATE accounting keys or REMOVE the
    last owner's. A new key comes only from a dnode without the accounting flags (an upgraded pool, k.37: userobj
    accounting enabled, the objset's upgrade incomplete), where the in-memory `zfs_wst_uacct` (ZFS_WRITE_SELFTEST) adds
    keys into a MICROZAP and — **(B8 review, k.90)** — `zfs_wst_uafat` adds 220 into a FAT one through the commit's
    own `zfs_wd_uqapply`: leaf splits, a special dnode's level grown in the objset_phys, within the admission's
    reservation. The fat-ZAP CODE is the directory add's (`b8/` and `dir3000` on disk, gate 37 on the host); the PHASE
    is not, and this item called the directory add its "fat twin" until the review: the real commit's pass-1 re-plan
    around such blocks still runs on no fixture (one with an incomplete userobj upgrade and fat accounting ZAPs is the
    follow-on if one is ever needed).
87. **(B8, found by the gate — fixed in this cut) What the first `fz` runs measured.** (a) **The fat accounting
    reservation** (production): `zfs_wz_urs` reserved a flat 128 KiB indirect per fat ZAP on top of its leaves, so a
    create in `uids` — fat user- AND groupused ZAPs, each with its 128 KiB L1 already dirtied by the op's own leaves —
    asked 1.52 MB of a 1.44 MB arena and was refused (`a create does not fit a txg`). The indirect is now reserved only
    when the split blocks reach past the L1 the header's dirtying holds or past what the levels map (`top > nblkptr ×
    1024^(nlevels−1)`): 128 KiB instead of 384. (b) **The op list's name buffer** (test code, a memory-safety defect): a
    function-local `var n[64]` (64 BYTES) took 255-byte names — the stack frame's `p` overwritten, the paths garbage;
    now `n[256]`. (c) **The op list's name generator** wrote 5 digits while the cursor passed 100,000 — the 21st `a`
    name was an earlier one (EEXIST); 7 digits, and `zfs-fz-ops.py` with it. (d) **The plan's cost**: 45-byte names made
    `b8/` ≈ 370 creates and the lane ran out of its 25 minutes (≈ 2–3 s an op, the console's framebuffer lines); the
    names are 255 bytes now (42 to a leaf): 141 creates, the same shapes, and every split moves 13-chunk names.
    (e) **The fixture** (k.81): `uidm`'s 1,022 files made OpenZFS upgrade the microzap itself. (f) **The oracle**:
    `zfs-sa-check.py` held a created directory to `nlevels` 1 — a directory agnos made and then upgraded has 2
    (`dnode_new_blkid`); and h9 had no SALT rule — a zeroed salt hashes every name consistently, so no structure rule
    sees it and OpenZFS's kernel imports, lists, scrubs and writes through it (negative M5 found the gap on the host
    before any disk run; on disk only zdb's path walk then failed — `zdb -O`, the guest's C-k15; the B8 review
    corrected this note, which said no zdb step sees it): now non-zero, and an upgraded microzap's kept.
88. **(B8 review — fixed in this cut) A pointer-table doubling's SWITCH: no failure swallowed, the old table freed
    through the kill list, an unpredictable salt.** (a) **The swallowed failure** (major): `zfs_zf_grow`'s last call
    wrote `zt_blks_copied = zt_numblks` and only then freed the old table and switched; a free failing there returned
    ZZ_IO — SWALLOWED when the call came from the maybe-grow (`zap_put_leaf_maybe_grow_ptrtbl`, where OpenZFS ignores
    the grow's error and where `fz` finishes its 4 → 8 doubling), so the op answered 0 and its txg would commit
    `zt_blks_copied == zt_numblks` with `zt_nextblk` set: a header OpenZFS never writes, from which its next
    `zap_table_grow` copies block `zt_blk + zt_numblks` — a LEAF — over blocks past the new table, losing entries; and
    a free failing at block k > 0 left blocks 0..k − 1 of the table still in use zeroed (holes at the commit). Its
    triggers: `zfs_zf_free`'s MOS refusal (k.85) and an unreadable old block; the review reproduced it on the host
    (`SWALLOWED: op 248 returned 0 after 1 failed free(s) … copied 4`). Now the last call FREES the old table first —
    all of it or a failure — and only then writes zt_blk, zt_numblks, zt_shift, zt_nextblk and zt_blks_copied together
    (OpenZFS's own end state; a failure leaves the doubling where it was, the last block copied again by the next call);
    the maybe-grow ignores only a refusal that changed nothing (ZZ_NOSPC, the new ZZ_NOGROW) and any other failure is
    the op's, its txg dropped; a header whose zt_blks_copied is past its table answers ZZ_CORRUPT. (b) **The MOS**: a
    growth of either kind refused before anything changes, the gate's external / mid-grow refusal (k.85). (c) **The
    switch's cost** (minor): every old table block was DIRTIED zeroed — 16 KiB of the metadata arena a block — so the
    switch of a 64-block table (2^17 → 2^18 entries) asked 1 MiB on top of the op and a 128-block one more than the
    arena: the ZAP stayed mid-grow and every later add to it was refused (`does not fit a txg`), for agnos, for good.
    Now a block already dirty in the txg is zeroed in place (it holds a slot already) and every other one's BP is
    killed through B5's kill list (`zfs_zf_kill` → `zfs_wf_kill`: judged killable, a hole of birth T in the dirtied
    L1, the kill inside the commit — `dmu_free_range` / `free_blocks`' result; no slot, no read). The switch is now
    bounded by the kill list and the extent list (`zfs_wf_xfit`: ≈ 3,300 killed DVAs a txg past the commit's own — ≈
    1,600 two-copy metadata blocks): every switch up to 1,024 old blocks (2^21 → 2^22 entries, ≈ 10^8 entries by
    size) fits; a larger one is refused by name (`txg full: a pointer table's old blocks`) and the ZAP stays mid-grow
    for agnos (OpenZFS's next writable import finishes it). The EXPAND path still does a whole doubling in one op
    (`zap_expand_leaf` loops `zap_grow_ptrtbl` until the shift grows, as OpenZFS does) — its 2 × N new blocks dirtied
    in one txg fit through N = 32 (2^16 → 2^17); from 64 that op is refused and the maybe-steps of other adds (one
    block an op) carry the doubling to its switch. (d) **The salt** (hostile names): production's `zfs_wc_salt` was
    the op list's derivation from the objset, the object and the txg — computable, so a writer could choose names
    whose hashes share a prefix (79 drove `fz`'s fresh `b8/` to a doubling table): production's salt is now RDRAND
    (the TSC where it answers 0) mixed with those, unpredictable as OpenZFS's address-derived one; the derivation and
    the knob `zfs_wc_fsalt` stay in the `ZFS_WRITE_OPS` build (byte-reproducible images, k.75). Proofs: gate 37's two
    new cases (§ i.1), mutation-proven (N1–N3, § h); on disk `fz`'s switch through the kill list (N6, § h).
89. **(B8 review — fixed in this cut) An accounting ZAP's shape is judged at ADMISSION.** `zfs_wz_ucap` marked any
    accounting ZAP whose block 0 is not a microzap 0xFFFF ("fat") unvalidated, and `zfs_wz_urs` ran
    `cap == 0xFFFF && zfs_wz_kind() == 1`: a `zfs_wz_kind` refusal (`zap_flags`, a block size not a power of two, a
    block 0 neither micro nor fat) fell through to "nothing to reserve" and the commit's `zap_increment`
    (`zfs_wd_uqapply` → `zfs_wz_get`) refused it INSIDE the commit — the pool read-only until reboot, on the first op
    of every boot (the B4 review's rule: nothing the commit dirties may refuse it); a NORMALISED accounting microzap
    the same (`zfs_wz_op` refuses its add). Now `zfs_wds_open` refuses both by name when it opens the dataset, and
    `zfs_wz_urs` returns the kind's refusal. OpenZFS writes neither; a hostile or foreign image is what reaches it.
    Proof: the boot's `uafat` sets `zap_flags` on a fat accounting header and requires the admission to refuse it
    (mutant N4, the fall-through restored, red there: `uafat: a fat accounting ZAP with zap_flags admitted`).
90. **(B8 review — fixed in this cut) The commit's fat accounting ADD in memory; stale words and test state out of
    production.** (a) k.86 called the directory add the fat accounting add's twin: the ZAP code is shared, the phase is
    not — the commit's own adds (inside `zfs_wd_phase`, a new leaf past `dn_maxblkid` growing a LEVEL of a special
    dnode in the objset_phys, inside the `zfs_wz_mres` reservation) ran on no gate. `zfs_wst_uafat` (ZFS_WRITE_SELFTEST,
    every rw lane's boot; zfs-smoke requires its line) does it in memory on the first writable accounted dataset: its
    user microzap upgraded as admission does, the admission's fat step (`zfs_wz_urs`: the shape — k.89 —, the external
    table, the reservation), 220 new keys whose hashes share their top 8 bits applied by `zfs_wd_uqapply` — the leaf
    splitting to prefix 8, 1 → 10 leaves, the special dnode's nlevels 1 → 2 — every new key and every T − 1 entry
    read back, the bytes dirtied within the reservation (278,528 of 7,340,032 B), every delta reversed and read back
    again (mutant N5, the fat reservation dropped, red there). Not covered still: the REAL commit's pass-1 re-plan
    around such blocks (k.86). (b) Stale words: the mkdir / rmdir / unlink / symlink arms' "a fat-ZAP parent (B8)", "a
    name of 50+ bytes", "B8's LAYOUTS add: refused"; the operand map's slot 8 (B7's microzap entry, unused since B8);
    `zfs_wu_open`'s "ONE microzap block"; the B6 / B7 section titles; the `fz` lane's "added to". (c) Test state compiled
    into production: the op list's `zfs_wc_fsalt` (read on every directory create), `zfs_wc_lnew` and `zfs_wz_up` —
    now in the `ZFS_WRITE_OPS` build only; production calls empty notes (`zfs_wc_lnote`, `zfs_wz_upn`).
91. **(B9) The deadlist budget's one-entry reserve.** A deadlist append dirties the entry's bpobj block — 128 KiB of
    the metadata arena however few BPs it takes — so k.70's rule is per ENTRY: each admission counts its snapshot-owned
    kills for their entry (`zfs_wdl_q`) and keeps ONE entry's first append (`ZWK_DLMAT` 144 KiB + a ZAP block) in
    reserve while a snapshotted dataset is open, for an entry the next walk reaches before any kill counted it (an
    indirect born in another snapshot interval than its children). A walk that reaches TWO new entries between two
    admissions near a full arena is refused by name (`txg full: a snapshot-owned block's deadlist blocks (commit first)`
    — the op fails, never the commit); a kill-list entry that does not fit ends the txg (a truncation goes on in the
    next, an unlink takes the lazy path). Exact alternative: predict a walk's entries at its admission (the path's BPs,
    dirty-first) — not built; no fixture walk reaches two new entries (agnostank's kills reach entry 1 only, snap2's
    two arrive in its FIRST txg with an empty arena). **⛔ SUPERSEDED by the B9 review (k.100):** the reserve is gone —
    one entry's was outrun by a walk reaching two, refused "commit first" in an EMPTY txg the same way on every retry,
    and a write's refusal discarded the bytes already placed in its txg. Every multi-txg op now PREDICTS a block's
    whole path (`zfs_wdl_path`) — the "exact alternative" above, built.
92. **(B9) Livelists: refused; the fixture's clone has none.** A clone's livelist (`com.delphix:livelist` on its
    zapified dsl_dir) takes an ALLOC record of every block born after the origin and a FREE record (bp_freed,
    `bpo_num_freed`, a 56-byte header) of every kill, flushed per txg into the last sub-livelist — a new key at
    `zfs_livelist_max_entries` — and condensed by a background thread (`dsl_flush_pending_livelist`,
    `dsl_livelist_try_condense`); zdb checks it ("livelist is incorrect"). Not built: a clone WITH one is refused at the
    gate (`clone with a livelist`). EVIDENCE: RECIPE 3 created `wr/clone` "with its own livelist", but OpenZFS dropped it
    at the clone's first sync after `own.txt` — 71 % shared ≤ `zfs_livelist_min_percent_shared` 75
    (`dsl_livelist_should_disable`, `dsl_dataset.c:2150-2163`); the RECIPE 7 image has no livelist key on it and the
    `livelist` refcount is 0. So the CLONE path B9 builds runs on disk (`keep.txt`, the origin's: deadlisted with
    neither unique nor HEAD → SNAP; `own.txt`, the clone's: freed), and the gate's refusal is unexercised. (The RECIPE 3
    comment in `zfs-fixture-guest.sh` said "with its own livelist" — wrong since the first build; corrected with RECIPE 7,
    which re-keyed the fixture anyway: a comment edit in the BUILD half re-keys it.)
93. **(B9 → B8) Two refusals that are fat-ZAP edits.** (1) The `empty_bpobj`'s LAST reference: `bpobj_decr_empty` at
    0 removes `empty_bpobj` from the MOS directory (a fat ZAP on every fixture) and frees the object — refused by name;
    unreachable while any snapshot-free head exists (each names it once: refcount 21 on main, 16 on wr). (2) A
    materialisation in a FAT deadlist ZAP (a head with ~2,000+ snapshots outgrows the microzap): the value update in a
    fat leaf — refused by name; reading a fat deadlist works (the reader's `zfs_zap_next`). Both are B8's edits.
94. **(B9) Built, not exercised: the old-format deadlist.** A pool from before SPA_VERSION_DEADLISTS (26) keeps a head's
    deadlist as ONE bpobj until OpenZFS regenerates it (`dsl_deadlist_regenerate` at its next snapshot); agnos appends
    to it directly (no header). No fixture pool is upgraded from v < 26; h6 / h7 check its bpobj but skip its space
    identities (no intervals). An upgraded-pool recipe would cover it.
95. **(B9) What proves a deadlist correct is OpenZFS consuming it.** `zdb -bcc` (A3) counts deadlisted blocks as
    referenced through the snapshot, so it sees a block freed instead (space map vs traversal), but a block deadlisted in
    the WRONG entry or with wrong sums only shows when OpenZFS uses the deadlist: `zfs destroy -n`'s reclaim
    (B-snapacct), the snapshot destroys of phase D (they free by entry, `dsl_deadlist_move_bpobj` / the next's
    `bpobj_iterate` over the range) followed by D-scrub and D-zdb, and the `written` property (B-acct identity 6 and the
    clone identity). h7's recount checks the entry ranges and the sums on the image before any guest.
96. **(B9, defect found and fixed on the way) `zfs_wfb_add`'s PSIZE of an embedded BP.** B7's append took PSIZE from
    the raw `blk_prop` bits even for an embedded BP (whose psize field is the payload length, BP_GET_PSIZE 0) — dormant
    while only gang / dedup / cloned blocks reached it (never embedded: `zfs_wd_kill` accounted those itself), wrong as
    soon as a DEADLIST takes embedded BPs (OpenZFS's `bpobj_enqueue` stores them and counts no PSIZE). The append is now
    `zfs_wbpo_add(obj, bp, dsize, psize, ucsize)` with the caller's sums (0 PSIZE for an embedded BP).
97. **(B9, defect found and fixed: k.69's luck came back) wr's room is now the op list's, not the build's.** RECIPE 7's
    first build left a space map on 3 of `wr`'s 15 metaslabs (RECIPE 6's: 5); agnos allocates in no other (k.46), so
    the room B5 / B7's gates are tuned to shrank by 32 MiB and B7's forced-deferral truncation ran out of it at its third
    txg (`trunc FAIL no space: the metaslabs with a space map are full`; B9's own wr ops cost 2.36 MB of it). A
    BOUNDED spread is not possible — 64 MiB written into a scratch dataset touched all 15 (OpenZFS's segment weight sends
    each next chunk to an untouched metaslab; the build's promise `5..7` refused it) — so RECIPE 7 spreads wr to every
    metaslab (as RECIPE 5 does main) and the op list caps agnos's allocation on `agnoswr` at the first 5 metaslabs with
    a space map (`zfs_wt_msmax`, a `ZFS_WRITE_OPS`-only knob like `zfs_wc_tight` / `zfs_wh_force`; `zfs_wpick` and
    `zfs_wphys` honour it, `zfs-room-check.py` takes it as MSMAX): the room is RECIPE 6's again, whatever a build does.
    The `wr@u0` forge (`zero-u`) then has every metaslab to choose from; the burst still runs the 5 out of space.
98. **(B9) What the oracle cannot see: an over-counted `empty_bpobj` refcount.** zdb never counts this feature's
    consumers (`zdb.c:8705-8706`: it stays at UINT64_MAX, skipped), and OpenZFS tolerates an over-count — the refcount
    simply never reaches 0, the `empty_bpobj` object stays (no leak: the MOS directory names it). An UNDER-count is a
    `VERIFY` panic at a later `bpobj_decr_empty`. So the materialisation's decrement is held by h7's recount (refcount ==
    the deadlist entries naming it) and by the op list's read-back (`zfs_wt_rb`: the refcount read back == the published
    one) — negative N2 / N2b below show which goes red.
99. **(B9 review, finding 1) A truncation's deadlists are resolved before its first txg.** B9 let the scan
    (`zfs_wf_okscan`) pass every snapshot-owned BP — `zfs_wd_okbp` answers 0 for them — while everything that can still
    refuse one ran at the first count of its entry, in whichever txg first reached it: loading the deadlist (more than
    `ZWK_MAXDLE` keys, a bpobj or an entry unreadable, a birth before every entry), the `empty_bpobj`'s last reference, a
    fat deadlist (k.100), and a kill whose entry no fresh txg holds (`truncation: not one block's free fits a txg`). A
    truncation whose upper range is branch A and lower range a snapshot's met them after earlier txgs had committed —
    `-1`, SIZE unchanged, the freed range reading zeros. Now the scan resolves every snapshot-owned BP's entry
    (`zfs_wdl_ent`, its kills counted in the entry's +64) and `zfs_wf_dlscan` judges every materialisation the whole
    truncation will need (the last reference over all of them, the fat check), so each of those refusals happens while
    nothing is freed; and it BOUNDS a later txg's first step — the blocks `zfs_wf_begin` dirties, (nlevels − 1)
    indirects, each snapshotted dataset's 32 KiB, the entries of accounting ZAPs that a later admission counts again
    unless txg 1 rewrote them, and the nlevels entries whose first appends cost most (`zfs_wf_dlx`: `zfs_wbpo_mx`, the
    most `zfs_wbpo_meta` can reach over every committed count the truncation can leave the entry at, + a
    materialisation) — refusing a truncation that needs a second txg while that bound exceeds the arena at the END of
    txg 1, before it commits (`truncation: a later txg might not hold one block's deadlist appends`). In every txg the
    walk's own dirtying is predicted exactly (`zfs_wdl_path`): the last L1's path (B9 dirtied it unchecked), each new
    indirect, and the boundary block — which waits for the next txg when it does not fit after this one freed blocks
    (it was dirtied unchecked too). The first L1's `dnode_dirty_l1` at the end reaches no entry (every non-hole block on
    its path inside the range was dirtied by the walk). The bound is conservative: a multi-txg truncation of a deep
    file whose remaining kills reach large entries can be refused where it would have fitted — by name, nothing
    changed. ON DISK (RECIPE 8): `edgec/t.bin`, 16 blocks, the upper 8 rewritten after the clone's snapshot (branch A),
    the lower 8 its origin's, truncated four kill-list entries a txg (`zfs_wt_ftight`, `ZFS_WRITE_OPS` only) under a
    deadlist table capped at 3 entries (`zfs_wt_dlmax`; edgec has 6 keys): `trunc ok refused (deadlist entries in one
    txg over 3)`, **txgs=0** — B9 as built would have freed blocks 15..8 in two committed txgs and been refused in the
    third; then uncapped: `-> 0 txgs=4`, the last two txgs deadlisting.
100. **(B9 review, findings 2 and 3) Every deadlist load and refusal is an op's; the reserve is gone.** (a) The commit
    COWs the user / group / project accounting microzaps (`zfs_wd_uqapply`, with `zfs_wc_in` set, so `zfs_wblk` counted
    nothing): a txg whose only snapshot-owned kills they were loaded the head's deadlist INSIDE the commit — B9's own
    gate showed it (wr txg 177, `snap2/a.bin`'s truncation: `dl=3 dlu=0`, snap2's three embedded accounting microzaps)
    — and any refusal there was `zfs_wfail_now`, the pool read-only until reboot. `zfs_wds_open` now counts their
    predecessors as `zfs_wos_os` counts the objset's. (b) `zfs_wdl_mat`'s two refusals (the `empty_bpobj`'s last
    reference, a fat deadlist) ran only inside the commit; `zfs_wdl_q` now judges both when it first counts an entry
    still naming the `empty_bpobj` (`zfs_wdl_matok` / `zfs_wdl_fatok`) — at the admission — and `zfs_wdl_mat` keeps
    them as an unreached backstop. PROOF: the plan line's new `dlx=` counts the appends whose entry no admission counted;
    the smoke holds it 0 on every txg of every lane (that truncation now: `txg 211 … dl=3 dlu=0 mat=0 dlx=0` — its three appends counted when `snap2` was opened). The fat refusal runs on disk
    under the op list's knob `zfs_wt_dlfat` (`edge/p.bin`: `write ok refused (deadlist is a fat ZAP (B8))` at its
    admission, txgs=0, the pool rw — the next op writes the same block). (c) The BUDGET: B9 kept one entry's first
    append in reserve (`ZWK_DLMAT`); a walk reaching two new entries outran it and was refused `txg full: … (commit
    first)` — in an EMPTY txg, the same way on every retry — and in `zfs_wf_write1` that refusal was a hard `-1` that
    dropped the bytes already placed in the txg. Now a write or a truncation PREDICTS each block's whole path before
    dirtying it (`zfs_wdl_path`: dirty-first down the path, each predecessor `zfs_wblk` will count counted, the
    deadlist bytes measured, then uncounted): a block that does not fit ends the txg before anything of it is dirtied
    and the next txg goes on with it; only a block first in its txg is refused, by an honest name (`one block does not
    fit a txg: the snapshot intervals its path reaches, N`); a `zfs_wblk` size refusal with bytes placed ends the txg
    (`zfs_wdl_full`, a backstop); every other verb starts an empty txg (policy A), so its refusal now reads `the op
    does not fit a txg: a snapshot-owned block's deadlist blocks`. No reserve anywhere (`zfs_wdl_meta()` has no
    argument; `ZWK_DLMAT` is gone). What remains is the ARENA: each counted entry dirties at least one 128 KiB bpobj
    block (one + an indirect past 3,072 BPs), and a fresh txg's path already holds ≈ 0.8 MB (2 levels: the objset, the
    meta-dnode's five 128 KiB indirects, the dnode block, the file's L1) / 0.94 MB (3 levels) of the 1.375 MB an op may
    use. ON DISK (RECIPE 8 `edge`, @e1..@e4, nothing written after @e4, so every head entry still names the
    `empty_bpobj`): `p.bin`'s block 0 (2 levels) reaches four intervals — L0 → entry 1, L1 → @e1, the dnode block →
    @e2, the chain → @e3 — written in ONE txg, four entries materialised (`dl=9 … mat=4 dlx=0`); `q.bin`'s (3 levels)
    the same four — refused by name, nothing written. **Open for the operator:** a larger metadata arena (with
    `ZWK_MDRES`, the commit's share of the room, decoupled from it — k.46) or a slot of a bpobj's tail block sized by
    its used bytes would let deeper paths through; neither is built.
101. **(B9 review, finding 7) Branch B's precise condition.** The gate and `zfs_wdl_open` skipped every deadlist read
    when `ds_prev_snap_obj` was `$ORIGIN@$ORIGIN`, while `zfs_wd_kill`'s branch B still applied to blocks born at or
    before `ds_prev_snap_txg`. The finding's premise — an upgraded pool carries the upgrade txg there — does not hold:
    OpenZFS creates `$ORIGIN@$ORIGIN` with `ds_creation_txg` 1 (`dsl_dataset_snapshot_sync_impl`: "The origin's
    ds_creation_txg has to be < TXG_INITIAL", `dsl_dataset.c:1752-1758`) and both `dsl_dataset_create_sync_dd` and
    `upgrade_clones_cb` (`dsl_pool.c:1069-1071`) give every head attached to it `ds_prev_snap_txg` = that 1, and no
    block is born before TXG_INITIAL (4, `txg.h`) — measured on the RECIPE 8 `wr` image: 20 datasets attached to it,
    every `ds_prev_snap_txg` 1 (`scratch origin.py`). So the branch was unreachable — but only by an argument the code
    did not state. Both now test the condition itself, `ds_prev_snap_txg ≥ TXG_INITIAL` (`ZWK_TXGI`), whatever object
