    `ds_prev_snap_obj` names: a dataset whose kills CAN be snapshot-owned has its deadlist type, format, block size and
    budget read; one whose cannot is never charged for it.
102. **(B9 review, finding 4) `zfs_wu_free`'s rollback restores the deadlist counts.** An object's free that is too big
    for the txg rolled back the kill list (`zfs_wfr_n`, `zfs_wfr_x`, B7's `zfs_wfb_q`) but not the per-entry counts
    `zfs_wu_kbp` had added (`zfs_wdl_q` chk 2) for the snapshot-owned kills it dropped, so the lazy fallback and every
    later admission of the txg were judged against appends that never happen — and could be refused `txg full` for
    them. `ZWA_DLE` +72 saves each entry's count at the free's start; the rollback restores them (an entry the free
    itself loaded goes back to 0). Not exercised on disk (no lazy unlink in a snapshotted dataset — the fixture's lazy
    object lives in `agnostank/off`); code-trace evidence only.
103. **(B9 review, finding 5) The boundary births — fixture RECIPE 8.** No fixture block a writer killed was born AT a
    boundary B9 compares against, so an off-by-one in any of the four comparisons passed every lane (the review's scan:
    0 BPs at any boundary on wr / main). RECIPE 8's `edge` (recordsize 4K, @e1..@e4) and its clone `edgec` (@c1) write a
    file and snapshot it in ONE txg — no `zpool sync` between, `vfs.zfs.txg.timeout` raised for the step: the snapshot
    is a sync task in the open txg, run after the dataset's blocks (`dsl_sync_task_common`, `dsl_pool_sync`) — and
    `oracle_wr_objects` holds each L0's birth to its snapshot's `createtxg`, so a txg boundary between the two fails the
    build (measured: @e1..@e4 = 71 / 75 / 79 / 83, @c1 = 88; `edge/e1.bin` 71, `e3.bin` 79, `e4.bin` 83, `edgec/e4.bin`
    83, `c1.bin` 88). The op list kills: `edge/e4.bin` (birth == `ds_prev_snap_txg`: branch B), `edge/e3.bin` (== the key
    79: the entry BEFORE it, 75; == @e4's `ds_prev_snap_txg`: no prev-unique), `edgec/e4.bin` (== its key 83 → entry
    79, == @c1's `ds_prev_snap_txg`, == `dd_origin_txg` 83: no HEAD → SNAP), `edgec/c1.bin` (== `ds_prev_snap_txg`). Note
    a snapshot RESETS the head's deadlist (`dsl_deadlist_clone` gives every key a fresh empty bpobj), so with nothing
    written after @e4 every one of edge's entries still names the `empty_bpobj` — the four-interval write materialises
    all four. Building it found two parser slips of the recipe's own (zdb's compact `B=` births, the objset BP in the
    header) and the oracle defect k.106. NEGATIVES (scratch mutants of `zfs_write.cyr` via cyim, the `ZFS_WRITE_OPS`
    kernel, lane `wr`, `ZWS_SPACE=advisory ZWS_CONTINUE=1`, the source restored byte-identical — sha256 `b516645c…` —
    after each; every one was GREEN on every lane before RECIPE 8): **Ma** branch B for `birth < ds_prev_snap_txg` only
    (the A/B split) → the op list (`dl` / `mat` counts, the `mat=4` txg, edge's deadlist line), h6 (`snapshot 83: a block
    a snapshot still owns is FREE`), h7 (edge / edgec `ds_unique_bytes` off by −61,440 / −4,096 — the chain born at @e4's
    txg too), guest H, A3 / A4 / C-zdb (zdb SIGABRT, `zio_claim`), B-acct; **Mb** the key's own entry for a birth equal
    to it (`>=` for `>` in `zfs_wdl_ent`) → h7 (`deadlist 84 entry 53 holds a BP born 83 (its entry is (83, 88])`, the
    unique off by −4,096), guest H, B-acct, B-snapacct — and then OpenZFS PANICKED processing the pool (`ms_unflushed_frees:
    adding segment … overlapping with existing one`: the misfiled BP freed twice); **Mc** prev-unique for a birth equal
    to the previous snapshot's `ds_prev_snap_txg` → h7 (`snapshot 80 ds_unique_bytes 73728 != its next's deadlist from
    txg 79 on: 61440`, edgec's +4,096), guest H, B-snapacct (`agnoswr/edge@e4 used 73728 != zfs destroy -n's reclaim
    61440`); **Md** HEAD → SNAP for a birth equal to `dd_origin_txg` → h7 (`dd_used_breakdown[SNAP] 90112 != … 86016`),
    guest H, B-acct (k.106's `usedbydataset 1183744 != written@agnoswr/edge@e4 1187840`), B-snapacct.
104. **(B9 review, finding 6) The 256-key limit.** `ZWA_DLE` holds EVERY key of each deadlist a txg loads
    (`ZWK_MAXDLE` 256, 32 KiB of the writer area since the review): a head or clone with 256 or more snapshot intervals
    before it is refused for every op whose txg loads its deadlist (`deadlist entries in one txg over 256`) — a
    truncation at its scan (k.99). A microzap deadlist holds ≈ 2,047 keys before OpenZFS makes it fat, so this limit
    binds first; a FAT deadlist with fewer than 256 keys exists only after pruning (OpenZFS never shrinks a fat ZAP —
    § a.3). Lifting it means keeping only the keys a txg reaches (a sorted key array + a table of the entries actually
    counted) — not built. Its refusal ran on disk under `zfs_wt_dlmax` (k.99).
105. **(B9 review, finding 8) The oracle's birth.** h6 (`owned_allocated`) and h7 (`dl_check`, `born_after`) compared
    LOGICAL births (`zfs-damage.py` `BP.birth`, word 10); OpenZFS's kill, its deadlist insert and its traversal use
    `BP_GET_BIRTH` — the physical birth under `blk_prop2`'s rewrite flag (`com.truenas:physical_rewrite` is on the
    writer's list), as the writer's `zfs_bp_birth` does. `BP.gbirth` is `BP_GET_BIRTH` now and the three use it;
    `dl_check` also caps the newest entry at its owner's `ds_prev_snap_txg` (it was open-ended). Validated on every
    pristine RECIPE 8 image and every rw lane (§ i.2). No fixture block carries the rewrite flag — a `zfs rewrite -P`
    step would exercise it (not added).
106. **(B9 review, defect found and fixed) The guest's clone identity.** B9's B-acct held a clone WITH snapshots of its
    own to `referenced − usedbydataset` UNCHANGED from the pristine pool — false as soon as such a clone kills one of
    its origin's blocks: referenced drops, HEAD does not (`dsl_dataset.c:358-361`). Never exercised (no fixture clone had
    a snapshot); RECIPE 8's `edgec` made it red on a CORRECT pool: 204,800 → 167,936, exactly the 36,864 B of origin
    blocks the op list killed (`e4.bin` 4 KiB + `t.bin`'s eight 4 KiB). A clone's HEAD is the bytes born after its
    origin it still references, and OpenZFS derives exactly that as `written@<origin>` (referenced − the origin's
    referenced + the deadlists back to it, `dsl_dataset_space_written_impl`, `dsl_dataset.c:4744-4810`): B-acct now
    captures it for every clone (`wo` lines, verify half — the fixture is not re-keyed) and holds `usedbydataset ==
    written@<origin>` (the no-snapshot rule `usedbydataset == written` is its special case). Re-verified: wr PASS 36/36.
107. **(The merge, 2026-10-12) ROOM, B8 and B9 — three tracks from one seed, merged into one tree.** Each track was a
    copy of the same base (B0–B7, the BSP move, gate 38; fixture RECIPE 6) and merged by `git merge-file` against it,
    ROOM first, then B8, then B9; every conflict resolved by hand with both tracks' intent kept. **Numbering:** the three
    tracks each numbered § k from 74 — ROOM's stays k.74, B8's k.74–k.89 are **k.75–k.90**, B9's k.74–k.89 **k.91–k.106**,
    every cross-reference with them (this spec, `zfs_write.cyr`'s comments, the smokes, the fixture scripts,
    `zfs.md`, the ABI). Both tracks also wrote `zfs.md` "§ 14": B8's fat ZAPs keep it, B9's snapshotted
    datasets are **§ 15**; and both added ring-3 clauses 148–149: B8 keeps them, B9's 148–153 are **150–155**
    (`zfsx.cyr`, the harness's WHY table, the ABI); `RING3_TXGS` 21 + 5 + 9 = **35**, the refusal list B8's **28**. The
    fixture: both tracks bumped RECIPE 6 → 7 (B8's `fz`; B9's `snap2`, wr's spread, `.snaps`) and B9's review went on to
    8 (`edge` / `edgec`) — one RECIPE 7 holding both, B9's 8 on top, `RECIPE=8` (k.81). § j's "standing rule" note,
    written by ROOM and by B8, is one note. **Found by the merge, fixed in it** — interactions neither track could see,
    since B8 had no snapshotted dataset to write and B9 no fat accounting ZAP:
    (a) **The accounting ZAPs' deadlist count covered one BP.** B9's `zfs_wds_open` counted the commit's COW of an
        accounting microzap through its ONE block pointer (`sd + 64`); B8 admits FAT accounting ZAPs — up to three
        top-level BPs, indirects under them. The count now runs on EVERY top-level BP, inside B8's `zfs_wd_okbp` loop (a
        fat ZAP's deeper blocks are counted by `zfs_wblk` when `zfs_wz_urs` dirties them at admission).
    (b) **B8's commit reserve fell out of B9's sums.** B8 added `zfs_wz_mres` (the fat accounting splits the commit will
        dirty) to `zfs_wf_mfit`; B9 added `zfs_wdl_meta()` to the same function — both are in it now — but B9 also
        REPLACED `zfs_wf_mfit` at four sites with explicit sums (the write's block, the walk's indirect, the last L1, the
        boundary block) and wrote a fifth (`zfs_wdl_q`'s fit): each now carries `zfs_wz_mres`, as `zfs_wf_mfit` did.
    (c) **A fat accounting ZAP's growth past its first indirect, in a snapshotted dataset.** `zfs_wz_urs` reserves the
        indirects a new leaf needs beyond the L1 the header's dirtying holds; inside the commit such an L1 — one no
        admission dirtied — is COWed and, a snapshot's, deadlisted UNCOUNTED (B9's `dlx=`), its deadlist loaded and
        judged in the commit. Refused by name at admission now (`a user accounting ZAP growing past its first indirect
        block in a snapshotted dataset …`), nothing changed. Reachable only past ≈ 1,024 leaves (an embedded table's
        limit, ≈ 10^5 owners); no fixture reaches it. Open: dirty that L1 at admission instead (`zfs_wblk` at level 1
        counts it as every other admission's block) — not built.
    (d) **B9's later-txg bound for a truncation saw one BP per accounting ZAP.** `zfs_wf_dlscan` bounds the entries a later
        txg's admission reaches through the accounting ZAPs by `sd + 64`'s entry; a FAT accounting ZAP's leaves and
        indirects, which every later admission's `zfs_wz_urs` dirties, reach entries it does not bound. A multi-txg
        truncation in a snapshotted dataset whose accounting ZAP is fat now sets the bound (`zfs_wf_tb`): refused at
        txg 1 by name (`truncation: a later txg might not hold one block's deadlist appends`), nothing freed. No fixture.
    (e) **Found by the merged ring-3 gate: the host audit asked a ring for more txgs than it holds.** `zfs-ring3-test.py`
        passes `--min-txgs RING3_TXGS`; at 35 (B8's 26 + B9's 9 more) the run outgrows the main pool's ring — 32 slots
        of 4 KiB at ashift 12 — so h3 saw txgs 290..321 and failed `only 32 new uberblock txg(s), the lane must have
        committed at least 35` on a correct pool. `zfs-image-audit.py` now requires min(N, slots), as `--final-txg`
        already did (it holds each of the last min(T − pristine, slots) slots to its txg, so nothing is lost).
    (f) **Found by the merged write gate: two tracks, one free tail.** B8's op list put the names of `dir3000`'s
        smallest leaf in "the writer area's free tail, [0x7F2000, 0x800000)" — where B9 placed `ZWA_DSB` and `ZWA_DLE`.
        The first unlink's `zfs_wds_open` → `zfs_wdl_open` zeroed the snapshot side over the list: on `fz` 120 of the
        leaf's 187 unlinks became `unlink /agnosfz/dir3000/` (`unlink of a directory (EPERM: rmdir)`) and two named
        garbage (`no such name`) — `done … fails=122`, the walk 122 extra files. The list has its own region now
        (`ZWA_TNL`, 16 KiB at `0x7FB200`, bounded: a longer list is the op's failure), 3.5 KiB left free.
    (g) **Found by the merged write gate: an exact count that followed a fixture build's luck.** wr / wr@u0 failed
        `--mat 6` (7 materialised) and edgec's `6 entries (3 naming empty_bpobj)` (2) on a CORRECT pool (`dlx=0` every
        txg; h6 / h7 green): object numbers come from per-CPU chunks, and B9's build had placed edgec's `e4.bin` and
        `c1.bin` in dnode blocks `t.bin` / `after.txt` rewrote after `@c1` — the merge's cold build did not, so edgec's
        two writes also deadlisted a dnode block born at `@c1`, materialising its entry. The recipe now `touch`es both
        after `@c1` (`build_wr_edge`): their dnode blocks move on every build, their DATA blocks keep the boundary
        births (`oracle_wr_objects`) — B9's measured counts, by construction instead of by luck.
    Open: k.93's two refusals (the `empty_bpobj`'s last reference, a materialisation in a FAT deadlist ZAP) are fat-ZAP
    edits B8's code could now make — not wired, still refused by name; (c) and (d) have no fixture.
108. **(B10) A destroyed log's deferral: GHOSTS — § c.2's SM-append rule was wrong.** § c.2's review fix rebuilt the
     deferred bitmap from disk as "every FREE of a log with txg ≥ T0 − 2, and every FREE in a metaslab-SM append whose
     debug txg is ≥ T0 + 1 (agnos's own B10 flushes, which outlive the logs B10 destroys)", and B1 built that rule into
     `zfs_wsm_ent`. It cannot hold once logs are destroyed: a flush appends the NET of a metaslab's unflushed changes,
     and a range agnos allocated in one txg and freed in a later one (every rewritten MOS block, every txg) nets to zero
     — it is in no append — so after its logs are gone a reload forgets it was freed this boot, and the allocator may
     hand it out while T − 1 / T − 2 still reference it (agnos's boot-long rule, and A4's rewinds, need it). And the
     rule defers too much the other way: an append's FREE of a range freed before T0 − 2 (an old OpenZFS log's) becomes
     deferred at a reload while the resident bitmap had it allocatable — the read-back's "a reload counts other
     deferred bytes". **Built:** a destroyed log with txg ≥ T0 − 2 becomes a GHOST (`ZWA_GHO`, `zfs_wl_kill_logs`: its
     txg, its FREE bytes, its dnode's first 216 bytes — header, the one blkptr, the space_map_phys_t's object / length /
     alloc), which `zfs_ms_load` walks for deferral only (never applied) and `zfs_wscan_logs` sums into `zfs_w_defer`.
     Its blocks are freed in the destroying txg and, like every free of the boot, never reused, so they still hold its
     entries for the rest of the boot. The SM-append rule is GONE (kernel and host replay alike): the deferred set every
     reload rebuilds is exactly the pre-B10 one, whatever B10 flushed or destroyed. Cost: 256 KiB of writer area (≤ 1,024
     ghosts; a txg whose logs the table could not keep skips its hygiene, `skip=2` — ⚠ corrected by the B10 review, k.122:
     not a per-txg event but a cliff ≈ 1,010 txgs into a boot, after which hygiene stops for good and the commits are
     refused at `ZWK_MAXLOG`); every reload reads every ghost, as it read every log of the boot before B10. **Bite B11's TXG_DEFER_SIZE release (k.40 / k.64) retires them**: a
     ghost older than T − 2 then drops out with the deferral it carries — built at the merge, k.133 (a).
109. **(B10) The flush's histogram.** OpenZFS's `metaslab_flush` leaves the histogram alone; its `metaslab_sync`
     rewrites a LOADED metaslab's from its range trees at every sync (§ e.4's B7 note) — the true state after T, with
     T's frees in. A flushed metaslab here gets the EXACT histogram of its true state BEFORE T (`zfs_wh_exact` on the
     allocated bitmap at the flush) — which is precisely what the space map ALONE now replays to: the read-only load
     (zdb `-mmmm`, the logs not replayed) weighs it equal, never below — and never above the writable load (SM + log T),
     whose free space is that state with T's frees added and T's allocations taken out. For the latter, k.68 judges T's
     allocations against this new stored histogram: `zfs_wh_need` reads it DIRTY-FIRST since B10 (a flushed metaslab's
     space map dnode is dirty), and lowers it where T took a weight below it (`zfs_wh_fill`). The negative N5 (bucket 31
     raised) is red on the host replay's rule and the guest's A3m.
110. **(B10, k.27 — DECIDED and built) A MOS gang predecessor is freed in its txg, tree and all.** Read from OpenZFS
     2.4.2: `dsl_free` → `zio_free` puts a gang (or dedup, or BRT) BP on `spa_free_bplist[txg]` (zio.c:1422-1456), and
     `spa_sync_iterate_to_convergence` drains that list IN THE SAME SYNC — `spa_sync_frees` on every pass while
     `log_spacemap` is active (spa.c:10650-10665: the deferral to `spa_deferred_bpobj` and the next txg is the
     pre-log_spacemap path, taken only from `zfs_sync_pass_deferred_free` on and only without the feature) —
     `zio_free_sync` walking the gang tree (`zio_gang_tree_issue`): every header DVA freed at `vdev_gang_header_asize`
     (vdev.h:150: `SPA_OLD_GANGBLOCKSIZE` rounded to the allocation unit — one unit on a disk vdev — `metaslab_free_dva`,
     metaslab.c:5801-5803), every child BP's DVAs, a nested header's tree. So agnos does exactly that (`zfs_wgang`,
     from `zfs_wc_kill`): mode 0 checks and counts — every header read and verified by its embedded checksum as
     `zfs_gang_read` verifies it (ashift-sized, else the legacy 512 B) — mode 1 frees, all as FREE extents of log T;
     log T's size bound counts the excess over three a block (`zfs_wgang_xs`, in `zfs_wc_plan` and `zfs_wh_grow`). NOT
     `free_bpobj` (k.27's earlier sketch, § e.2's review fix): OpenZFS never defers a MOS gang free to the async
     destroy — that is the DATASET kill's branch A for gang / dedup / clone blocks (`dsl_dataset_block_kill`). The
     `$FREE` charge § e.2 required for a deferral therefore does not arise. A MOS DEDUP predecessor stays refused by
     name (the DDT would hold its references; the MOS is never deduplicated); a tree deeper than 2 too (the reader's
     own bound). **Proof:** no fixture's MOS holds a gang block (k.27: the forced-ganging txg made none), so the commit
     path runs in memory on a REAL tree — `zfs_wst_gang` (ZFS_WRITE_SELFTEST, main): gang.bin's first gang L0 counted,
     freed (exactly the counted extents, every one a FREE, each header at one allocation unit), undone, the closing
     digests the allocator as found (`zfs-wb3: gang … undone ok`, which both ZFS smokes' main lanes print). **Open:** a
     MOS gang ON DISK needs a pool OpenZFS gangs its MOS on — `vfs.zfs.metaslab.force_ganging` low enough for MOS blocks
     (≈ 512 B) with `force_ganging_pct` 100 while a NEW fixture pool is built (RECIPE 9: additive, every lane re-keyed,
     one cold build) — not taken in this bite: the gate's lanes run unchanged, and no lane can show it without the new
     pool and a lane for it.
111. **(B10, k.63 — DECIDED and built) agnos drains its own DELETE_QUEUE entries, in later txgs.** FreeBSD frees every
     unlinked object through the queue (`zfs_remove_` → `zfs_unlinked_add`; at the vnode's last release, or
     `zfs_unlinked_drain` at the next mount, `zfs_rmnode`): `dmu_free_long_range` over the data — chunk after chunk, each
     its own tx — then ONE tx frees the dnode, removes the queue's entry and (with ZPL_XATTR) queues the
     extended-attribute directory. **Built** (`zfs_write.cyr` § "k.63"): the op that queued an object (agnos's lazy
     path, § f.5: its free did not fit one txg, or it holds an xattr directory) drains it in the txgs after its own
     (`zfs_wu_end` → `zfs_wq_obj`, fs_lock released between them — `zfs_wf_yield`), one txg a step (`zfs_wq_one`):
     whole when its free fits (`zfs_wu_free`, the entry removed through B8's ZAP path — a fat queue too), else a
     bounded part of its data from the top first (`zfs_wf_trunc1`, B5's truncation: its kill-list and extent-list
     bounds are the chunk), the object staying queued between steps. Decisions: **(a)** no drain at mount — G1 (a mount
     writes not one byte until a write verb runs); "at rw mount" is the first txgs after the op, which FreeBSD's
     vnode-release path also is; **(b)** an object holding an extended-attribute DIRECTORY is refused by name and stays
     queued for OpenZFS's next writable mount (zfs_rmnode's purge — the directory queued, each xattr link_destroyed in a
     tx of its own — is not built); so is a queued DIRECTORY too big for one txg (its ZAP is not truncated); **(c)**
     the bounded part goes through `zfs_wf_trunc1`, whose last part stamps SIZE 0 / MTIME / CTIME on the unlinked
     object (FreeBSD's `dmu_free_long_range` stamps nothing): invisible — the next step frees the object, and between
     the two its state is a valid unlinked file of length 0; **(d)** FreeBSD's own queue entries (an fd open across the
     last unlink at export) are not drained by any agnos verb — OpenZFS's next mount drains them (a `sync`#12-time
     drain is bite B11's, with its policy). On disk: `agnos9`'s B7 entries `lz2.bin` and `lzd` (one txg each) and
     `xd.txt` refused by name; `agnostank/off/lazy.bin` in bounded parts (the op list's knobs `zfs_wq_tight` 1 and
     `zfs_wt_ftight` 4: four kill entries a truncation txg); and the production path itself — `off/b10.bin` unlinked with
     `zfs_wq_auto` on, queued by its txg and drained by the same op. The B7 lists' own lazy unlinks keep their entries
     (the op list holds `zfs_wq_auto` at 0 there), so OpenZFS's drain of agnos's entries is still judged in the guest.
112. **(B10, k.93 — reviewed, kept named) Neither refusal is reachable on a fixture.** (1) The `empty_bpobj`'s last
     reference needs every deadlist entry naming it materialised — but every snapshot-free head's deadlist names it
     once (refcount 21 on main, 16 on wr: ≥ the heads), so no op sequence on a pool with a head reaches 0. (2) A fat
     deadlist ZAP needs ≈ 2,047 snapshot intervals (a microzap's 128 KiB), none on any fixture. B8's code could make both
     edits (a MOS fat-ZAP removal of `empty_bpobj` + `dmu_object_free`; `zap_update` of the entry in a fat leaf through
     `zfs_wz_op`), but wired without a pool that reaches them it would be untested code on the commit path — the
     bite's rule: kept named (`zfs_wdl_matok` at the admission, `zfs_wdl_mat` as the backstop), and the op list's FAT
     knob (`zfs_wt_dlfat`) keeps the refusal running on disk. A fixture with a 2,048-snapshot dataset would lift (2).
113. **(B10) What B10 costs a long boot: ghosts and the room.** Every txg of a boot frees the MOS blocks it rewrites and
     now also flushes ≥ 1 metaslab (a 128 KiB space map block, ZLE, × 3 copies), its dnode block and the U block — so a
     txg defers ≈ 45 KB more for the boot than before (k.40's rule), and destroys ≈ one log, which becomes a
     ghost. wr's burst now stops at write 67 — 66 txgs, 270,336 B (80 at the merge; `zfs-room-check.py`, which takes a destroyed log's
     txg from its plan line since B10, holds where); `full`'s fill is unchanged (131,072 B in one txg, as at the merge). Main's burst is **180** appends (250
     since the B5 review): at 250 its list ran out at the slop at the 224th append (913,408 B) — and `off/b10.bin`'s
     create after it — so the burst gives back 70 txgs' deferral; its truncation still frees all 180 in ONE txg whose
     log holds them (B5 review finding 2's point). All of it is k.40's (bite B11): releasing deferred frees after
     TXG_DEFER_SIZE gives the room back and retires the ghosts — B11's gate may restore 250.
114. **(B10) The writer area grows 8 → 10 MiB.** `ZWA_GHO` (256 KiB, 1,024 ghosts) at 8 MiB, `ZWA_FSB` (512 KiB, the
     largest bitmap: a 16 GiB metaslab at ashift 12) at 8.25 MiB; `[0x8C0000, 0xA00000)` 1.25 MiB free for B11 / B12
     (k.107 (f)'s rule: no region overlaps another — the table in § g). `pmm_alloc_2mb_run(ZWK_WRUNS = 5)`; a host
     without five contiguous 2 MiB runs below 256 MB refuses rw by name (`no 10 MiB writer area`).
115. **(B10) How many flushes, and the bound.** `zfs_wl_est` is `spa_estimate_metaslabs_to_flush` over agnos's summary
     (one row per live log: txgcount 1, msdcount the dirty metaslabs whose U is its txg) with
     `zfs_unflushed_log_txg_max` = `ZWL_TXGMAX` **14** and the block limit never binding (an agnos log is one small
     block: OpenZFS's own `incoming == 0` arm) — at least `zfs_min_metaslabs_to_flush` (1); past the estimate, the walk
     flushes every metaslab whose U is 14 txgs old. **14, not § c.5's 16**, because a flush is a heuristic that may
     wait (OpenZFS's `metaslab_flush` returns early too): one whose blocks the metadata arena cannot hold beside every
     reservation an admission counts (`zfs_wf_mfit` — the dirty metadata, the fat-ZAP split reserve, the B9 deadlist
     reserve, ZWF_MRSV the commit's own), or the dirty set or the extent list cannot, skips to a later txg (`skip=1`),
     and every such txg leaves one more log alive. fz's 300-op list measured it at 16: three txgs in a row on its
     uid-accounting datasets (641–643, one fat ZAP upgrade among them) took the log count 15 → 18 — two txgs of slack
     keep the design's 16 on every lane (⚠ the B10 review, k.119: only because no fixture truncates for more than six
     txgs — a txg its admissions filled could not take even its FIRST flush). Before any U moves, the destruction of EVERY live log is reserved (one MOS
     dnode block per distinct block holding them, at the meta-dnode's block size; their blocks' DVAs as FREE extents;
     `skip=3` when the txg cannot hold it), so a txg never moves a U past a log it then lacks the room to destroy. The
     backstop in `zfs_wl_kill_logs` stayed (until the B10 review made it a named commit failure, k.121) — a log left older than every U is legal for OpenZFS's import (its first
     sync's `spa_cleanup_old_sm_logs` destroys it), a failed commit is not — and counts, inside the commit, only what
     remains: the MOS (`zfs_wl_kroom`; k.117 (6)). No admission reserve was added: every
     pinned txg count of B5–B9 stands (wr's truncation still 6 txgs). ⚠ Superseded by the B10 review: the admissions
     hold hygiene's minimum back (k.119), and the pins that moved are recorded there. Measured, every lane: at most 5 logs alive after a txg on ashift9, 12 on wr / wr@u0, 14 on main, 6 on full / full@pre, 11 on logs, 15 on fz — never above 16; zdb's own count at the end (A3y) 5 / 5 / 6 / 14 / 5 / 5 / 6 / 14.
116. **(B10) The oracle's new rules, and what proves them.** Host (h6, `zfs-wms-check.py --replay`): every entry under a
     space map's debug word is of that word's type; no debug txg of a metaslab's space map passes its U ≠ 0 (a flush
     sets U = its txg: an append past U is replayed again from the logs — the import's "overlapping"); no log is
     older than every U ≠ 0 (spa_cleanup_old_sm_logs). Validated on every pristine fixture image first (all PASS;
     `makefs` fails as before: no spacemap_histogram). Guest: **A3y** (a new REQUIRED step) holds zdb `-m`'s log set —
     "Log Spacemap object O txg T" and each metaslab's "unflushed txg=U" — to the host replay's count, none older than
     the least U, every U naming one, the newest the lane's own txg. Smoke: one hygiene line per commit, ≤ 16 logs
     alive after every txg, flushes and destroyed logs on every lane of 20+ txgs, both § c.5 cases on ashift9 and main
     (`fonly`, `self` ≥ 1), the image's log count == the last hygiene line's; `zfs-room-check.py --log`;
     `zfs-sa-check.py` (a drained object is FREED). Negatives N1–N5: § h's B10 record.
117. **(B10) Found on the way.** (1) § c.2's SM-append deferral rule, built at B1 (`zfs_wsm_ent`), is wrong once logs are destroyed —
     k.108: gone from the kernel and the host replay; the ghosts carry the deferral. (2) (an interaction, not a
     pre-B10 defect) `zfs_wh_need` read the stored histogram from disk; a flush rewrites it in T, so it reads the
     dirty one first (k.109). (3) The flush's fit ignored what the admissions had reserved in the metadata arena:
     fz's list ran 18 logs alive (the gate's 5th run: `up to 18 log space maps alive after a txg`) — `zfs_wf_mfit`
     beside every admission reserve, and `ZWL_TXGMAX` 14 (k.115). (4) A txg could move a U past a log and then lack
     the room to destroy it — the destruction of every live log is reserved before any U moves (`skip=3`, k.115);
     the reservation first counted one dnode block and 16 KiB per LOG, now one per distinct dnode block holding
     them, at the meta-dnode's block size. (5) Main's 250-append burst ran out at the slop at append 224 (the 5th
     run: `burst stopped at write 224`, then `off/b10.bin`'s create) — the op list's count, not the code: 180
     (k.113). (6) `zfs_wl_kroom`: the backstop's room check inside `zfs_wc_plan` (`zfs_wl_room`) counted the
     dataset phase's blocks twice — six extents each beside the extents they already made — and on wr's 2 MiB
     write txgs refused every destroy while the flushes moved U (the 6th run: wr at most 15 logs alive, 17 txgs
     skipping, 43 destroying none; with `zfs_wl_kroom`, which counts only the MOS still to come: 12 / 7 / 35).
     (7) The smoke's hold measure (§ k.8) charged a drain's txgs and the op list's stats after a plan line to the
     commit: a drain counts from its begin / object line, a txg after another of its op from that one's hygiene
     line (a measurement; no verdict changes).
118. **(B10) Open.** (1) On-disk proof of a space map's GROWTH past its first block (and of a two-word entry / pad word in a
     metaslab space map): every fixture space map is one 128 KiB block holding ≤ 16 KiB, a metaslab ≤ 32,768 units
     (no run past 2^15) — proven in memory on every ashift-9 lane (`zfs_wst_flgrow`: 16,386 words across blocks 0..1,
     nlevels 1 → 2, replayed back) and on the host (gate 37: `zfs_sm_seg`'s two-word / pad cases). (2) A MOS gang on disk
     (k.110). (3) The xattr-directory drain (k.111 (b)). (4) Hold time on iron (k.8): measured on QEMU/KVM only — B10's
     txgs 205–577 ms on every lane but two outliers under host load (wr@u0's burst 1.1–2.0 s, one fz unlink 4.7 s;
     § h's B10 record), means 216–419 ms.

119. **(B10 review) Hygiene's own room: a txg its admissions fill still takes its first flush; the log limit is judged
     after the plan.** The review found a txg its admissions fill to a bound starving hygiene: the kill paths filled the
     metadata arena to the admissions' limit (`ZWK_MARENA - ZWF_MRSV`), `zfs_wl_plan`'s first flush no longer fit
     (`skip=1 flushed=0`) and the txg moved no U — wr's deferred-kill truncation of `rs4k/r64k.bin` (run8, txgs
     241–246, `fbpo` free_bpobj appends) took the logs 6 → 11, one per txg; fz's txg 643 did the same. A truncation or a
     k.63 drain long enough (`zfs_wq_obj` allows `ZWF_FRMAX` steps) would carry the logs to `ZWK_MAXLOG`, and
     `zfs_commit1`'s refusal there — checked BEFORE `zfs_wl_plan` — refused every later commit: a write lockout for the
     boot. k.115's "16 on every lane" held only because no fixture truncates for more than six txgs. **What did not
     work, measured (scratch `b10/fix1/lanesA-try1`, `diag1`, `diag2`):** holding an exact minimum back from the
     admissions' own limit (the first unit of a txg judged as before, so an op still progresses) — wr's truncation
     went 6 → 19 txgs and still skipped three in a row: the plan's arena read 1,331,200 B in every skipping txg, 52 KiB
     past what the last admission had seen (an op dirties past its last check — the CRSV / MRSV margins were sized
     for exactly that), and a 128 KiB slack on top made it 66 txgs and a failed truncation — a reserve carved from the admissions' limit trades the starvation for slower, refusing ops.
     **Built (grow before refactor):** (1) the metadata arena grows by `ZWL_HAR` = 384 KiB (1.875 → 2.25 MiB; `ZWA_CMP`
     `0x5E0000` → `0x640000`, `ZWA_BM` `0x600000` → `0x8C0000`, § g) and NO admission reaches the new part
     (`zfs_wf_alim`: every admission's limit is exactly the pre-review one — no op, no pinned txg count changes; wr's
     truncation is 6 txgs again); the commit's plan, inside `zfs_commit` (`zfs_wc_in`), sees it — hygiene's first
     flush and every live log's destruction, beside an op's overrun past its last check. (2) The EXTENT list has no
     such slack, so there the admissions hold back the next txg's exact need: `zfs_wl_hres` — at each publish and at
     the rw mount — measures destroying every live log (`zfs_wl_rsv`, factored out of `zfs_wl_plan`) and the FIRST
     flush the plan will try (the dirty metaslab of least U < T, the lowest index among equals; k.24's preset counted)
     at its EXACT cost — a dry `zfs_wl_flush` (`dry` 1: the count phase, nothing changed), exact because nothing of T
     is allocated or freed before its commit — and `zfs_wf_xfit` holds 6 × `zfs_wl_hb` + `zfs_wl_hx` back outside the
     commit (≈ 40 of 4,096 extents; capped at half the list). A first flush larger than `ZWL_HAR` waits for a lighter
     txg, as before (none on any fixture: `rsv=` ≤ 180,224 B). (3) The log limit: refused up front, nothing done, only
     when the ghost table makes hygiene skip (k.122); otherwise the commit runs, and after `zfs_wc_plan`
     `zfs_nlogs - zfs_wl_nd >= ZWK_MAXLOG` fails it by name before its uberblock (T − 1 stays; the 1,024-entry table
     never overflows) — the limit can no longer lock a pool whose hygiene can destroy. (4) The hygiene line prints
     `rsv=` (the first flush + the destroys, from `zfs_wl_hres`) and, when a flush waits, `wait=<arena in use>+<asked>
     xroom=<extent room>`; the smoke FAILs two txgs in a row that skip their first flush (one alone is an op whose
     first unit filled its txg — bounded slack). Prior art: OpenZFS's `spa_flush_metaslabs` has no resource bound to
     miss; what keeps sync's own work possible is held out of what writes may take (`spa_get_slop_space`'s slop, the
     dirty-data limit) — agnos holds hygiene's share out of the admissions the same way. **Measured** (scratch
     `b10/fix1`): on every targeted lane no txg skipped its first flush and no
     pinned count moved — wr 136 txgs: 136 flushes (15 FREE-only / 74 self), 202 bumps, 131 destroyed, ≤ 11 alive, 0
     txgs skipping (run8: 129 flushes, 7 skipping — six of them the truncation's — ≤ 12), `trunc … r64k.bin -> 0
     txgs=6` as pinned; wr@u0 136: 136 / 209 bumps / 132 destroyed, ≤ 12, 0 skipping, the burst still stops at write
     67; main 284: 286 flushes, 271 destroyed, ≤ 14, 0 skipping; ashift9 56: 56, ≤ 5; full 16: 16, ≤ 6; logs 9: 18
     flushes, ≤ 8 alive (run8: 11), 1 skip after three flushes (never a first); `rsv=` 163,840–229,376 B. Commit hold
     (§ k.8's measure, QEMU/KVM): ashift9 202–229 ms (mean 213), wr 204–490 (343), wr@u0 206–495 (343), main 206–423
     (346), full 211–375 (250), logs 205–223 (214) — no slower than run8 (the dry flush at each publish is one load of
     an already-resident metaslab and a walk of its space map). OpenZFS verify 37/37 on wr, ashift9, wr@u0, full, logs
     and main (§ h's second-review record). Not run this round: fz (its txg 643 was the second instance; the merge's
     full gate runs it) and the four ashift9 copies (identical op list).
120. **(B10 review) A gang header COPY is verified against DVA[0]'s identity.** `zfs_wgang` (k.27) and the reader's
     `zfs_gang_read` verified the header read from DVA d against that DVA's own vdev and offset; OpenZFS's
     `zio_checksum_gang_verifier` (zio_checksum.c:279-287) uses `BP_IDENTITY(bp)` — `&blk_dva[0]` (spa.h:587) — and
     `BP_GET_PHYSICAL_BIRTH` for every copy, and `dmu_write_policy` gives a gang header ≥ 2 copies
     (`zp_gang_copies`, dmu.c:2543). So an intact ditto copy never verified: a lost DVA[0] header failed the read, and a
     MOS gang predecessor's free failed its commit (`gang header unreadable`, the pool read-only). **Fixed** in both:
     DVA[0]'s vdev and offset and the physical birth for every copy (`zfs_gang_read` loses its `w0` argument). Proven in
     memory on gang.bin's real header (main): `zfs-wb3: gang …` now verifies EVERY copy against DVA[0]'s identity and
     counts how many verify against their own — `2 header copies verified against DVA[0]'s identity (1 against their own)`
     on gang.bin's header (main, both smokes): the copy at DVA[1] verifies only under DVA[0]'s identity — it was
     unreadable to the old code. No fixture loses a DVA[0] header on disk (a media error), so
     the read path's own fallback is proven by that stage plus zfs-smoke's unchanged reads.
121. **(B10 review) The destroy's backstop is a named commit failure, not a log left below the least U.**
     `zfs_wl_kill_logs` broke out with `skip=1` when `zfs_wl_kroom` refused a destroy, calling the obsolete log "legal
     for OpenZFS's import" — true (its first sync's `spa_cleanup_old_sm_logs` destroys it), but OpenZFS's own sync never
     leaves one, and both of agnos's oracles hold the image to that (`zfs-wms-check.py`: "log txg … is older than every
     unflushed txg"; the guest's A3y): the first firing would have turned a lane red on a pool the kernel called valid,
     and the hygiene line could not tell it from a flush that did not fit. The plan reserves every live log's
     destruction before any U moves and (k.119) every admission holds it back, so a refused destroy is that reservation
     failing: `log destroy: the reserved room did not hold, txg <lt>` — the commit fails before its uberblock. Negative
     N7 (kroom refuses every destroy) is red there: on ashift9 (scratch `b10/fix1/neg-n7.log`), `zfs: commit txg 31 failed: log destroy: the reserved room did not
     hold, txg 25 - pool read-only until reboot`, the op list's next op `mkdir FAIL` with the same name and the
     lane red; the source restored byte-identical (sha256 `7e3de455…`).
122. **(B10 review) What the ghosts cost a boot until B11 — k.108 corrected.** k.108 recorded `skip=2` (the ghost table
     cannot take every live log) as a per-txg event. It is a cliff: ghosts are retired by nothing in B10 (only
     `zfs_wmem_get` resets them) and every destroyed log of the boot becomes one (lt ≥ T0 − 2 always holds for logs this
     boot made) — ≈ one per txg (fz: 28 at txg 259, 228 at 459, 388 at 619). From ≈ `ZWL_MAXGHO` − 14 destroyed logs on,
     `ngho + nlogs > 1,024` holds on EVERY txg: hygiene stops for the rest of the boot, the logs climb one per txg to
     `ZWK_MAXLOG`, and there (k.119 (3)) every commit is refused by name, nothing done. **A boot of B10 alone commits
     ≈ 2,000 txgs** (≈ 1,010 + ≈ 1,010); each create, write chunk, unlink or rename is ≥ 1. Before the cliff every
     metaslab load walks every ghost (one log block read each), so a load costs more with uptime. Not grown: a bigger
     table moves the cliff and lengthens every load. Retired by B11 (k.40 / k.64: TXG_DEFER_SIZE = 2, OpenZFS's
     `ms_defer` rotation) — after it a ghost older than T − 2 carries no deferral and goes. **Merge-gate requirement:**
     ghosts bounded — a lane or selftest of ≥ 2,100 txgs, or the smoke asserting `ghosts` ≤ a few on every hygiene line
     once B11 releases them; until then the ceiling stands as recorded here.
123. **(B10 review) The gate's evidence, the room oracle, a stale comment.** (a) The B10 record cited
     `run8-verify/verify.out`; that scratch directory is empty, and the worktree's `build/zfs-write-smoke*` hold the N3
     negative's red (run after run8: `verify ashift9 FAIL … VERIFY0(sls->sls_mscount)`, `A3y FAIL no host count`) — the
     merge must not read them as B10's gate. run8's per-step guest results survive in `run8-logs/verify.err` (each
     step's `guest: verify <lane> <step> PASS` line — the eight A3y lines with zdb's counts quoted in the record) and
     `run8-logs/verify.out` (the eight summary lines). The review round keeps every targeted run's per-step output
     beside its logs (scratch `b10/fix1/{diag3,lanesB,lanesC}-logs/verify.{out,err}` — `verify.err` carries each
     guest step's line). (b) Since B10 `zfs-room-check.py
     --log` takes a destroyed log's ALLOC / FREE bytes from the kernel's own plan line; only the surviving logs (≤ 16 a
     lane) are cross-checked against the image — so on a long lane most txgs' room figures are the kernel's claim (§
     i.2 says so now). Re-deriving them independently needs each destroyed log's block (freed, never reused this boot,
     but its dnode is gone from the final MOS) — an older MOS from the uberblock ring reaches only the last 128 / 32
     txgs; left open. (c) `syscall.cyr`'s unlink#30 arm still said a too-big object waits for OpenZFS's next mount;
     since B10 the same call drains it over further txgs (comment fixed). (d) **Open:** the ghost ceiling until B11
     (k.122) and its merge-gate bound; fz and the four ashift9 copies not run this round (the merge's full gate); a
     first flush over `ZWL_HAR` (≈ 2 space-map blocks of 128 KiB of delta — no fixture) still waits; the admissions'
     own formulas do not count everything an op dirties after its last check (52 KiB measured on wr; B5's CRSV and
     ZWF_MRSV absorb it, and since k.119 `ZWL_HAR` too) — counting it exactly would move B5–B9's pinned txg counts;
     hold time on iron (k.8).

124. **(B11) Policy B as built, and the NARROWER commit-before-observe rule (proved).** § b.2 asked for a commit before
    *any* other ZFS entry, or a narrower rule with its proof. The rule built: a pending txg commits before an entry that
    READS ITS OBJECT (read#5 through any fd, lseek SEEK_END, stat / lstat, open#7 of it, the exec / kernel readers —
    `zfs_wp_obs(ref)`), before statfs#103, before every mutation and before a write#1 of another object
    (`zfs_wp_flush`); a read of another file, getdents, readdir and readlink commit nothing (they only apply the age
    bound). PROOF: the reader never consults the dirty set, so an entry observes T − 1 for every block; a pending txg
    holds (i) the object O's L0 / indirect copies and its dnode block — whose OTHER dnodes are byte copies of T − 1's —
    (ii) the accounting ZAP blocks `zfs_wd_urs1` dirtied at admission (copies; their VALUES change only inside the
    commit), (iii) deadlist / free_bpobj state counted at admission and written inside the commit. What the commit then
    changes, as the ABI can read it: O's data, SIZE / MTIME / CTIME / FLAGS / st_blocks (its dnode), and the pool's
    space (statfs's f_bfree / f_bavail) — the accounting ZAPs and the dataset / dsl_dir figures have no ABI reader;
    names, types, inode numbers and every other object's content are T − 1's in both states. So an entry that reads
    none of O's bytes, attributes or the space reads exactly what it would read after the commit. A hard link of O
    resolves to the same ref (dataset slot + object), so it is covered. Measured on the op list (agnos9): a read of
    hello.txt with b.bin pending left it pending (0 txgs), a read of b.bin through a second fd committed it first and
    returned the new bytes (1 txg) — `zfs-wtest: b11 observe … ok`; ring 3: zfsx 157 / 158. ⚠ What it does not give:
    POSIX's "a read after a write returns the written data" across PROCESSES is kept (any read of O commits);
    `st_blocks` of a file with coalesced writes is the committed one until something observes it — which stat does.
    The policy layer is `zfs_wp_*` (§ B11 of `zfs_write.cyr`); the engine is unchanged but for (a) a continued txg
    (`zfs_wf_begin`: the same object's pending txg is entered again — no second `zfs_wd_urs1`, the in-flight bytes kept),
    (b) `ZWD_CHG` (a block written twice in a txg charged once; a block 0 that grows re-charged at its new size), (c) a
    continued txg is never dropped (-2: commit it, retry fresh), (d) a single-level `dnode_increase_indirection` inside
    a continued txg (what policy A's single write already did; a 2+-level growth commits first).
125. **(B11) k.40 / k.64 BUILT: deferred frees released after TXG_DEFER_SIZE = 2 txgs, exactly as OpenZFS.**
    `metaslab_sync_done(msp, txg)` (metaslab.c:4545-4655) vacates `ms_defer[txg % TXG_DEFER_SIZE]` — the frees of
    txg − 2 — into `ms_allocatable` and swaps `ms_freed` (txg's frees) into that slot; allocations of txg X happen in
    `spa_sync(X)`, after `sync_done(X − 1)`, so a block freed in T is allocatable from T + 3. ⚠ The header comment
    (metaslab_impl.h:296-302: "freed in txg 50 … not available for reallocation until txg 52 (50 + TXG_DEFER_SIZE)")
    means "until 52 has synced"; the code is the rule, and spa_load's `safe_rewind_txg = last_ubsync − TXG_DEFER_SIZE`
    (spa.c:6365) is its consequence. agnos: `zfs_wdef_rel(t)` once txg t is DURABLE (the end of `zfs_wc_pub`, and the op
    list's ub-only commit) moves the window's low txg `zfs_wdef_lo` to t − 1 and releases every log below it — its FREE
    bytes leave `zfs_w_defer`, its units leave every resident metaslab's deferred bitmap (a STRICT clear: an agnos free is
    deferred by exactly one log — a deferred unit is never allocated, so never freed again inside the window — and a
    unit that is not deferred fails the pool by name), a non-resident metaslab rebuilds the window at its next load
    (`zfs_ms_load`: FREEs of logs ≥ `zfs_wdef_lo`; agnos's own space-map flushes (B10) by their txg against the same
    window). The mount's window (T0 − 2 .. T0, kept: stricter than OpenZFS, which defers nothing after an import) is
    released by EVICTING every resident metaslab: OpenZFS at its slop does not defer (`defer_allowed`), so a unit it
    freed and reallocated inside that window is both allocated and deferred (the `pre` overlap, § c.2) — a reload
    rebuilds exactly the window. ⭐ CRASH, for agnos's commit order (§ b.3, § d): txg T allocates only units released
    by T − 1's publish — freed in T − 3 or earlier, referenced by no uberblock after T − 4 — so whatever T's writes did
    before its uberblock, T − 1, T − 2 and T − 3 stay whole: the pool is T − 1 with OpenZFS's full rewind window; once
    one copy of uberblock T lands its blocks were flushed (the first FLUSH) and T − 1 .. T − 3 are whole too. The mount's
    first commits keep it: T0 + 1 allocates nothing released (lo = T0 − 2 until its publish), T0 + 2 at most T0 − 2's /
    T0 − 1's frees (blocks of T0 − 3 / T0 − 2's trees). Held on disk by the cut lanes (k.127: the cursor on a block
    freed in T0 + 4, the cut txg T0 + 6 must not take it, A4 rewinds to T0 + 3) and in memory by the op list's release
    op (`b11 release …`: P + 2's block elsewhere, P + 3's ON it) and by every read-back (`zfs_wt_rb` re-runs the mount's
    log scan with the LIVE window: `zfs_w_defer` and every reloaded metaslab's deferred count must equal what the
    releases left — after every op of every lane). What changed on disk because of it: wr's exhaustion burst became
    1 MiB appends (250 one-block ones no longer reach the room's end — measured: all 250 landed, the room never ran
    out), the room check's model (`zfs-room-check.py`: deferred(t) = the window's logs; UPPER summed per txg, since room
    − alloc now grows when frees leave the window — measured: `full`'s fill 1,179,648 B in txgs 243..250 and wr's 1 MiB
    burst 40,820,736 B in txgs 248..286, each held to the space record: < one block left where it stopped), and k.64's
    refusal is gone: `full`'s rename after the fill and `main`'s after its 250-txg burst COMMIT. ⚠ wr / wr@u0's `--mat`
    (Σ deadlist entries materialised) went 6 → 7 with the fixture re-key B11's payload forced (object numbers differ per
    cold build — e4.bin is object 256 in `r8-edfb6e66c0a9`, 38 in `r8-4ee74703473a`), the k.107 (g) luck again: the lane
    accepts `6|7` (k.129 (f)). ⚠ B10 (its own track) destroys old logs: a log
    destroyed while still inside the window is not found by the release walk — its bytes stay in `zfs_w_defer` and its
    units deferred until an eviction (stricter, never looser); the merge should release such a log before destroying it. (The merge did — through B10's ghost, made only inside
    the window and released and retired by `zfs_wdef_rel`: k.133 (a).)
126. **(B11) The hold time (k.8, k.48): measured without the op list's console, windowed, and what is left.** (1) THE
    MEASURE: `zfs_commit`'s own clock (`zfs_wc_hold`: entry → the publish, before the op list's plan line) — every
    figure in k.8 before B11 included that plan line (≈ 0.4 s of console rendering on QEMU, k.49) or the op's own
    dirtying. A commit on QEMU/KVM NVMe holds `fs_lock` for (zfs-write-smoke on the final source, every txg of every
    lane): ashift9 77 txgs 12.8–69.3 ms (mean 20.5; torn 23.1, grown 21.2), main 346 txgs 16.6–56.7 (22.3), wr / wr@u0
    111 txgs 15.1–1,415.9 / 15.2–103.4 (70.5 / 40.0), full / full@pre 24 txgs 37.6–235.5 / 36.2–200.2 (113 / 107 — one
    bitmap slot: metaslab loads inside the commit), logs 9 txgs 11.7–49.9 (28.3), fz 430 txgs 14.7–2,522.6 (31.3), the
    cut lanes' five 16.0–242.5; AHCI 41.4–488.4 (80.5) and USB 39.8–474.6 (76.6) on the same 77 txgs. The tails (wr's
    txg 213 1.4 s, fz's txg 318 2.5 s) are device time — QEMU's flush is a host `fdatasync`, and another track's QEMU ran
    beside this one — not CPU: their longest stretch between two window points was 0.56 / 0.68 ms. The figures of k.8
    and of the gate records B2–B9 are 3–25× these (the op list's console inside them, k.129 (d)). (2) THE
    WINDOWS (built): `kirqw_point` (io.cyr) opens a one-instruction interrupt window (`pushfq; clac; sti; nop; cli;
    popfq`) at every device command of the commit (`zfs_wr`), around its flushes and inside the NVMe / AHCI completion
    polls it waits in, at most every 20 µs, on the committing CPU only, once the scheduler is live, never inside an
    interrupt handler (`apic_in_service`: the timer ISR's death chain reaches `proc_destroy_fd_table`, which commits a
    killed orphan's coalesced writes) and never on USB mass storage (its xHCI event ring is `hid_poll`'s, which the timer
    ISR runs) or virtio-blk. Safe because the commit holds only plain spinlocks (preempt_count > 0: the timer ISR never
    switches it out), no interrupt handler takes `fs_lock`, a block-device lock or `console_lock` from the kernel frame a
    window interrupts (timer_handler: net / hid / hda / wq / sched; NVMe and AHCI are polled; the tick's death chain,
    which reaches `proc_destroy_fd_table` and so `fs_lock`, acts only on a CPL3 frame with preempt_count 0 — sched.cyr
    INV-L2), the window clears AC (no ISR runs with SMAP lifted) and popfq gives the caller back its exact IF / AC. The
    longest stretch between two window points (`irqmax`, the latency a live window leaves) is ≤ 0.96 ms on ashift9 (mean
    0.61 against its 20.5 ms hold), ≤ 2.90 ms on main (0.64), ≤ 2.55 ms on every NVMe lane and 2.54 on AHCI; USB, where
    no window opens, shows the stretches it would have (≤ 17.6 ms, mean 4.0). The op list runs at boot, before the
    scheduler, so its windows only measure (`windows=0` on its b11 lines); ring 3 runs them live. (3) MEASURED FROM
    RING 3 (zfsx clause 160: sysinfo#35's per-CPU 100 Hz tick counters around the close that commits 2 MiB, four CPUs):
    WITHOUT the windows (a scratch build whose `zfs_wirq_on` answers 0) cpu0 = 1, cpu1–3 = 10 — the committing CPU
    missed 9 ticks, ≈ 90 ms of its timer and devices; WITH them 11 / 11 / 11 / 11, and 10 / 10 / 10 / 10 on the final
    source — none missed (zfs-ring3-smoke's report line). (4) WHAT IS LEFT (not built — the rest, with its numbers):
    another CPU that wants `fs_lock` (any filesystem, not only ZFS) still spins for the whole commit — the holds of (1):
    ≈ 20 ms typical on NVMe, 80 ms on AHCI, up to 0.5 s on a one-slot pool and 2.5 s in a device stall — with IF=0 if it
    is in a syscall. The fix is
    OpenZFS's and every Unix's: never hold a spinlock across device I/O — a ZFS OWNERSHIP lock (`zfs_owner` = a CPU +
    1, taken under `fs_lock`, `fs_lock` then RELEASED for the commit; every ZFS entry that finds it owned drops
    `fs_lock` and waits) so ext2 / FAT run beside a ZFS commit, and with Path 2's blocking waits (roadmap § 1.57.x) the
    waiter sleeps instead of spinning. It touches every ZFS entry (≈ 25 arms and the op list) and the re-entry proofs of
    smp.cyr's lock banner — larger than this cut; until then the thresholds bound the hold (the 4 MiB data arena).
127. **(B11) The crash cut — what OpenZFS opens after a commit stopped part-way.** The knob is the boot command line:
    gnoboot loads `\boot\cmdline` from the ESP into `boot_info` (no kernel consumer before B11), and the op list reads the
    same file back through the ESP's FAT mount (`zfs_wt_cmdline`: `zfs_cut=N` or `zfs_cut=CLASS:N`, CLASS data / dsmeta /
    mos / chain / ub) — `ZFS_WRITE_OPS` builds only. It selects the CUT LIST on agnos9 (`zfs_wt_cutlist`): mkdir b11c
    (T0 + 1), create b11c/f.bin (T0 + 2), 4 × 64 KiB through one fd and closed (T0 + 3), block 0 rewritten — its DVA X
    freed (T0 + 4), hello.txt written (T0 + 5), each a policy-B close's commit; then the allocator restricted to X's
    metaslab with its cursor ON X (`zfs_wt_msonly`, the B11 knob) and the knob ARMED for T0 + 6, block 1 rewritten and
    closed: every device write of T0 + 6 passes `zfs_wt_cutw` (in `zfs_wr`, before the write), which halts the machine
    (`cli; hlt`) after N writes of the class. T0 + 6's write map on agnos9: data 1, dataset metadata 16, MOS 9, MOS chain
    15, uberblock 4 (45). Lanes (each verified by OpenZFS in the guest, on the image exactly as the halt left it):
    `cut-data` (data:1 — its one data block written, onto the space after X: X is deferred until T0 + 6 is durable) and
    `cut-mos` (chain:1 — every block but the MOS chain's written) must open T0 + 5 EXACTLY (`--final-txg`, h3; A1 / A2;
    the expected manifest of T0 + 5's ops, phase B) with T0 + 4 and T0 + 3 intact (A4: `zdb -t … -bcc` — T0 + 3 names X
    as f.bin's block 0: a free released one txg early puts T0 + 6's block ON it, and only this phase sees that, k.129 N3);
    `cut-ub1` / `cut-ub3` (ub:1 / ub:3 — after the first FLUSH, one or three of the four label slots written) must open
    T0 + 6 (`vdev_uberblock_load` takes the best txg of ANY label) with its blocks whole, the audit holding the cut txg's
    slot to exactly labels 0 .. K − 1 (`--cut-labels K`, new) and every earlier slot to all four. A close that did not
    commit leaves its writes for the cut txg — lost by the crash (k.129 N1). Every lane: h1–h9 PASS (the space record
    replays T0 + 5 / T0 + 6 — the cut txg's half-written log is no MOS object of it). RESULT (final source, T0 = 25):
    the four lanes PASS boot + audit and OpenZFS's verify, 36 steps each (A4's rewinds included) — `cut-data` halted after 1 device write (data 1) and `cut-mos`
    after 27 (data 1, dataset metadata 16, MOS 9, MOS chain 1), each opened at txg 30 exactly (h3 `new txgs 26..30`, A2)
    with A4's rewinds to 29 and 28 clean; `cut-ub1` after 42 writes (… MOS chain 15, uberblock 1) and `cut-ub3` after 44
    (uberblock 3), each opened at txg 31 with its slot in exactly labels 0 / 0–2 (`--cut-labels`). A free released one
    txg early (k.129's N3) passes every host check and goes red in A4 alone (`zdb -t 28`: error 97 reading f.bin's block
    0 — T0 + 6's block landed on it); a close that does not commit (N1) goes red in the kernel's own count, h3 and the
    guest's A2 / B-manifest / C (the cut txg took hello.txt's write with it).
128. **(B11) UTF-8 names (k.55's `utf8only` half).** FreeBSD validates every name on a `utf8only` (or normalised —
    normalization requires utf8only) dataset with `u8_validate(nm, strlen(nm), NULL, U8_VALIDATE_ENTIRE, &error)` and
    answers EILSEQ before the lookup, create, mkdir, rename, symlink and link (`zfs_vnops_os.c:846, :1073, :1444, :3340,
    :3629, :3858`). `zfs_u8_valid` (`zfs_wenc.cyr`) is that function from its tables (u8_textprep.c:202-336): lead bytes
    0x80-0xC1 EILSEQ, 0xF5-0xFF ERANGE, a sequence the bytes left cannot hold EINVAL, the second byte's range by lead
    (E0 A0-BF, ED 80-9F, F0 90-BF, F4 80-8F), continuations 80-BF — Unicode's well-formed table. `zfs_wname` runs it on
    every NEW name (create / mkdir / symlink, rename's and link's target) of a utf8only dataset; a normalised dataset
    keeps refusing any non-ASCII name by name (the normal form needs the Unicode tables; such datasets are read-only at
    the gate anyway, § a.2). Gate 37: 1,358 cases — all 128 lead bytes against eight boundary second bytes, overlongs,
    surrogates, past U+10FFFF, truncations, a 300-string fuzz — each verdict cross-checked against Python's strict codec,
    each name ending at the guard page. On disk (wr): `café.txt` (B7's rename, refused until B11) and a 4-byte name are
    written and verified by OpenZFS (B manifest, stat); an overlong '/', a surrogate, U+110000, a 0xF5 lead and a
    truncated sequence are refused with their classes; the "\xff" link refused (class 2). ⚠ agnos's own lookup of an
    invalid name answers "not found" where FreeBSD answers EILSEQ (the reader does not validate) — no such name can
    exist on the dataset, so nothing observable differs but the errno.
129. **(B11) Defects found on the way, fixed in this cut.** (a) `sync`#12 called `ext2_sync` with NO lock (it touches
    the ext2 scratch and superblock that every other ext2 path reaches under `fs_lock`; `power_flush` takes it) — now
    under `fs_lock` with the ZFS commit. (b) B5's first txg refused a write that met ERESTART at its first block after
    its block-0 growth was charged: it dropped the txg and answered -1 near the slop where the next txg would have taken
    it — now the growth commits and the write goes on (-2, `zfs_wf_txflush`). (c) THE ORACLE: the write smoke read the
    serial log through `strings`, which cuts a line at every non-ASCII byte — a valid UTF-8 name's op line lost its
    `-> r txgs=k` (the lane's final txg one short) and its `--want` lines never matched: `slog` keeps UTF-8; zfs-sa-check
    and zfs-hole-check decode the log as UTF-8. (d) THE MEASURE: every commit-hold figure before B11 (k.8) included the
    op list's own plan-line printing (≈ 0.4 s of console a txg) — `zfs_wc_hold` ends before it. (e) The room check's model
    assumed room − alloc never grows (the boot-long deferral) — UPPER is summed per txg now (k.125). (f) THE FIXTURE
    (B9's, k.107 (g)): the exact count of deadlist entries wr's ops materialise still follows a cold fixture build's
    object numbering — B11's payload change re-keyed the fixture and the count went 6 → 7 on a correct pool (the extra
    one: edgec/e4.bin's write, txg 221, `dl=1 mat=1`); the lane's `--mat` is an ERE now (`6|7`) — the deadlists
    themselves are judged by h7 and the guest's B-snap / D-destroy, which a wrong materialisation fails. (g) THE
    READ-ONLY LANE (found by the first full run): on `ashift9-ro` (the pool read-only after its first commit fails) B11's
    fd ops named their files' absence (`path unresolvable: 2` — the list's creates were refused) where every op of the
    list names `pool read-only` for a file it would have created first (zfs_wt_w's rule since B6), and the release op
    FAILED outright (`block 0 unreadable`), so the lane counted 74 ops and 2 failures — now the fd / observe / hook ops
    follow the list's rule and the release op runs its four write ops, each refused (78 ops, the one failure). (h) THE
    VERIFY CAP: one guest boot verifies at most 8 lanes (three disks each, zfs-fixture.sh VERIFY_MAX_LANES); B11's four
    cut lanes made 12, and the full run's verify went VOID (`12 lanes to boot`). The smoke now verifies in equal batches
    (`$VDIR/b1`, `b2` — hard links to each lane's files, a verify.out each); one FAIL fails it, one VOID voids it.
130. **(B11) Open.** (1) THE OWNERSHIP LOCK (k.126 (4)): other CPUs still wait on `fs_lock` for a whole commit. (2) No
    sync thread (policy C): a process that writes and then idles without closing keeps its writes in memory until the
    next ZFS entry, `sync`, exit or shutdown — the age bound is only seen at an entry; a kthread (region 7's four are
    taken) or a timer-armed deferred commit would close it. (3) A killed orphan's teardown runs from the timer ISR's death
    chain (INV-L2): its coalesced writes commit THERE, under the ISR, without windows (`apic_in_service`) — up to a whole
    commit inside an interrupt handler; leaving them pending for the next entry is the alternative. (4) The interrupt
    windows are not opened on USB mass storage (xHCI shared with `hid_poll`) or virtio-blk. (5) B10's log destruction
    and the release walk (k.125's merge note). (6) Ring 3 cannot count txgs; the exact final txg (h3) and the op list's
    per-op counts are the coalescing oracle. (After the review: (3) CLOSED by k.131 (c); the rest in k.132.)
131. **(B11) The review of B11 (2026-10-13), eleven findings — each confirmed by reading the code, fixed in this cut.**
    (a) **CRITICAL — another process's acknowledged policy-B writes could be DROPPED, silently, on SMP.** A write#1 or a
    truncation of several txgs releases `fs_lock` between them (`zfs_wf_yield`), and B11 is the first state that leaves
    a dirty set across a lock release; the check for another object's pending txg ran only at `zfs_wf_write`'s entry.
    A write#1 to Y on another CPU in the gap left Y pending; X's next txg then met `zfs_wf_begin`'s "dirty set not
    empty" refusal, and `zfs_wf_drop` threw Y's dirty blocks away. `zfs_wp_ref` still named Y, so Y's close
    committed an empty dirty set and answered 0. **Fix, in three layers.** (1) After every yield the entry rule runs
    again: `zfs_wf_write` commits a pending txg of another object (or an aged one, or any under policy A) and
    `zfs_wtrunc` commits any (`by=yield`). (2) `zfs_wp_own`: every op that opens a txg of its own (`zfs_wf_begin`
    uncontinued, `zfs_wc1`, `zfs_wu_begin`) commits a pending txg first (`by=begin`), so no refusal can drop one.
    (3) `zfs_wf_drop` is never silent. If a pending ref is still set (a backstop that should never fire), it names
    the loss (`zfs: a pending txg was dropped - its writes are lost (EIO at close)`), clears the ref and sets
    `zfs_wp_eref`, so the owner's close / sync answers -1 rather than 0.
    **Proof:** the op list's RACE op (`zfs_wt_race`, agnos9). The interleaving is made deterministic by
    `zfs_wt_yhook`, which `zfs_wf_yield` calls once `fs_lock` is back (a no-op twin outside `ZFS_WRITE_OPS`). Armed,
    the first yield writes `a.bin [0, 4096)` through fd Y, policy B, and leaves it pending, exactly as a write#1 on
    another CPU would. Big.bin is rewritten by ONE 4.5 MiB write#1 through fd X. Measured on ashift9: 5 txgs,
    txg 102 `by=yield`, X whole, Y's bytes read back through a third fd: `ok`. The host model (`zfs-expect.py`, the
    op's F lines, `zfs-sa-check.py`'s clock: Y is written on the race op's step) and OpenZFS hold the image.
    **Negative N5** (the re-check and `zfs_wp_own` removed, the backstop kept): red in the race op (below, § h's
    record). The review's SMP ring-3 form (two writers, a 6 MiB write#1 against a loop) is NOT built. The hook
    reproduces the same interleaving at the exact point, every run. A ring-3 race depends on which CPU wins the
    unlock / lock gap, and it re-keys the fixture.
    (b) **MAJOR — "durable at exit" held only for orphans and processes reaped at once.** `proc_death_release` released
    no fds. A child whose parent was alive became a zombie with its fd table intact, so its pending txg waited for
    the parent's `waitpid` / `execwait`, which a background `#43` job's parent or a busy daemon may never call. **Fix:**
    `zfs_release_pid(pid, isr)`, called from `proc_death_release` for EVERY death (exit#0, a fault, a kill at B1, a
    claim, a wait's ABORT, the tick). It commits the pending txg one of the dying process's ZFS fds names (`by=exit`)
    BEFORE the death is published, under `fs_lock`. It has the same contract as `proc_destroy_fd_table`, which an
    orphan's reap already ran from all these paths. ABI § 3.6 now says "the death of the process … before its parent
    can see it exit". **Proof:** zfs-ring3-smoke. zfsx's clause 161 (written, never closed, exit) now commits at the
    death. Every exit of the run goes through the hook, so a lock misuse there would hang it. The review's ring-3
    clause (a background child, the parent never waits, QEMU killed) is NOT built: it needs a forking payload and a
    re-keyed fixture (k.132 (2)).
    (c) **MAJOR — a killed orphan's pending txg committed INSIDE the timer ISR** (`proc_tick_lifecycle` →
    `proc_reap_orphan` → `zfs_wp_close` → `zfs_commit`, IF=0, the vector in service, the EOI only after: up to a whole
    commit, 1.4–2.5 s on wr / fz's tails; k.130 (3)). **Fix:** in an interrupt handler nothing commits. `zfs_wp_close`
    (when `apic_in_service`) and `zfs_release_pid` (`VIA_TICK`, or `apic_in_service`) set `zfs_wp_owed` instead. That
    flag is the gpu spill's deferral (D-34): the next B1 of any process runs `zfs_wp_owed_run` (`proc_b1_check`'s
    fast path reads one more word), and meanwhile `zfs_wp_aged` treats the pending txg as aged, so any ZFS entry
    commits it. Every commit clears the flag; sync / `power_flush` commit it as ever. `zfs_release_pid`'s ISR path
    takes no lock; its racy look at `zfs_wp_ref` can only make the owed commit an early one. ⚠ The orphan's teardown
    still takes `fs_lock` in the ISR, for its pipes; that spin predates B11 (k.132 (3)).
    (d) **MINOR — the live windows ran in one gate only.** Every zfs-write-smoke commit prints `windows=0` (the op
    list runs before `sched_active`); zfs-ring3's NVMe close was the one live run, and AHCI's `kirqw_point` in
    `ahci_poll_slot0` had never opened a window. **Fix:** zfs-ring3-test.py takes `ZR3_BUS=ahci` (the disk on the q35
    AHCI port) and `ZR3_KSTACK_HW=1` (accepts a `KSTACK_HW=1` kernel and prints its `kstack-hw:` lines). One run on
    AHCI with KSTACK_HW is recorded in § h's record. That run is NOT a sweep row: the plain NVMe row stays the gate.
    (e) **MINOR — the windows overrode `power_stop_final`'s deliberate IF=0.** `power_flush` → `zfs_wp_sync` →
    `zfs_commit` opened `sti` windows whenever `sched_active` was set. **Fix:** `zfs_wirq_no`, set by `power_flush`
    before its sync and never cleared (every caller of `power_flush` is a terminus: `power_quiesce_devices` and the
    reset after a failed modeset); `zfs_wirq_on` answers 0 under it.
    (f) **MINOR — a dirty set could outlive its op with no pending ref.** `zfs_wf_begin` dirties the dnode, so a
    fresh txg whose first block meets ERESTART ALWAYS answers -2 (not only after a block-0 growth). A second -2 after
    the retry's commit broke out of `zfs_wf_write`, leaving that dirty set behind; the next unrelated op was refused
    once ("dirty set not empty") and dropped it. **Fix:** the second -2 drops the fresh txg, which holds nothing of
    the write (its dnode; a growth the first commit already published).
    (g) **MINOR — a continued txg's grown block 0 could commit charged at its OLD size.** `zfs_wf_grow` applied the
    growth before the level check, the s0 space check or the loop's block-0 charge could answer -2. **Fix:**
    `zfs_wf_gsz`, the growth's decision split from `zfs_wf_grow`. A continued txg's growth is decided, judged (the
    level growth) and charged at its new size (`zfs_wd_space`, `ZWD_CHG` set) BEFORE it is applied; any refusal
    answers -2 with nothing changed. A fresh txg is as before. The charge is the same amount the loop / s0 took
    before, so images are unchanged where no -2 fired: ashift9's `cp` growth 64 → 128 KiB.
    (h) **MINOR — two observers ignored a failed commit-before-observe:** `zfs_stat_ref` (stat / lstat) and lseek's
    `SEEK_END` returned the committed, pre-write size as success. A third, `zfs_fill_statfs`, did the same for a
    failed `zfs_wp_flush`. All three now answer -1, as read#5, open#7 and `zfs_file_size` do. The age-only
    `zfs_wp_obs(0)` of readlink / getdents / readdir still ignore it: they read names, which a pending txg never
    changes.
    (i) **MINOR — a failed release was reported as a failed commit.** `zfs_wc_pub` returned `zfs_wdef_rel(t)`, so a
    release failure after uberblock T was durable made close / sync answer EIO for writes that were on disk. **Fix:**
    the release still fails the pool read-only (named), but the commit answers 0.
    (j) **MINOR — `var zfs_wt_msonly` was declared twice** in `ZFS_WRITE_OPS` builds (module scope at the allocator,
    which `zfs_wpick` reads, and again in the op list's section). Whether cycc merges the two was never established,
    and the release op passed either way. The second declaration is gone.
    (k) **MINOR — comments described the pre-B11 behaviour.** Six places said "never reused this boot" / "deferred
    for the boot", and the write arm said "ONE txg per write#1 … durable when it returns". They now give the k.125
    window and policy B (`zfs_write.cyr` ×5, `vfs.cyr`'s write arm, the B11 section's hook list).
132. **(B11) Open after the review.** (1) THE OWNERSHIP LOCK (k.126 (4), unchanged): other CPUs still wait on `fs_lock`
    for a whole commit. (2) The race and the death hook are proved IN-KERNEL (the race op, by construction plus
    ring 3's exit path), not by the review's ring-3 forms: two concurrent writers, and a background child whose
    parent never waits with QEMU killed under it. Both need a forking zfsx and re-key the fixture, so they are B12's
    to decide with the sweep. (3) An orphan killed at the tick still SPINS on `fs_lock` inside the ISR when another
    CPU commits (`proc_destroy_fd_table`'s pipe sweep takes it; this predates B11). Deferring the whole teardown of a
    tick-killed orphan to B1 would close it. (4) A fault-killed process now commits in the fault path
    (`fault_kill_current`, VIA_FAULT). That path is CPL3 only (the stub gates on RPL 3), so no kernel lock is held
    there. Its one console line, a FAILED commit's, is the one an orphan's reap there already risked (that banner calls
    kprint unsafe under the faulting CR3); a quiet failure mode is the alternative. (5) k.130 (2), (4)–(6) stand. (3) is
    CLOSED by k.131 (c).
133. **(The merge, 2026-10-13) B10 and B11 — two tracks from one seed, merged into one tree.** Each track was a copy of
    the same base (B0–B9, ROOM, the BSP move, gate 38; fixture RECIPE 8) and was merged by `git merge-file` against it,
    B10 first, then B11; every conflict was resolved by hand with both tracks' intent kept. **Numbering:** both tracks
    numbered § k from 108 — B10 keeps **k.108–k.123**, B11's k.108–k.116 are **k.124–k.132**, with every
    cross-reference to them (this spec, `zfs_write.cyr`'s and `io.cyr`'s comments, `zfsx.cyr`, `zfs-ring3-test.py`,
    `zfs-room-check.py`, `zfs.md`). Both tracks wrote `zfs.md` "§ 16": B10's log hygiene keeps it,
    B11's policy B is **§ 17** (its two "§ 16" pointers moved; its "§§ 9–15" engine is §§ 9–16). **The fixture:**
    neither track changed the recipe's build half — B10 added A3y and the host's log count, B11 `--cut-labels`, both to
    the VERIFY half — so RECIPE stays 8 and the key is B11's payload's, `r8-4ee74703473a-c579a04a` (only B11 changed
    `zfsx.cyr`; its clauses 156–161 and `RING3_TXGS` 44 collide with nothing). **Op lists:** `zfs_wtest` runs B10's
    drains (`zfs_wt_b10`) then B11's ops (`zfs_wt_b11`, `zfs_wt_b11late`), and the smoke's expected manifest and
    counts follow that order (`b10_*` then `b11_*`; `_nd` is B10's drains). **Smoke:** B11 moved every serial-log read
    to `slog` (a `strings` line split at a valid UTF-8 name); B10's new reads — the drain count, the hygiene line, the
    `zfs-wb3: flush` / `gang` wants, the hold measurement — were `strings` and are `slog` now. B11's Gates sentence in
    `zfs.md` closed its parenthesis twice; it closes once. **Found by the merge, fixed in it** — neither
    track could see these, since B10 had no deferral release and B11 never destroyed a log:
    (a) **A ghost outlived its blocks (major).** B10 kept a destroyed log's deferral as a GHOST for every log with txg
        ≥ T0 − 2, for the whole boot. Every metaslab load walked the ghost's dnode copy, and every scan summed it. That
        was sound only under B10's assumption that nothing freed this boot is reused (§ c.2). B11 releases a free of
        txg T at T + 2's publish, and that includes the destroyed log's own blocks, freed in the destroying txg D.
        Three failures followed in the merged tree:
        - A load after D + 2 walked blocks that may have been REALLOCATED. That is garbage at best and a failed load at
          worst ("space map inconsistent": the pool goes read-only).
        - The ghost's FREEs were deferred again at every reload, after B11 had released them. The read-back's deferred
          count then disagreed with the tracked one.
        - B11's release walks only the live logs (k.125's ⚠). A log destroyed inside the window kept its bytes in
          `zfs_w_defer` and its units deferred until an eviction.

        The fix has four parts:
        - A ghost is made only for a log whose txg L is inside the window (`zfs_wl_kill_logs`: L ≥ `zfs_wdef_lo`, so
          L ∈ {D − 2, D − 1}).
        - `zfs_wdef_rel` walks the ghosts beside the live logs. It releases their FREEs with a strict clear (the mount's
          own, L ≤ T0, by eviction, as for logs) and RETIRES each ghost as its txg leaves the window — at the latest at
          D + 1's publish. Its blocks are released at D + 2's, so every walk of a ghost reads intact blocks.
        - `zfs_ms_load` and `zfs_wscan_logs` read only in-window ghosts.
        - The table holds at most 3 ghosts (the mount's T0 − 2 .. T0), so k.122's ceiling of about 2,000 txgs is gone.

        **Proof:** zfs-write-smoke holds every hygiene line to ≤ 3 ghosts, and every rw lane's new `zfs-wtest: ghost
        logs: made= retired= alive=` line to made = retired + alive. No fixture destroys a log inside the window by
        itself: on ashift9 the least U trails T by ≥ 2 on every txg. So agnos9's LAST null txg flushes every dirty
        metaslab (`zfs_wt_flall`, the op list's knob; 0 in production). Logs T − 1 and T − 2 then die inside the window:
        on ashift9, 2 ghosts made, 1 released and retired by T's own publish, 1 alive, read back (`zfs_wt_rb`) and
        handed to OpenZFS's verify on that image.
    (b) **The SM-append rule: B10 removed it, B11 narrowed it.** B11's form deferred, at load, a FREE appended to a
        metaslab's space map whose debug txg is ≥ `zfs_wdef_lo`. B11's release never clears such a range:
        `zfs_wdef_rel` walks logs, not space maps. A flush at F of a free from T < F, loaded after T left the window,
        would re-defer released units until an eviction, and the load's deferred count would disagree with the tracked
        one at the read-back. B10's rule stands: a flush appends what logs and ghosts carry, and they carry the window.
    (c) **The publish's tail was outside B11's hold and its interrupt windows.** B11's hold ends at `zfs_wc_mark`, before
        the op list's console, and `zfs_wdef_rel` ran after it. B10 put `zfs_wl_hres` there too: a dry flush that may
        LOAD a metaslab — device reads of its space map and of every log ≥ U. Both ran under `fs_lock` with IF=0, no
        window and no count. `zfs_wc_tail` now runs them with windows reopened and adds their time to the hold. The
        release runs first, then hygiene's minimum for t + 1, so the minimum is measured on the released state (the
        mount window's release evicts every resident metaslab).
    (d) **B11's admission predicate against B10's admission limit.** B11 rewrote `zfs_wf_write1`'s admission against
        `ZWK_MARENA − ZWF_MRSV`: a block already dirtied in the txg takes no slot and no new charge (`ZWD_CHG`). B10 had
        moved every admission to `zfs_wf_alim()`, which keeps hygiene's `ZWL_HAR` out of an admission's reach (k.119);
        B10's negative N6 is exactly an admission reaching it. The merged predicate is B11's with B10's limit. No other
        site of B11's uses the raw arena bound.
    (e) **A flush cut mid-way.** Every txg of B11's cut list now runs B10's hygiene, so the cut txg T0 + 6 flushes a
        metaslab:
        - cut-data halts before the flush's appends are written;
        - cut-mos halts after them (the MOS class precedes the chain);
        - cut-ub1 and cut-ub3 halt after uberblock T0 + 6 landed, with the flush in it.

        The halt now prints the cut txg's own hygiene (`zfs-wtest: cut txg T hygiene: flushed= …`, planned before any
        of its writes), and all four lanes require flushed ≥ 1. OpenZFS opened each image at exactly the txg its lane
        names.
    (f) **Checked, no change:**
        - B10's drain (`zfs_wq_obj`) yields between steps, so another CPU's write#1 may leave a pending txg in the gap.
          Each step's `zfs_wu_begin` (or `zfs_wf_begin`, for a directory's part) commits it first (`zfs_wp_own`,
          `by=begin`) before any refusal can drop the txg, and a drained object's own pending writes (an fd still open
          on it) commit before it is freed.
        - A log's destruction frees its blocks in log D. They are deferred and released like any free, strictly: each
          unit is freed once.
        - `zfs-room-check.py` takes B11's window over B10's plan-line logs (`--log`), so a log destroyed inside the
          window still counts as deferred there.
        - B10's ashift9 wants ride on B11's 600 s lane.

    **Open:** § h's merge record lists what stays open (none of it the merge's).

134. **(B12, 2026-10-13) The full sweep's row ceiling killed the ZFS write row.** Found preparing B12's one full sweep
    (`SWEEP_JOBS=4 sh scripts/sweep.sh`, 71 rows). Every row of the parallel phase ran under the one
    `SWEEP_ROW_TIMEOUT` (default 1500 s, `sweep_run_row`), and `zfs-write-smoke` — this release's row, grown from
    ≈ 13 min at B3b to ≈ 81 min (4,875 s, measured alone at the B10 / B11 merge: twenty-one lanes, two FreeBSD verify
    batches) — could only be killed at 25 min, scored `ROW TIMEOUT` and re-run serially after the parallel phase: a red
    row and ≈ 80 min of tail from the harness, on a green kernel. Not the kernel's; the writer's own row outgrew a
    harness default nobody had re-read since 1.57.9. **Fix** (smallest correct change, `scripts/sweep.sh`): a smoke
    DECLARES its own ceiling in its header, `# sweep-row-timeout: <s>`, and `sweep_row_ceiling` runs its row under
    max(`SWEEP_ROW_TIMEOUT`, `<s>`) — a declaration only RAISES the ceiling, so `SWEEP_ROW_TIMEOUT` stays the knob for a
    slow box. Prior art: kselftest's per-collection `settings` file, whose `timeout=` overrides the runner's default
    (`tools/testing/selftests/kselftest/runner.sh`); Bazel's per-target `timeout = "long"`. `zfs-write-smoke.sh`
    declares **7200 s** (one attempt with ≈ 1.5× margin; a second attempt does not fit and does not need to — the
    serial retry is the second chance). The serial retry and the `exclusive` rows have no ceiling and are unchanged.
    For this one run the dispatch order was seeded with the merge's 4,875 s for the row
    (`build/sweep-durations.prev.tsv`, the local joblog), so it started first rather than at the 60 s an unknown row
    weighs; the next sweep's joblog carries the measured figure. Recorded in CHANGELOG Fixed and `build.md`'s
    `SWEEP_ROW_TIMEOUT` row. **Open:** none.

135. **(1.57.13, the repair sweep) k.132 (3) CLOSED, and the B12 sweep's three harness carry-overs.** (a) **The
    tick-killed orphan.** A tick death (`proc_death_finish`, `via=tick`) no longer reaps in the timer ISR: its zombie
    children become DYING (4) in the final hold instead of being reaped before it, its own orphan reap is skipped, and
    each pid's bit is set in `proc_orphan_owed` (under `proctab_lock`, in that hold). `proc_orphan_owed_run`
    (`syscall.cyr`) claims the mask under `proctab_lock` and reaps each pid REMOTELY (`proc_reap_orphan`: the off-CPU
    fence, the fd table — the pipe sweep and a ZFS fd's commit, now in process context —, the address space, the
    publish), from the next B1 of any process (`proc_b1`, beside `klug_spill_owed` / `zfs_wp_owed`) or the next idle
    step of any CPU (`sched_idle_step` — an AP's idle loops on the step from `ap_entry`, not `kernel_idle_loop`).
    **The guarantee, as built after the track review:** the drain TAKES `fs_lock` with `fs_spin_trylock` (one xchg) or
    does nothing and is retried — it never waits out another context's commit (it first only LOOKED at `fs_lock` and
    took it later inside `proc_destroy_fd_table`, so a commit starting in between was waited out, at IF=0 in a B1); it
    still runs the reaped process's OWN pending-txg commit inside its fd-table step (`zfs_wp_close`, the reap's own
    work on every reap path). `zfs_wp_owed_run` — the commit the same tick death owes, which took `fs_lock`
    unconditionally at every B1 — takes it the same way. The drain is non-preemptible from the claim to the last
    publish (the idle is switchable at preempt 0; a claimed pid must not wait on a switched-out idle), the idle enters
    it at IF=1, and `proc_b1` skips `lifecycle_park_or_abort`'s `sched_lock` when no kill / stop is pending (every
    syscall exit enters B1 while something is owed). `pipe_ends_open` skips a DYING slot whose death is done, so the
    owed window never withdraws an EOF / EPIPE a zombie had already given. The INV-L2 wording is "no `fs_lock`, no
    heap lock, no reap in the ISR; pmm only through `shm_free`'s 2 MB release". Prior art: Linux `do_exit` in
    process context on the way to user mode and `finish_task_switch`'s mmdrop in the next task (idle included);
    FreeBSD `ast()`. **Gate:** `kstack-smoke`'s `kstack: tick orphan OK` (-smp 4): kmain holds `fs_lock` 300 ms while
    an orphan with a zombie child spins in ring 3 on an AP and is SIGKILLed at its tick — that CPU took 29 ticks in
    the hold, both reaps owed, both reaped after the release, 0 pages lost. **Negative N-ORPH** (the deferral removed):
    `ticks_in_hold=1 done=0` — the ISR spun in `proc_reap_child(Z)` — FAIL. The phase also plants a pipe's write end in
    Z's table and its read end in O's: during the hold both must read closed (`pipe_w=0 pipe_r=0`; **N-PIPE**, the
    `pipe_ends_open` skip removed: `pipe_w=1 pipe_r=1`, FAIL), and both owed runners called by kmain under its own
    `fs_lock` must refuse (`refused=1/0/1`; **N-ZOWED**, `zfs_wp_owed_run`'s unconditional take restored: the boot
    self-deadlocks at the phase, FAIL). (b) **doom / naad-ring3 and SIBLINGS_ROOT**:
    both smokes (and doom-audio) default their sibling roots through `${SIBLINGS_ROOT:-$ROOT/..}`, as `sweep.sh`'s
    export block now does; naad-ring3 builds naadex from a copy in its tree's `build/` and never writes the naad repo.
    (c) **`sun_path`**: every smoke's QEMU socket goes through `smoke_qsock` (qemu-dwell.sh; `_qsock.py` for the
    sweep-row harnesses): `${XDG_RUNTIME_DIR:-/tmp}/agnos-q.<md5(path)[:12]>/<name>`, used only when lstat says it is
    a real directory of this uid with no group / other bits (else `mktemp -d`), `set -e`-safe, pruned recursively in
    both twins. Records: CHANGELOG 1.57.13.
